The organization, the guides, the workflows, `teams_reading` and `message_style` are left out of the document while empty or at their defaults, so an account without them keeps the document and digest an older host computes; once one of them holds something, a host that predates it cannot install the document (`deny_unknown_fields`) until it is updated. Never portable: data and repository paths, `repositories` bindings, allowed roots, bind, public URL, tunnels and webhook prefix, `[secrets]`, Microsoft tenant/client/user IDs, what each computer receives (`graph.discover_all_chats`, `graph.allowed_chats`, `graph.self_chat`), `allowed_senders` of the policy, people profiles, agent prompts, legacy cases, activity records and settings, the knowledge-index settings, machine/control credentials, internal keys, and Microsoft, GitHub, Azure and coding-provider sessions. `Document`, `Settings` and the patch types use `deny_unknown_fields`, so a local field cannot be smuggled into a snapshot or a change.
- **Revision.** The coordinator's revision is `{generation, digest}`: the digest is a domain-separated SHA-256 of the canonical document plus its notes, and the generation (private `portable/revision.json`) moves forward whenever the digest differs from the last one it observed, however the content was changed (GUI, CLI, MCP, a hand edit or a forwarded change), so it only grows. A change is checked against the digest, not the counter. The installation shows the **loaded** revision (installed in its profile) next to the **saved** one (the newest the coordinator reported).
- **Coordinator side.** The machine protocol admits exactly `portable_snapshot`, `portable_apply`, `portable_note_save`, `portable_note_remove` and `portable_records` (machine credential and account version as for the variable catalog; windows, the CLI and browsers are refused; no other method is proxied). `portable_snapshot {section}` returns `config` (the document, its revision, the account-source fingerprint and the note count) or `notes` in pages of 8 ordered by ID; it refuses a document larger than 900 kB or more than 2,048 notes instead of truncating. `portable_apply {base, patch, unavailable}` holds the profile operations lock, rejects a `base` that is not the current digest as `state_conflict`, applies the patch to the full document, re-validates with the knowledge-map and configuration rules and the real `validate_local`, and commits through the journaled settings commit (`SettingsStore::commit`) without restarting a running Teams service (the existing «restart to apply» notice shows). A note change names the content token of the note it read; a note changed since is a conflict (timestamps have a one-second resolution, so they are not used). A patch can **never broaden access** (`Patch::apply_to`): a new source must be disabled with no audiences or external processing; an existing one may not gain `enabled`, external processing, an audience entry or an unrestricted audience, nor change its destination while enabled; `teams_reading` may not gain `all_chats`, the personal chat or a chat ID; narrowing is allowed. Granting access is done on the coordinator's own surfaces.
- **Installation side.** `portable_sync {reset?}` (MCP `portable_sync`) reads the config document and every note page (restarting up to three times if the revision, total or source changes in between), validates them as the coordinator would (`Document::check`, note rules, digest equality) and installs them under the operations lock: settings are applied to the profile's own `Config`, sources whose repository alias is not bound here (or whose file source is not readable) are left out and recorded as **unavailable** together with the questions that read only them, contexts and questions that lose references are `partial`, the organization's documenting sources lose the unavailable ones, local bindings, people, agent prompts, cases and identity are kept, the whole is validated with `validate_local`, committed with the journaled settings commit (`SettingsStore::commit`), notes replaced in one transaction (`NotesStore::replace_notes`) and `portable/replica.json` records the link (coordinator, account, machine), the source fingerprint, loaded and saved revisions, the digest of the installation's own projection right after (`applied`) and the unavailable items. Nothing starts Teams, selects a machine, enables a root, changes the chats this computer receives, installs or signs in to a provider, or defaults a source: the document's flags and reading scope are installed exactly. A profile is **pristine** when its projection equals a freshly created profile's; only then does the first synchronization install without `reset`. Otherwise, and whenever the profile's projection differs from `applied` (unsaved local changes, a hand edit, `config import`), nothing is replaced: `state_conflict` with `local_changes`, and the equal-revision check still records the newest saved revision. `reset` backs up the portable parts being replaced to `portable/backups/replaced-<time>.json` (private, last three) first. A different account fingerprint, a lower generation, a different coordinator/account/machine link or an invalid snapshot also change nothing (`state_conflict`/`operation_failed`); a changed link discards the replica and its reported status.
- **One synchronization.** `account_sync {reset?}` (CLI `pta account sync [--reset]`, MCP `account_sync`, the GUI's sidebar Synchronize and its sign-in screen) runs `variable_sync` and then `portable_sync` with the same `reset`, both always, and replies with the configuration's `sync`/`changes`, the catalog's `variables` and the `standing`; the first failure is the reply, so a configuration conflict still leaves the catalog current. It shares the `portable_*` dispatch (`account_portable_support` gate, profile-lock behaviour, refusal to machine credentials, portal allowlist). On a coordinator or standalone installation it only reports both revisions. `pta auth machine finish --wait [--replace-settings]` calls it once connected (`--replace-settings` is `reset`) and reports it under `account`; sign-in stays done if it fails.
- **Edits from a connected installation.** `save_settings` (GUI, `config set/apply`, question association) computes the patch between the profile's previous and next projections. An empty patch is a local-only save (works offline). Otherwise the installation needs a loaded replica equal to the saved revision with no unsaved local changes, sends `portable_apply` with the loaded digest and the unavailable source IDs (so a context, question or the organization's source list edited here keeps references only the account can resolve, and unavailable sources can be neither edited nor removed from here), and saves locally only after the coordinator accepted; on acceptance with `previous == loaded` the new revision becomes loaded and saved. A conflict records `sync.conflict`, saves nothing and tells the user to synchronize; an unreachable coordinator saves nothing (`dependency_or_network`); never queued or retried. `notes_save`/`notes_remove` do the same per note. A deletion therefore cannot be undone by an old copy: the old client is `stale`/conflicted until it synchronizes, and replacing the notes removes what the account removed.
- **Status.** `portable_status` (CLI `pta account status`, MCP `portable_status`) reads local state: `mode` (`replica` or `authoritative`), `state` (`never`, `syncing`, `synced`, `stale`, `diverged`, `conflict`, `error`), `loaded`/`saved`, times, a sanitized `error`/`conflict`, `local_changes`, `unavailable`, `local_setup` (repository folders to connect, providers not installed or signed in, Microsoft sign-in, empty coding directories) and the allowlist. The connected host's poll loop synchronizes at most every 30 seconds, the first time right after sign-in, without delaying presence. The GUI shows it in Settings › General › **Account configuration** (only on a signed-in installation): state badge, loaded versus account revision, the local setup list, and a two-tap «Replace this computer's settings with the account's» when there is something to resolve (including a computer configured before it signed in: `never` with `local_changes`). Synchronizing is the sidebar's account indicator, not a button of this panel. Hosts and CLIs advertise additive `account_portable_support`; `portable_*` is refused to a host without it, and the portal allowlist admits `portable_sync` and `portable_status` (a portal account's host is its own coordinator, so it reports `authoritative`).
- **Retained records.** Records that stay on the coordinator (the saved daily task record) are read through `portable_records` / `account_records` / `pta account records`: read-only, one of two fixed kinds, reply bounded to 900 kB, authorized by the machine credential and account. It is not a proxy and copies no database or history.

### Teams reading on request

Two scopes decide which chats a computer touches. **Reception** (`graph.discover_all_chats`, `graph.allowed_chats`, `graph.self_chat`) is each computer's own: `Graph::allowed_collection` gates subscriptions, webhook notifications, recovery, the fetch of each incoming message, the model's Teams tools while answering and the destination of an answer, so a change on another computer never changes what the machine that answers Teams receives or sends. **Reading on request** is the account's `[teams_reading]` (`all_chats`: every chat the user belongs to, the personal chat included; `personal_chat`: the user's Teams notes; `chats`: up to 500 canonical chat IDs, each once): `Graph::readable_on_request` allows a chat that reception allows or `TeamsReading::reads` allows, and only the operator's reads use it (`readable_chat` behind `teams messages|search|chats` and the MCP `teams_messages`, `teams_search`, `teams_chats` and their `readable` flag). Each computer reads with its own Microsoft session and `Chat.Read`, and Graph still refuses a chat the user does not belong to. Posting (`teams_post`) is not limited by either scope.

The section is omitted while it holds its defaults (`TeamsReading::is_default`), so it starts off and a host that predates it keeps loading the profile. It travels in the portable `Settings` (replica path) and, being an account section, in the account store's `config` record (primary path); `install_account_config` builds the configuration from the record and keeps only the installation's sections, so a scope the account turns off returns to its defaults on every computer instead of keeping a former copy (the same holds for `message_style`). A running assistant's Graph session gets the current scope for each read (`app::teams_graph`), without a restart. Through a coordinator a replica may only narrow it (`Patch::apply_to`); in primary mode every enrolled computer writes it as any other account setting. Surfaces: Settings › Microsoft Teams («Read on request», marked Account, next to «Receive and answer», marked This computer), `pta config get|set teams_reading.*`, MCP `teams_reading` (both scopes) and `teams_reading_update` (needs confirmation).

### Account store link, mirror, primary and migration (phases 3a to 4b)

`app/cloud.rs` links an installation to the account store Worker (`cloud/`, [cloud storage design](organization-and-cloud-storage.md)), `app/cloud/mirror.rs` with the core `mirror` module keeps an encrypted copy of the account's records there, `app/cloud/mirror/primary.rs` with `mirror/primary.rs` makes the store the source of truth of the configuration (mode `primary`), and `app/cloud/mirror/migration.rs` moves an account into the store and back (`migrate`, `rollback`, `erase`). Reads are always local and the Teams pipeline never waits on the store.

- **Profile.** `[cloud]` in `Config` (`#[serde(default)]`, omitted while default, so older hosts keep loading the profile until the installation connects): `endpoint` (HTTPS origin), `installation` (the UUID the Worker enrolled) and `mode` (`off`, `mirror`, `primary`; anything but `off` needs an enrolled installation). It is installation-local: the portable allowlist does not carry it, and `save_settings` (GUI, `config set/apply`) always keeps the stored section, so only `cloud_connect` changes it.
- **Credentials.** The account's Access service token (`CLOUD_ACCESS_CLIENT_ID`, `CLOUD_ACCESS_CLIENT_SECRET`) and the installation credential the Worker issues (`CLOUD_STORE_TOKEN`, `ptac_…`, 256 bits, the Worker keeps only its SHA-256) are internal credentials in the system store, listed by `credentials list` and never required to start; launchers cannot receive them.
- **Who.** Every `cloud_*` operation is for the owner of an unshared profile or for a portal account whose operator enabled the account store for it, never for a machine credential (`cloud::refused_here`; `check_shared_operation` no longer lists them, so portal accounts reach the host, which decides). The operator enables a portal account with `pta web users cloud enable USER --endpoint URL [--token-expires YYYY-MM-DD]` (operator-local, the account's Access service token as `{client_id, client_secret}` JSON on stdin; `scripts/configure-cloud-access.py production --portal-user USER` creates the token and runs it): the token goes only into that account's own file credential store (`<profile>/credentials/default/`, 0600) and `<profile>/cloud-grant.json` (0600: endpoint, time, expiry) records the permission. Only operator administration writes the grant; the account's host only reads it. `pta web users cloud disable USER` removes both: that host's loop stops sending and pulling and every operation but `cloud_status` is refused («The portal operator has not enabled the account store for this account»), while its local data, its content key and its copy in the store stay. Inside, the account decides about its own data (connect, migrate, verify, promote, demote, pull, roll back, erase, installations) from its GUI, CLI and MCP; its content key (`keys/`), ledger and backups are in its own profile, its Worker account is that of its own service token, and the key backup (`pta variables key backup`, operator-local) carries its `cloud_content` too. `web users list` shows `cloud_enabled`.
- **`cloud_connect`** (CLI `pta cloud connect --endpoint URL [--label TEXT] --confirmed` with `{client_id, client_secret}` JSON on stdin, GUI Settings › Advanced › Cloud storage › «Connect this computer», a fold hidden while the installation is enrolled and live and shown again when the link breaks, MCP `cloud_connect` with the stored service token only). A portal account connects only to its grant's endpoint and only with the token the operator stored (a token on stdin or as arguments is refused); the expiry comes from the grant. It checks the endpoint (HTTPS origin), the label and the service token's shape, then the Worker's health (configured, protocol 1); when the installation is already enrolled and live with the stored token it changes nothing. A new service token given to an enrolled installation (a renewal or a replacement), or stored by the portal operator while the stored one no longer answers, moves the account to it when the Worker offers `rebind` (0.4.0): `POST /v1/rebind` with the new token at the edge and this installation's credential keeps the account, its store, the enrollment and the mode, and only the Access credentials and the recorded expiry change here (`rebound`); a token that already belongs to another account is refused (`state_conflict`: disconnect first to join that account), and a credential the Worker no longer knows falls back to enrolling. The account's other computers then need the new token too. Otherwise it calls `POST /v1/enroll`, stores the three credentials first and then the `[cloud]` section under the operations lock (a section without credentials reads as not enrolled, never the reverse).
- **`cloud_status`** (CLI `pta cloud status`, MCP `cloud_status`, the same panel). Reads `[cloud]` and the mirror's local state (`mirror`: key fingerprint, outbox, last pass, last success and error, last verification), then the Worker's health and, when enrolled, `/v1/whoami` and `/v1/store` with both credentials: `standing` is `disconnected`, `not_enrolled`, `live`, `offline` (network, server errors or the account's daily share used up), `revoked` (Access or the Worker refused the credentials, or another installation answered) or `unconfigured` (the Worker has no Access settings), with a `problem` sentence, the Worker's environment, version, deployment, protocols and schemas, the principal (label, enrollment time, the account's active installations) and the store's counts and usage: today's use against the account's daily share (`usage.limits`) and, from Worker 0.4.0 (`plan`), the plan's daily quota every account shares with how many accounts share it now and whether every account at its share would exceed it (`store.plan {requests, rows_written, accounts, oversubscribed}`). Exchanges have a 20 s timeout and never follow redirects; Access refusals (HTML) and Worker refusals (JSON) are told apart only where it matters.
- **Content key.** `keys/cloud-content.json` (0600, created once by the first `cloud_migrate start`, never replaced; `cloud_erase` sets it aside): the content keys by version and the active one, a random **content account** UUID and a separate key that blinds record IDs. None of it reaches Cloudflare; status shows only a fingerprint. The operator key backup (`pta variables key backup|restore`) carries it as the additive `cloud_content` field for the owner of an unshared profile; a restore that would replace a different key changes nothing. A trusted computer receives it, sealed to its sign-in's key, when the person ticks «Also give it the account store» (see browser-assisted sign-in in §16).
- **Sealing** (`mirror/seal.rs`). A record's plaintext is the canonical JSON (keys sorted) of `{id, data}`, sealed with AES-256-GCM-SIV under the active key; the associated data binds, length-prefixed, the domain `personal-teams-assistant/cloud-record/v1`, the content account, the key version, the collection, the record ID the Worker sees, the revision and the payload schema. The sealed bytes are the key version (4 bytes), a synthetic nonce (HMAC-SHA256 of the associated data and the plaintext under a key derived from the content key, first 12 bytes) and the ciphertext, so sealing the same record at the same revision is repeatable and a repeated send is `unchanged`, never `conflict`. The Worker's record ID is a blinded ID: HMAC-SHA256 under the ID key of the collection and the natural ID (a Teams resource, a day, a note or run ID), so no identifier reaches Cloudflare; the natural ID travels inside the plaintext and verification checks it against the blinded one.
- **What is mirrored.** The eleven collections, read by each local store's `export` (every finished record, not a page): `settings` (the account's `config` without `[secrets]`, `[cloud]` and the installation's own sections, the account's `knowledge_map` without the organization, the guides and the repository folders, and, once enrolled, `installation/<installation ID>` with this installation's own settings: `server`, `graph`, the map path `knowledge_map`, `knowledge_index`, `policy.dry_run` and the map's `repositories`; decision 19), `organization` (one record), `notes`, `evaluations`, `examples` (knowledge index), `audits` (finished messages of `assistant.db`; simulations stay local), `activity_snapshots`, `activity_summaries`, `activity_runs` (finished), `implementation_runs`, `implementation_requests` (finished). In-flight rows, send nonces, sessions, credentials and the legacy activity entries stay local.
- **Outbox** (`mirror/ledger.rs`, `cloud/mirror.db`). One row per record: blinded ID, the highest revision used, the revision, content hash and ciphertext digest the Worker acknowledged, and the content hash being sent. A sweep compares every local record with its row: a different hash allocates the next revision and records it before the send; a record gone locally becomes a tombstone (one never sent is forgotten). Only IDs and hashes are queued, never content, so the outbox is bounded by the local records and needs no announcement to be durable: the stores call `mirror::wake()` after a finished-record write (audits, activity, implementation, notes, settings commits) to send soon, and the loop also sweeps every 10 minutes and after a start, which catches writes that do not announce themselves (`Store::open` and `Database::recover` recoveries, `Database::remote`, a restored database). A pass sends up to 500 records in batches of at most 100 and 2.5 MB of sealed bytes; `created`, `updated` and `unchanged` acknowledge, `stale`/`conflict` read the Worker's revision and continue above it, a refused batch backs off (30 s doubling to 1 h), offline or quota stops the pass (the loop retries after 2, 15 or 30 minutes) and a record whose sealed form exceeds 1 MiB is `blocked` until it changes. Status shows pending, retrying, blocked, synced and the oldest pending age, in total and per collection.
- **`cloud_migrate`** (CLI `pta cloud migrate --dry-run|--confirmed`, MCP `cloud_migrate` with `action` plan|start and confirmation, GUI Settings › Advanced › Cloud storage › Encrypted copy, «Migrate to the account store», two taps). Owner or an enabled portal account (above). `plan` counts records and plaintext bytes per collection without a key, ledger or request, and lists what stays local and why (`stays_local`) and the backup's size. `start` requires an enrolled installation, first takes the **backup** (below), then needs a healthy Worker, creates the content key if missing (and says to back it up), runs passes until nothing is eligible, verifies, and only when the verification is clean (pending local changes and blocked records are allowed and reported) records `initial_verified_at` and sets the mode to `mirror`; otherwise the mode stays and running it again continues. In `primary` it changes nothing. The loop (`cloud::run`, spawned by the host) does nothing while the mode is `off`. The host still accepts the 3b method `cloud_mirror` for older clients: `plan` and `start` are the migration's (so no path skips the backup) and `stop` is a rollback.
- **Backup.** `backups/cloud-<time>/` in the profile (0700): the updater's data-safety copy (`maintenance::apply::protected_files` and `copy_data`: every `*.db` of the data directory and of the profile one level down, copied with an online `VACUUM INTO`, plus the profile's small `*.toml` and `implementation/registry.json`) and a `manifest.json` (time, reason, host version, bytes, and for each copy the original path). `cloud_migrate start`, `cloud_promote` (after the clean verification, before the switch) and `cloud_rollback` take one first and change nothing if it fails; the newest three are kept. `cloud_status` shows `mirror.backups` (count and the latest's time, reason and size).
- **Restoring a backup** (`app/cloud/restore.rs`, 0.10.40; CLI `pta cloud backups` and `pta cloud restore NAME --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_backups` and `cloud_restore` with confirmation, GUI Cloud storage › Backups of this computer, «Restore», two taps; `cloud_restore_support`). `cloud_backups` (owner or an enabled portal account, read-only) lists the backups newest first, each checked as a restore would check it, the copies kept before earlier restores (`before_restore`), a `pending` restore, `last_restore` (`backups/restore-result.json`) and `restores_here`. `cloud_restore {backup}` is only for the owner of an unshared profile at the local page or the local CLI (`is_local_owner`), because it restarts the host. It checks the backup: a `cloud-<seconds>[-n]` name, a readable manifest from this version or an older one, files named `0000`… that exist, every `from` absolute, without `..`, inside this profile or its data directory and of a kind a backup holds (`*.db`, `*.toml`, `registry.json`), and `PRAGMA quick_check` on each database copy. Under the operations lock and the pass gate it refuses (`state_conflict` with `reasons`) while anything an update would wait for runs (`maintenance::apply::busy`, a migration, a protected variable entry, an activity review), records `backups/restore.json` (ID, backup, time, whether the Teams assistant was running) and restarts: the main process of a systemd unit (`SYSTEMD_EXEC_PID`) stops as `app_quit` does and exits with status 75, which `Restart=on-failure` restarts; any other host (one a person or `pta` started, the owner's host under the portal) first starts its successor detached with the same arguments and `PTA_RESTORE_HANDOVER`, which waits up to a minute for the profile, and then quits. On every host start, after the profile lock and before any store opens, `apply_pending` checks the backup again, copies the current data (the same set, `copy_data`) to `backups/before-restore-<time>/` with its manifest (never pruned or deleted), puts each file back (a temporary copy renamed over it, a database's `-wal`/`-shm` removed first), keeps this installation's `[cloud]` as it was (its credentials are not in the backup) and records the outcome; a failed copy restores nothing. The assistant is started again as after an update. In `primary` the configuration then comes back from the account store at the next pull; history and this computer's own data are as in the backup. Keys, credentials and variables are never part of a backup.
- **`cloud_rollback`** (CLI `pta cloud rollback [--keep-local] --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_rollback` with `keep_local` and confirmation, GUI «Bring everything back to this computer» and, in `primary` with a conflict or offline, «Bring it back keeping this computer's», two taps each). Owner or an enabled portal account, under the operations lock and the pass gate. Takes the backup; in `primary` pulls once (what the store accepted, such as edits made through it or from another computer, is installed here) and refuses with `state_conflict` while a configuration record conflicts and with the store's failure when it does not answer; then verifies and refuses with `state_conflict` when the store holds records this installation does not know (`unknown_remote`, reported as `only_in_store`). Only then sets the mode to `off` and records `rolled_back_at`. Nothing local is deleted and the copy, the ledger and the key stay, so `migrate` continues from them. `keep_local` skips the pull and accepts an unanswered or differing verification, keeping this computer's configuration as it is. History collections are local first by construction, so they need no pull.
- **`cloud_erase`** (CLI `pta cloud erase --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_erase` with confirmation, GUI «Erase the account store's copy», two taps, shown in `off` after a rollback with a note on recovery). Owner or an enabled portal account, only in mode `off`, with a Worker offering `erase` (0.3.0). Calls `DELETE /v1/store` until `more` is false (the Worker refuses while the account has another active installation: `state_conflict`; a failure part way reports the counts and running it again finishes). Then clears the ledger (records and meta, keeping `erased_at`) and renames the content key to `keys/cloud-content-retired-<time>.json`, so the next `migrate` creates a new key and the copy Cloudflare's 30-day point-in-time recovery may still restore stays unreadable without the retired one. Erased R2 backups cannot be recovered. The enrollment, the credentials and every local store stay.
- **`cloud_verify`** (CLI `pta cloud verify`, MCP `cloud_verify`, the same panel). Owner or an enabled portal account, read-only. Per collection: local and remote counts, tombstones, a digest over the sorted `(blinded ID, revision, ciphertext digest, deleted)` computed from the ledger and from the Worker's listing (pages since generation 0), and every live record's ciphertext checked against its digest, opened with the exact associated data, its natural ID re-blinded and its plaintext hash compared with what was acknowledged. Differences are counted as `missing_remote`, `unknown_remote`, `differing`, `undecryptable` or `content_differs`; local changes not sent yet are `pending`, not differences. History is each installation's own: from Worker 0.4.0 (`origin`) a listed record says whether this installation wrote it (`own`), and history records other computers of the account wrote are left out of the comparison and counted as `others` (each verifies its own); the configuration collections are the account's and are compared whole. Without `origin` every record counts as this installation's, as before. `cloud_rollback` inherits the scope, so another computer's history never blocks it. The last result is kept and shown by status.
- **`cloud_repair`** (0.10.47; CLI `pta cloud repair --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_repair` with confirmation, GUI Encrypted copy › «Repair the copy», shown in `mirror` or `primary` after a verification that found differences, two taps; `cloud_repair_support`). Owner or an enabled portal account, under the operations lock and the pass gate. `Engine::repair` lists every collection as `cloud_verify` does and collects the acknowledged records the Worker lacks (`missing_remote`), holds at another revision or digest (`differing`) or holds in a form that does not open or does not match (`undecryptable`, `content_differs`); `Ledger::requeue` forgets their acknowledgement but keeps their revisions, so the next sweep sends each again (or its tombstone) above every revision used, and above the Worker's after a `stale`; a record in conflict is left to its resolution. Records only the Worker holds (`unknown_remote`) are counted and never removed. In `primary` the configuration collections are skipped (their differences are conflicts, `cloud_conflicts`) and the flush uses compare-and-swap as the loop does. It then flushes what it queued (up to 200 passes), verifies, keeps the result as the last verification and answers `repaired` per collection, `requeued`, `uploaded`, `unknown_remote`, `ok`, `verify` and `next`.
- **Latency.** `cloud_status` reports `latency_ms` for its health, whoami and store exchanges, and the last pass keeps the median and maximum batch round trip. Measured from the production server on 2026-11-03: health about 30 ms, whoami (Registry) about 77 ms, store (account store object) about 70 ms, a write batch about 166 ms (design §5).
- **Standing.** The link is a standing of its own kind (`standing::State::observe_store`/`store_view`, `kind: account_store`, next to the coordinator's `kind: coordinator`): `disconnected`, `not_enrolled`, `unknown`, `live`, `offline` (network, server errors, the daily share or a Worker without Access settings) or `revoked`, with `checked_at`, `last_live_at`, the mode and an opaque `account_key` of endpoint and installation. Every exchange records it: `cloud_status`, each pass that sent something, the upload and the verification; an operation refusal leaves it alone. `cloud_status` returns it as `link`, and `setup_status` reports it in the `account_store` step.
- **Service token expiry.** `[cloud] token_expires_at` (Unix seconds, omitted while unknown) is what the user gave with `cloud_connect --token-expires YYYY-MM-DD` (MCP `token_expires`; GUI Cloud storage › Service token › «Service token expires on» and «Save the date» while live, which connects again with only the endpoint and the date, and the optional date in «Connect this computer»; neither for a portal account, whose date comes from its grant); on a live installation that is the only thing a repeated connect changes, and a new service token without a date clears it. `cloud_status` shows `service_token {expires_at, days_left, renew}` and warns (`renew`) 30 days ahead; nothing reads the Access API.
- **A computer signed in to a coordinator** (a trusted machine that received the account store) mirrors only its own history while it is in `mirror`: the configuration collections are the coordinator's to write (`Local::configuration` is false), so it never sends them and its verification counts what the store holds there as `others`; its configuration keeps arriving through `portable_*`. Once the coordinator is in `primary` it can **join** the store as its source: `cloud_promote` asks the coordinator's mode over the machine protocol (action `store_mode`) and refuses until it is `primary`; then, after the usual clean verification and backup, it adopts the account's configuration from the store (`primary::adopt`: a private `cloud/backups/replaced-<time>.json`, then a pull with replace) before any sweep. From then on it reads and writes the configuration and the notes through the store like the coordinator: `portable::before_save`, `note_save`/`note_remove`, `synchronize` and the automatic sync step aside (`sync.source: account_store`), and since 0.10.43 `portable_status` answers `sync {mode, state, source}: account_store` instead of describing its last copy of the coordinator's document, so `setup_status` reports the `configuration` step `done` from the store and the GUI offers no account reset there; variables, machines and the Teams lease still go through the coordinator. The coordinator keeps serving `portable_*` (snapshot, apply, notes) from its replica to the account's computers in `mirror` and to hosts before 0.10.37 (decided in phase 5: nothing of the portable protocol is retired while any of them may use it).
- **A second computer of the account** that is not signed in to a coordinator (`Local::configuration` true) and whose store already holds configuration it never acknowledged is told so (`cloud_status` › `adopt: true`; GUI «Adopt the account's configuration», two taps) and `cloud_migrate start` refuses (`state_conflict`) instead of uploading its configuration over the account's; `adopt: true` (CLI `pta cloud migrate --adopt --confirmed`) backs the profile up, adopts the account's configuration (`primary::adopt`), then uploads only its history and verifies. It needs the account's content key first (`not_ready` otherwise): the trust-time handoff or the key backup. Its own settings stay (decision 19). Verification leaves out a tombstone of a record this installation never had (one removed before it joined).
- **Another installation's own settings.** An `installation/<ID>` record of another installation (`mirror::foreign_installation`, the engine's `Local::installation` naming this one) is never installed by a pull, never gets a ledger row (so no sweep can tombstone it) and is left out of verification as `others`, opened to read its natural ID; a `settings` tombstone this installation never had and another wrote is too.
- **Primary** (`cloud_promote`, CLI `pta cloud promote|demote --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_promote` with `action` promote|demote and confirmation, GUI «Make the account store the source», two taps, and «Make this computer the source again»). Owner or an enabled portal account. `promote` needs mode `mirror`, an installation not signed in to a coordinator (only the coordinator or a standalone installation is promoted; the account's other computers keep `portable_*` through it), and a Worker with the `cas` feature (`/v1/health` `features`); under the operations lock and the pass gate it sends what is pending, verifies, and only with a clean verification and no pending configuration sets `mode = primary` (else `state_conflict` with the report) and pulls once. `demote` returns to `mirror`: the local stores are always complete, so nothing moves. The **configuration collections** (`Collection::is_configuration`: `settings`, all of `config` but `[secrets]` and `[cloud]`, and of the map but the organization; `organization` with its guides; `notes`) then follow these rules; every other collection keeps the outbox.
- **Write-through.** `save_settings` (GUI, `config set/apply`, every MCP edit, question association), `notes_save`/`notes_remove` and, on the coordinator, `portable_apply` and `portable_note_save`/`portable_note_remove` compute the records the stores will export after the commit (`settings::read_back` reproduces the TOML round trip and the map's initial question) and call `primary::before_save`/`save_note`/`remove_note` before anything local changes: each record differing from the acknowledged one is sealed at the next revision (recorded first) and sent with `base_revision` = the acknowledged revision in one `atomic` batch. Accepted: the local commit happens (notes keep the ID and times fixed before the send) and the ledger is settled, all under the pass gate. `conflict`/`stale`/`aborted`: nothing is saved, `state_conflict` («changed in the account store… being synchronized now»), and the loop is woken to pull. No answer, quota or refusal: nothing is saved and nothing is queued (`dependency_or_network`/`not_ready`); the message says the change appears after the next pull if the store received it before the connection failed. A local commit that fails after acceptance leaves the ledger unsettled, so the next pull brings the accepted version. System writes (sign-in identity, Graph chat, a cloned repository, the Microsoft setup) stay local first and the outbox sends them naming their base.
- **Pull.** Each loop pass in `primary` first takes the operations lock and the pass gate and pulls the configuration collections since the last generation (`pulled:<collection>` in the ledger's meta), opening each record with the exact associated data and checking its re-blinded natural ID. A record equal to the local one is acknowledged; one whose local copy still equals the acknowledged content is installed (`HostApply`: the account's configuration is taken from the record with this installation's own sections, `policy.dry_run`, `[secrets]` and `[cloud]` put back (`install_account_config`), even from a record written before the split, so an account section the record leaves out at its defaults returns to them; the map keeps its repository folders; this installation's own record is local first and never installed; the result is validated with `validate_local` and committed with the journaled commit and `forget_removed_sources`; the organization replaces the map's organization and guides; notes are put or removed as they are); a local change never acknowledged meets a newer store version as a **conflict**: kept as it is, `error = conflict` in its ledger row, never pushed by the outbox and refused by write-through (`state_conflict`) until resolved. A pull that fails stops the pass. `portable_snapshot` of the config pulls first (best effort) so the account's other computers get what the store holds. `cloud_pull` (CLI `pta cloud pull [--replace --confirmed]`, MCP `cloud_pull`, GUI «Replace with the account store's», two taps) pulls now; `replace` backs up the configuration and notes to `cloud/backups/replaced-<time>.json` (private, the newest three), re-reads every configuration record and adopts the store's version of the conflicts. A profile that already had its own configuration adopts the account's the same way, never silently. `cloud_status` shows `mirror.primary` (`promoted_at`, `conflicts`, `last_pull`, `pulled_generations`) and `setup_status` turns `account_store` to `attention` on a conflict.
- **Conflicts** (0.10.41; CLI `pta cloud conflicts` and `pta cloud resolve COLLECTION RECORD --keep here|store --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_conflicts` and `cloud_resolve` with confirmation, GUI Cloud storage › Encrypted copy, one entry per record with «Keep the account store's» and «Keep this computer's», two taps each, in place of the earlier «Replace with the account store's»; `cloud_conflicts_support`). Owner or an enabled portal account, in `primary`, under the operations lock and the pass gate. `cloud_conflicts` reads the store's current version of every configuration record whose ledger row is a conflict (`GET /v1/records/{collection}/{id}`), opens it and lists `{collection, record, label, store_revision, removed_here, removed_in_store, fields}`, where `fields` is `primary::diff` of the two versions: objects compared key by key into dotted paths, anything else (arrays included) whole; each side's value is cut to 300 characters (`truncated`) and at most 200 fields are listed (`more_fields`). `cloud_resolve` settles one record with the person's choice and never merges: `store` backs up this computer's configuration and notes (`cloud/backups/replaced-<time>.json`) and installs the store's version of that record only (`HostApply`, acknowledged at the store's revision); `here` keeps the store's version privately (`cloud/backups/overwritten-<time>.json`, the newest three), takes the store's current revision as the agreed base and writes this computer's version over it in an atomic compare-and-swap batch, so a store that changed again in between answers `state_conflict` and the record stays in conflict. `cloud pull --replace` still takes the store's version of every record at once.
- **Locks.** The operations lock is always taken before the pass gate (the loop's pull, `promote`, `pull`, `disconnect`; ordinary operations and `portable_apply` already hold it when they write through).
- **`cloud_rotate_key`** (CLI `pta cloud rotate-key --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_rotate_key` with confirmation, GUI «Rotate the content key», two taps; `cloud_handoff_support`). Owner or an enabled portal account, in `mirror` or `primary`, under the operations lock and the pass gate. `key::rotate` adds a random key as the next version and makes it active (the earlier versions stay, so what is not sealed again yet still opens; the content account and the ID key stay, so blinded IDs do not change) and replaces the key file atomically; `Ledger::reseal` puts every live acknowledged record back in the outbox at its next revision with the same content (tombstones and conflicts stay as they are), so the loop sends the whole copy again sealed with the new key, naming the acknowledged revision under compare-and-swap in `primary`. The reply says to back up the key again; the account's computers trusted with the account store since 0.10.38 receive it from the coordinator (`store_delivery`), and `cloud_token` lists which already hold it (`computers[].key`). It is the second half of handling a lost computer, after `machine_revoke`.
- **Delivering replaced credentials** (`app/cloud/delivery.rs`, 0.10.38). A computer signed in to a coordinator and connected to the store asks, from its loop at most every ten minutes, at once after the store refused its service token, and within seconds when the coordinator announces new credentials (0.10.46: the coordinator's answer to a machine's `poll` carries `store_delivery: true` when that machine recorded a `store_key` and its last `store_held` is older than the coordinator's own token or key, read at most every 30 s per profile; the machine then asks at most every 30 s), with the machine protocol action `store_delivery {held: {token, key}}`: the fingerprint of the service token it holds (`handoff::token_fingerprint`, an HMAC-SHA256 of client ID and secret under a fixed domain, 32 hex digits, so neither side sends the token) and its content key's active version. The coordinator records them on the machine (`store_held`) and answers `current` when the fingerprint matches its own, `retrust` when it recorded no key for the machine (trusted without the account store or before 0.10.38), `unavailable` without a store of its own, or `sealed`: the same bundle as at trust time sealed to the machine's recorded `store_key`, bound to `store-delivery:<machine>:<issued_at>`. A revoked machine is refused by the machine protocol before anything is sealed. The computer opens it with its kept key, refuses one not newer than the last it installed (`issued_at`, against a replay on the way) or for another account store (endpoint), stores the new service token (and its expiry in `[cloud]`) and the content key (`key::update`: written when it has none, and replacing its own only with the same content account and ID key, every version it holds unchanged and an active version at least as new, as after a `cloud_rotate_key`; anything else is refused), and wakes the loop; nothing else changes (enrollment, mode). The coordinator also seals for a machine whose content key version is older than its active one (0.10.39), and names that version in every answer (`key`); the computer keeps it, and `cloud_status` › `delivery.key {here, coordinator, behind}` and `setup_status` (`account_store` `waiting`, or `attention` for `retrust`) say when this computer does not have the current key yet. The last answer is in private `cloud/delivery.json` and `cloud_status` shows it as `delivery {receives, state, problem, checked_at, installed_at}`.
- **`cloud_token`** (CLI `pta cloud token`, MCP `cloud_token`, GUI Cloud storage › Service token; `cloud_delivery_support`). Owner or an enabled portal account, read-only. The service token (stored, expiry) and how to replace it: on a coordinator, the conventional launcher `cloud-token-rotate` (defined, approved, `runs_here` for the owner on the local page or the local CLI), which runs `scripts/configure-cloud-access.py production --rotate`: Access rotates the token's secret with the client ID kept and the previous secret revoked at once, so the Worker's identity (the client ID) and `ACCOUNT_SHARES` stay and the script hands the new secret to `pta cloud connect` on stdin (`rebind` answers `rebound: false` and only the secret changes here); a portal account's is rotated by the operator (`--portal-user USER`). Then `computers`: every non-revoked machine given the store, whether it can receive (`receives`), whether it reported the current token (`token`: `current`, `previous`, `unknown`) and content key (`key`, 0.10.39) and when; `pending` counts the others. On a computer signed in to a coordinator it shows its own `delivery`. GUI: the list and «Replace the service token» (two taps, runs the launcher) where it runs here.
- **`cloud_lost`** (`app/cloud/lost.rs`, 0.10.46; CLI `pta cloud lost INSTALLATION --confirmed` or `pta cloud lost --machine ID --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_lost` with `installation` or `machine` and confirmation, GUI «Computers of this account» › «Lost or stolen» on the coordinator for every other computer, revoked or not, two taps; `cloud_lost_support`). Owner or an enabled portal account, only on a computer not signed in to a coordinator (the account's coordinator). One action for a lost or stolen computer, in four recorded steps: `machine` (revoke it on this coordinator, as `machine_revoke`; already revoked counts as done), `store` (revoke its installation in the account store, the machine's `store_installation` or the one given), `token` (run the approved launcher `cloud-token-rotate` through the same checks as `variable_launcher_run`, so only from this machine's window or local CLI; `pending` with what to do when it is not defined or approved, when called from the portal, or for a portal account, whose operator replaces it) and `key` (`cloud_rotate_key`, in `mirror` or `primary`). A step that does not apply is `not_needed` (no machine, no store installation, no store, mode `off`); a failed revocation stops before the token and the key; a pending or failed token still rotates the key. Each step's state, time and detail is kept in private `cloud/lost.json`: calling it again for the same computer continues an unfinished record and never repeats a settled step, another computer starts a new one, and one run at a time per profile (`state_conflict`). The reply is the record (`name`, `machine`, `installation`, `steps`, `complete`) with `next`; `cloud_token` shows the last record as `lost`, and the GUI shows an unfinished one under Cloud storage › Lost computer with each step and «Continue». The other computers receive the new token and key announced in their next poll (above).
- **`cloud_installations` and `cloud_revoke`** (CLI `pta cloud installations` and `pta cloud installations revoke ID --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_installations` and `cloud_revoke` with confirmation, GUI «Computers of this account», shown while live with more than one, «Revoke» with two taps). Owner or an enabled portal account (above). The list is `GET /v1/installations` (ID, label, host version, enrollment, last seen, revocation, `current`); revoking is `DELETE /v1/installations/{id}` for another installation of the same account (this one is refused: disconnect instead). The revoked computer keeps its data and can connect again with the service token. `cloud_erase` needs the others revoked first.
- **`cloud_disconnect`** (CLI `pta cloud disconnect [--forget-service-token] --confirmed`, MCP `cloud_disconnect` with confirmation, GUI «Disconnect this computer», two taps). Owner or an enabled portal account. Waits for any pass, revokes this installation with `DELETE /v1/installations/self` when the Worker answers (`store_notified`; a store that already refuses the credential counts as notified, an unreachable one leaves it listed until another installation revokes it), removes `CLOUD_STORE_TOKEN`, sets the mode to `off` and clears the installation; `--forget-service-token` also removes the Access service token and the endpoint. Always succeeds locally and is safe to repeat. The encrypted copy, the content key and the ledger stay, so connecting again continues.
- **No mixed versions** (0.10.42, Worker 0.5.0 feature `versions`). Every authenticated request refreshes the installation's recorded host version from the host's User-Agent (`personal-teams-assistant/X.Y.Z`), so `GET /v1/installations` shows what each computer runs now, not what it enrolled with. `POST /v1/enroll` is refused with `outdated` (409) from a host older than `MIN_HOST_VERSION` (default 0.10.37) or into an account with an active installation older than that; `cloud_connect` answers `state_conflict` with what to do. Before `cloud_migrate --adopt` and `cloud_promote` (a coordinator, or a computer joining `primary`), `cloud::outdated_elsewhere` lists the account's installations and refuses (`state_conflict`, `outdated: ["label (version)"]`) while another active one is older than 0.10.37; with a Worker without `versions` it does not check, because the recorded versions are the enrollment's.
- **Compatibility.** Hosts advertise `cloud_store_support`, `cloud_mirror_support`, `cloud_disconnect_support`, `cloud_primary_support`, `cloud_migration_support`, `cloud_installations_support`, `cloud_handoff_support`, `cloud_delivery_support` and `cloud_restore_support` in `control.json` and the CLI in `capabilities`; a CLI refuses `cloud_*` to a host without the first, `cloud_mirror`/`cloud_verify` to one without the second, `cloud_disconnect` to one without the third, `cloud_promote`/`cloud_pull` to one without the fourth `cloud_migrate`/`cloud_rollback`/`cloud_erase` to one without the fifth `cloud_installations`/`cloud_revoke` to one without the sixth `cloud_rotate_key` to one without the seventh `cloud_token` to one without the eighth and `cloud_backups`/`cloud_restore` to one without the ninth. A coordinator before 0.10.38 does not know `store_delivery`; its computers report `unsupported` and keep their credentials. The compare-and-swap fields go to the Worker only when used, so a 0.1 Worker keeps serving `mirror`; `promote` refuses a Worker without `cas`.

### Account setup and readiness

`app/readiness.rs` composes the first-run path ("add this computer to my account and configure the app") without a second implementation of anything: `setup_readiness` (CLI `pta setup status [--expect NAME]...`, MCP `setup_status`, GUI Home › Your account on this computer) is a read that calls the operations that already own each fact and orders them as a person meets them. It runs outside the operations lock, is refused to machine credentials, takes no authority from its arguments (`expect` is the only one: up to 20 variable names, validated like any name) and is listed for browser sessions so an ordinary portal account can read its own.

- **Coordinating.** The report's `coordinating` is true for a standalone installation that other non-revoked machines are registered with: it is the account's coordinator, so a GUI offers it no sign-in.
- **Steps.** `coordinator` (connected, signed out, a sign-in pending, ended without trust, or not wanted), `connection` (the latest `standing`: live, unknown, offline, revoked), `machine` (this computer on the coordinator's machine list, which is the same list every client shows), `configuration` (the portable state, loaded versus saved), `variables` (catalog availability; each `expect` name is `present` with id and version, `waiting` for a pending protected entry or `missing`; a consumer bound to a vanished variable is `attention`), `migration` (the journal; `done` only when verified), `providers` (the existing local-setup check), `roots` (count and paths of the directories this computer allows; none is a safe default), `teams`, `account_store` (the account store link and mirror from a live `cloud_status`: `not_applicable` when never connected or for a shared account, `optional` connected without a copy, `todo` not enrolled, `attention` for offline, revoked, unconfigured, a differing verification, blocked records or a service token within 30 days of expiry, `waiting` while the outbox holds changes, `done` when the copy is current) and `versions`. Each carries `scope` (`coordinator` or `this_machine`), `required`, `state` (`done`, `todo`, `waiting`, `attention`, `unknown`, `optional`, `not_applicable`), a fixed English `detail` (the GUI translates it by exact match; `scripts/test-ui-i18n.cjs` fails when a text has no catalog entry), small `facts` and `actions` naming a GUI place, a `pta` command or a tool call. `next` is the first required step that is open, else the first open optional one.
- **Distinct states.** Configured, running, received and sent are different facts: `teams` reports the Microsoft sign-in, whether another machine is selected, whether the assistant runs, and from the recorded jobs how many messages arrived and how many answers were sent (simulations excluded); only `running` with a sign-in is `done`. Installed, running and available versions are separate, and a newer installed version than the running one is `attention` (restart). Account scope and this-computer scope never merge.
- **Live check.** For a signed-in installation the report performs one coordinator exchange (the machine list) through the same account call as synchronization, so the standing it shows is current: an unreachable coordinator is `offline` (nothing is used, configuration stays readable), a refused credential is `revoked` (sign in again) and neither is confused with the other.
- **What it never does.** It never signs in, trusts a machine, enters, reveals or returns a value, creates a request, grants a directory, installs or signs in to a provider, changes an audience, starts Teams, migrates or updates anything; every step that needs one of those names the person or the operation. A browser session is never a machine: the `machine` step is `not_applicable` for it and the machine list is not touched. Variable descriptions, provider messages and names are not copied into `detail` or `facts` (names appear only for the caller's own `expect` list).

### Installation updates: discovery and staging

(Applying a prepared pair is [the next section](#applying-an-update-restart-and-rollback).)

`maintenance.rs` (core, no application dependencies), `maintenance/release.rs` (release archives) and `app/maintenance.rs` (host operations `crate_update_status`, `crate_update_check`, `crate_update_stage`, `crate_update_dismiss` and `crate_update_unattended`, which bypass the profile operations lock because a build takes minutes) let the owner of a computer find and prepare a newer release of this exact package. Discovery and staging **install nothing**; installing the prepared pair is [the next section](#applying-an-update-restart-and-rollback), and doing both without being asked is [unattended updating](#unattended-updating). The shared server's timer (`scripts/vps-update.py`) is independent and never calls any of it.

- **Versions.** Status keeps `running` (the host's own version), `installed` (read from Cargo's install record, or the install scripts' `install.json`, next to the executable) and `available` apart. `available` is the newest **stable, not yanked** version of `personal-teams-assistant` in the sparse index that is greater than both the installed and the running one (numeric `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` ordering, so 0.10.0 > 0.9.0); pre-releases and build metadata, yanked versions, lines about another crate and malformed lines are ignored, and an answer that does not describe this crate at all fails the check instead of reading as «nothing newer». `restart_required` is true when the installed version is newer than the running one (the existing outdated-host retirement handles it).
- **Pinned source.** The registry is the constant `https://index.crates.io` and the crate is `personal-teams-assistant`; the request is `GET` on the crate's index path, with no redirects, 20 s and 4 MiB bounds and no credentials. An archive installation's release comes from `release::Source::official()`: `CARGO_PKG_REPOSITORY` (checked to be `https://github.com/OWNER/NAME`) plus `/releases/download/vX.Y.Z/personal-teams-assistant-<target>-vX.Y.Z.{tgz|zip}` (`.zip` for a Windows target) and its `.sha256`, built in code from the version the registry listed and the target `install.json` recorded. No argument, description, browser field or model output chooses a URL, crate, registry, directory or command: `crate_update_stage` takes only `{version}` and the version must equal the newest the registry lists *at that moment* (a stale or forged one is `state_conflict`), and unknown or authority-bearing fields are rejected. The installation root and features come from the running executable's own location and Cargo's record, never from input. A source replacement in the user's own `CARGO_HOME` configuration is the user's configuration and is honored by Cargo.
- **Supported installations.** The executable must sit in `<root>/bin` with `<root>/.crates2.json` holding exactly one record for this package from crates.io (`registry+…crates.io-index` or `sparse+https://index.crates.io/`), a release profile, both binaries (`personal-teams-assistant`, `pta`) and no feature but `gui` (empty and deprecated, kept so such records still update). An installation made with `cargo binstall` (the release's prebuilt archive) has no `.crates2.json`: it is recognized by `<root>/.crates.toml` naming the package from crates.io with both binaries beside binstall's own `<root>/binstall/crates-v1.json`, and is marked `prebuilt`. The third kind, **`archive`**, is what the [one-command installers](#one-command-installation) leave: `<root>/install.json` (schema 1, `kind: "archive"`, this package, a stable version, the target and both binaries; Cargo's own record wins when both exist). `maintenance::archive` reads it and `inspect` turns it into an installation with `archive: true` and the recorded `target` (no feature choice); status reports `install.kind` `archive`, `cargo` or `binstall`, and a record that is not exactly valid gets a diagnostic (run the install command again) instead of being guessed. A development build, a `--path`/`--git`/custom-registry install or a custom feature gets a `diagnostic` and `can_stage: false` instead of a silently different package or feature set.
- **Authority.** Check, stage, dismiss and the unattended setting need `is_local_owner`: the control endpoint's own token or the owner's local page (`Transport::Local|Window`) on a profile that is an owner and not a shared portal account. A portal session, a machine credential and an ordinary account are refused (`authority_denied`); the portal allowlist admits only `crate_update_status`, which answers `{managed: "server", can_stage: false, message}` without versions, paths or build details. A model-supplied `confirmed` is not authority: the CLI (`--confirmed`) and MCP (`update_stage`, an `External` tool) ask the user, and the GUI button is the user's explicit request. No download or build starts on its own unless the owner turned [unattended updating](#unattended-updating) on for this computer; otherwise the host only checks the registry (read-only) about every 6 hours (first after 90 s) on an owner's supported installation, and the Home notice can be dismissed per release.
- **Staging.** `crate_update_stage` runs `cargo install personal-teams-assistant --version X.Y.Z --locked --root <profile>/maintenance/staged/X.Y.Z.partial/root --target-dir <profile>/maintenance/target --color never` plus the installed choices (`--no-default-features`, `--features gui`, `--all-features`, which all build the same package, and `--target` only when it differs from the compiler's host), in the background, one build per profile (`state_conflict` otherwise). The old executables are never opened for writing and keep running; any earlier prepared pair is replaced. A `prebuilt` installation instead runs `cargo binstall personal-teams-assistant --version =X.Y.Z --locked --no-confirm --root <the same staging root>` with `BINSTALL_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=true`, which fetches that release's archive from the repository's release (it compiles only when the archive is missing) and records it in the staging root's `.crates.toml`; it needs `cargo-binstall` on the build's `PATH` or in Cargo's `bin` (`not_ready` otherwise) and skips the `rust-version` check. An **archive** installation needs no Cargo: `release::stage` downloads the `.sha256` (at most 4 KiB) and the archive (at most 256 MiB) for the installation's own target over HTTPS only (`https_only`), with a 20 s connect and 15-minute total limit, no credentials and a redirect policy that follows at most five hops and only to the release host's own asset hosts (`objects.githubusercontent.com`, `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`, `github-releases.githubusercontent.com`, as the install scripts do); any other location is refused before a byte is read. The digest file's first field must be a SHA-256 and its second exactly the archive's name; the archive must have the announced length (else «interrupted») and that digest (else «does not match») before anything is unpacked. Unpacking (`tar`+`flate2`, or `zip` with deflate only) takes nothing but `<dir>/personal-teams-assistant` and `<dir>/pta` (`.exe` on Windows), each written new (`create_new`, 0755, at most 512 MiB; 2 GiB decompressed in all); an absolute path, `..`, a drive, a path outside the expected directory, a link or a special file refuses the whole archive, and other regular files (licence, readme) are skipped. The pair must then report the version (`verify_pair`, the same probes as below); the manifest records `archive: {name, sha256, size}` (omitted for Cargo kinds, so their journals stay readable by earlier versions). A 404 says the release has no archive for that target (yet: crates.io lists a version minutes before `binaries.yml` attaches its archives); every failure removes the partial directory and records the reason. For a Cargo kind, before building it checks that Cargo runs and satisfies the release's `rust-version` (`not_ready` with `rustup` advice otherwise) and, once built or fetched, verifies the pair (`verify`): Cargo's own record in the staging root names exactly this version from crates.io with the installed feature choice and both binaries; `pta --version --json` and `personal-teams-assistant --version` (a flag that prints one line and exits, so it never starts a host) report the same version; SHA-256 and size of each are stored in `manifest.json`. Only then is `X.Y.Z.partial` renamed to `X.Y.Z`. A failed build or verification removes the partial directory and records the error. `maintenance/target` is Cargo's build cache, kept between updates so a release recompiles only this crate and what changed (without it Cargo builds every dependency again in a temporary directory); it is removed before a build when it exceeds 8 GiB and after a build that ran out of disk space.
- **Build environment.** Cargo runs with `env_clear` and only an allowlist (`PATH`, home and Cargo/Rustup locations, temporary directories, Windows and macOS toolchain discovery variables, compiler and `pkg-config` locations, proxy and CA variables) plus fixed `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never`, `CARGO_TERM_PROGRESS_WHEN=never`, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`. Account variables, credentials, registry tokens, `RUSTFLAGS`, `CARGO_BUILD_*`, `PTA_*` and every other variable are dropped; registry credentials of another account are never imported. The captured output is bounded (last 20 lines of 300 characters, redacted), shown only to the local owner and never contains the environment; known failures (no C compiler or linker, a system library `pkg-config` cannot find, no disk space, network) add a fix.
- **State.** Private `<profile>/maintenance/status.json` (schema 1: last check time and result, newest release seen, dismissed version, last staging record with download progress, the unattended setting, the release unattended updating skips, why it waits and its audit; no path or secret) `staged/<version>/{root,manifest.json}` and the build cache `target/`. A build recorded as running by a process that no longer exists is reported `failed` («stopped before the build finished»). `state` is `idle`, `staging` (with `phase` `building`, `downloading` or `verifying` and compiled-package or byte progress), `staged` (only while the files still have the recorded sizes and the version is still newer than what is installed and running) or `failed`.
- **Surfaces.** GUI: a Home notice (prepare or dismiss; install once prepared) and Settings › Advanced › Updates (versions, diagnostics, progress, failure with the build tail, installation progress and outcome, the unattended choice on this computer's own page); in the portal the panel shows only the managed-server notice. CLI: `pta update [--machine NAME] [--confirmed]` (in a terminal it asks; elsewhere it needs `--confirmed`) and `pta update status|check|stage VERSION --confirmed|apply VERSION --confirmed|cancel|dismiss VERSION|unattended on|off --confirmed`. MCP: `update_latest`, `update_status`, `update_check`, `update_stage`, `update_apply`, `update_cancel`, `update_dismiss` (no tool changes the unattended setting). `status` makes no network call.
### Updating to the newest release in one request

`crate_update_latest` (CLI `pta update`, MCP `update_latest` without `machine`) and `machine_upgrade {machine}` (CLI `pta update --machine NAME`, MCP `update_latest` with `machine`, the **Update to X.Y.Z** button of each row in Settings › Machines, which needs a second press) chain the steps above for a person who just wants the newest release. They take no version: the host reads the pinned registry, and if something newer than what is installed and running exists it prepares that release (or reuses a prepared copy that still matches its manifest) and then makes the ordinary `crate_update_apply` request, with its deferral, restart, health check and rollback. A preparation still running is waited for in the background (one follower per profile) and installed when it ends; a failed preparation is recorded in the audit (`update_stage`) and left for the person, never retried. The reply carries `outcome` (`current`, `staging`, `deferred` or `applying`) and `target`; each request is audited as `update_requested` with `by` (`cli`, `local_page` or `remote`).

- **This computer.** `crate_update_latest` needs `is_local_owner` like every update operation; `machine_upgrade` naming this installation's own ID goes the same way (`own_latest`), so a portal session, or another computer forwarding through the coordinator, asking for the coordinator's own row is refused, and that row offers no button (`capabilities.upgrade` is true only for the local owner of a supported installation that can restart itself).
- **Another computer.** `machine_upgrade` is a one-shot fleet call like `machine_assistant`: the account's client (the portal, or a connected computer through `forward`) needs the account binding; the coordinator queues it only for a machine whose last poll carried `upgrade_support: true` (`maintenance::remote_capable`: its host owns its installation, which is supported and not owned by a hand-written unit), and waits up to 90 s for the answer. The worker runs `remote_latest`, which refuses unless its own host is the local owner of its installation and, on a Mac whose credentials are in the Keychain, refuses with the reason (the new version would wait for someone to allow it, so it is updated at the computer). A refusal's own message returns to the requester (at most 600 characters, this package's fixed texts). Remote agents never reach `crate_update_latest` or `machine_upgrade` (`agent::NEVER`).

### Applying an update: restart and rollback

`crate_update_apply {version}` (CLI `pta update apply VERSION --confirmed`, MCP `update_apply`, the GUI's Install update, which needs a second press), `crate_update_cancel` and the status fields `can_apply`, `apply`, `deferred` and `apply_unsupported` complete the update on the owner's computer. They need the same `is_local_owner` authority as staging, and the version must be the prepared one whose files still match `manifest.json` byte for byte. No path, URL, command or package is an argument. The portal allowlist does not admit them, so an ordinary account or a machine credential cannot replace executables, and the shared server's timer remains the only thing that updates the server.

- **Request.** The host holds the profile operations lock (so nothing new starts while it decides), takes the maintenance lock, recovers an interrupted earlier update, and **defers** if anything an update would interrupt is running in any affected profile: the profile itself and the isolated profiles of a portal under it. Checked: Teams jobs being answered, sent or refined (`assistant.db`), an activity review or task being written, implementation runs and child sends (`implementation/registry.json`, `runs.db` agent and request runs), a workflow run advancing a stage (in memory: the engine's step, a director session included), a migration, a protected variable entry waiting for its value, and a profile operation that does not release the lock within two seconds (a configuration or variable write, a chat). A database that cannot be read counts as work. A deferral is `state: deferred` with fixed, path-free reasons; nothing is stopped, no journal is written, and the request is retried every 30 seconds until it can proceed or `crate_update_cancel` withdraws it. The request also refuses when the safety copy or the new executables would not fit on disk (`not_ready`, with the fix), and on a headless host started by a systemd unit someone wrote by hand (`INVOCATION_ID` with `--headless` and without `--service`, such as the shared server's), which would start a second copy: that one updates through its unit (`cargo install … --locked --force`, then restart the unit). A host whose profile has a current [start-at-login](#start-at-login) entry for the installed executable is updated like any other, through its manager (`intent.service` in the journal).
- **Journal and lock.** `<profile>/maintenance/journal.json` (atomically replaced, private) records the update's ID, versions, install root, the prepared manifest, what the previous host was doing (launch flags `--headless`/`--host`/`--service`, assistant running, active subscriptions, tunnel, whether a start-at-login entry runs it), the affected profiles, the digests of the executables being replaced, the data copies and each completed step, in the phases `requested`, `stopping`, `backing_up`, `replacing`, `restarting`, `verifying`, `committed`, `rolling_back`, `rolled_back` and `failed`. `apply.lock` is held by whoever drives the update for as long as it does, so a second update cannot run and a dead driver releases it. An unfinished journal whose lock is free and that was last written more than 60 s ago is an interrupted update: `recover` undoes it (status, a host start and the CLI's host start all do this first); within 60 s it is the hand-over between host and helper and is left alone. No host is started while an update is in progress (`pta` says to try again; `PTA_UPDATE_HANDOVER` marks the host the helper itself starts), and `pta update status` reads the journal directly while the host is down.
- **Helper.** There is no separate updater: the helper is `personal-teams-assistant --maintenance-apply`, run from the **prepared** copy (so it never holds the installed files, which matters on Windows). It re-checks the work list, asks the running host to quit with the ordinary `app_quit` (assistant and tunnel stop first), waits for the profile lock to be released (a host that does not quit leaves everything unchanged: `rolled_back`, «nothing was changed»), copies the data, replaces the executables, starts the new host with the previous launch flags (a host a person launched had none and now runs in the background; the restart never opens the page), asks it to start the assistant only if it was running, waits for the health check and commits. When a start-at-login entry runs the host, the helper starts the new host (and, on rollback, the previous one) **through the service manager** (`launchctl kickstart`, `systemctl --user start`, `schtasks /Run`), so the manager tracks the process instead of believing its entry stopped; it first writes `maintenance/handover` with the update's ID, which lets that host start while the journal is unfinished (the manager cannot pass `PTA_UPDATE_HANDOVER`). The quit stays the ordinary `app_quit`: the host exits successfully, which no entry restarts; if it does not quit within a minute the manager is asked to stop it. The helper survives the host it replaces on every manager: a separate process group under launchd (`AbandonProcessGroup`), `KillMode=process` in the unit, a detached process on Windows.
- **Replacement.** Before anything changes, every database (`*.db` in the data directory, the profile and `implementation/`) is copied with `VACUUM INTO`, plus the profile's `*.toml` and `implementation/registry.json`, and the installation's records (Cargo's two, or the install scripts' `install.json`), under `maintenance/backup/<id>/`. Each executable is replaced by: a copy of the prepared file next to it (verified against the manifest digest), the live file **renamed aside** (`.name.<id>.previous`; Windows allows renaming a running image, which is why nothing is overwritten in place) and the copy renamed into its name, with bounded retries for a scanner holding the file; then this package's entry in `.crates.toml`/`.crates2.json` is replaced by the prepared root's, leaving other packages' entries alone, so Cargo later sees the new version and the same install root, features and target. A crash or failure at any step leaves a state the undo understands; every step has an inverse that is safe to repeat.
- **Health and rollback.** The new host must answer a snapshot with the new version; if the assistant was running it must run again (started once by the helper, never retried as a send), with active subscriptions and the tunnel as before. An assistant that ran while its Microsoft session needed renewal (`Store::session_needs_renewal`: the last `oauth` event in `assistant.db` is the `renewal_required` the token refresh records, once per change, when Entra refuses the saved session with `invalid_grant`/`interaction_required`; a sign-in records `authorized`) was not answering and cannot start until the person signs in (the snapshot, and so `pta --json status`, reports it as the additive `microsoft_session_needs_renewal`, which the [server's updater](delivery.md#server-updates-scriptsvps-updatepy) reads too): the journal marks it `intent.degraded`, its restart is attempted once and neither it nor its subscriptions count against the new version, and the notes say to sign in again. Nothing asks Microsoft anything for this. Failure of any step, or no health within three minutes, rolls back: the new host is stopped, both previous executables and Cargo's records are restored and checked against the digests taken before, the previous host is started with the same flags and the assistant restored (the safety copy is then deleted; it stays after a failed rollback or an interrupted update). The data keeps what happened meanwhile; only if the previous version does not start on it is the safety copy put back (`data_restored`), and if that fails too the outcome is `failed` with a recovery path (reinstall the exact version with `cargo install … --locked --force`, or run the install command again with `--version` for an archive installation; nothing is deleted). The schema of the data is additive, so the previous version normally reads what the new one wrote; the copy covers the case it does not. The helper never touches a Graph send or a work-item write: it waits for them (deferral) and the assistant's own start/stop owns Teams ownership, the lease and the account, none of which the update changes.
- **After success.** `committed` is the only success, written after the health check; `installed` or `running` alone never mean done. For an archive installation `install.json` is rewritten (version, archive name, its SHA-256, `installed_at`; every other field kept) right before `committed` is recorded: a failure there rolls back, and a rollback or an interrupted update restores the record from the safety copy with the executables. The helper then refreshes the **application's own** skill copies: only directories registered by `pta skill install` and still identical to the manifest `.pta-skill.json` it wrote (version and SHA-256 per file); modified or extended copies are never overwritten and are reported with how to reinstall, and the notes always say that agents keeping `pta mcp` running must reconnect to read the new catalog. The skill and catalog embedded in the binary report the new version immediately. The backup, `.previous` files and the prepared pair are swept by the new host once no update runs (a program still holding an old file on Windows only delays that).
- **Not covered.** The update cannot see work in a profile that is not under this profile (an unrelated host started with another `PTA_PROFILE_DIR`); a host that exits before the helper's last check is fine, but one that starts work between the helper's check and the quit is stopped by the quit like by `pta app quit`. A rollback after the new version has run cannot recover work only the new version could read. Cargo's own lock is not taken: do not run `cargo install` for this package while an update is applying.

### Unattended updating

A per-computer setting (`crate_update_unattended {enabled}`; CLI `pta update unattended on|off --confirmed`; the checkbox in Settings › Advanced › Updates on the local page; `install.sh --unattended-updates` / `install.ps1 -UnattendedUpdates` at installation, which otherwise print how to turn it on) lets a supported installation prepare and install newer releases by itself.

- **Who and where.** It lives in `<profile>/maintenance/status.json`, never in `config.toml`, the knowledge map or the [portable configuration](#account-configuration), so no synchronization, import or other computer can turn it on (a test projects the portable document before and after). It is off for every existing installation.
