Only `is_local_owner` changes it: a portal session, a machine credential and an ordinary account are refused (`authority_denied`), the portal allowlist does not admit the method, the remote agent transport lists it as never reachable, no MCP tool sets it and the worker protocol cannot call it. Turning it on needs a supported installation not owned by a hand-written unit (`not_ready` otherwise). Every change and every attempt is appended to its audit (`history`, the last 50 events: `unattended_on`/`off` with `cli` or `local_page`, `unattended_stage`, `unattended_apply`, `unattended_deferred` when the reasons change, `unattended_waiting`, `unattended_cancelled`, `unattended_result` with the outcome) and logged; status shows the last ten.
- **A pass.** The background loop (first after 90 s, then every 10 minutes; the registry check itself stays due every 6 hours) runs `unattended`: nothing when it is off, the installation is unsupported, an update applies or a staging runs. It records the outcome of a finished update once: a rollback or failure marks that release `skip` (`reason: failed`). Otherwise, for the newest release newer than what is installed and running: a skipped release is left alone (a person can still install it); without a prepared pair it stages it (a failed staging, or one refused before it began, is retried after an hour, never in a loop); with one it asks for the ordinary `crate_update_apply` request marked `unattended`, which defers for the same busy work as a person's request (retried every 30 s) and, in addition, while the assistant runs (or reports `running`) with a Microsoft session that needs renewal: a restart would turn a degraded but answering assistant into a stopped one, so the reason says to sign in again and the update proceeds afterwards. Cancelling an unattended wait marks that release `skip` (`reason: cancelled`); turning the setting off withdraws a wait it started. The apply, journal, health check and rollback are the ones above; the journal's `intent.unattended` says who asked.
- **macOS and the Keychain.** Each build of this package carries its own ad-hoc linker signature, and the Keychain's access list trusts the signature that created an item, so a new version asks the person to allow it before it reads `personal-teams-assistant.default` (architecture §4). A Mac where `security::keychain_in_use` finds an item of that service (a metadata listing with `/usr/bin/security`, which opens no dialog) therefore stages unattended but does not install: status shows `unattended.waiting` and the prepared update waits for the person to install it, at the computer, and choose Always Allow. A Mac whose credentials come from files or variables updates unattended like Linux and Windows.
- **Fleet view.** Each worker poll carries `host_version` and `update: {state, pending, failed, unattended}` (`maintenance::report`, from local files only); see [§16](#16-work-item-implementation-and-execution-machines).

### Headless host

```sh
cargo install personal-teams-assistant --locked   # every OS; or `cargo binstall personal-teams-assistant`
personal-teams-assistant --start                  # foreground; `--start` starts the service
```

- The host has no window on any operating system, and Linux, macOS and Windows build the same package (no GTK or WebKit): default features, `--no-default-features` and the empty `gui` feature are equivalent. `--headless` and `PTA_HEADLESS` are accepted and ignored, so units, launchers and install records that carry them keep working; a hand-written systemd unit (the shared server's) keeps `--headless`, which is how an update recognizes a host that unit owns (§3, applying an update). On a personal computer prefer [`pta service install`](#start-at-login), whose entry updates restart themselves. It is operated with `pta`, `pta mcp`, the [local page](#owners-local-page) and, for an account, the portal. On Windows it is a GUI-subsystem program, so it never opens a console window.
- It runs in the foreground: `pta app quit`, SIGTERM or Ctrl-C stop service and tunnel before exiting. A second host on the same profile never starts: a person's launch opens the running host's page instead (§3). Logs go to stderr (no ANSI outside a terminal), fit for journald.
- `--start` tries to start the service; if it fails, the host stays alive to diagnose with `pta status/doctor/audit`.
- Remote Microsoft login: `pta auth microsoft login --no-browser` returns the URL; after authorizing on any device, the browser cannot open `http://localhost:PORT/?code=…`; that URL is pasted into `pta auth microsoft finish --redirect 'URL'` and the host forwards it to its own loopback listener (`forward_microsoft_redirect`: same pending port, rejected at once if the callback does not accept it). Alternative: migrate an existing profile with `pta config import` plus `STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY`.
- The server still needs a stable HTTPS URL to the listener (Cloudflare tunnel with a token) and must not coexist with another active instance of the same account: it would duplicate answers and cause loops in the personal chat.

### Start at login

`app/service.rs` (host operations `service_status`, `service_install`, `service_uninstall`; CLI `pta service status|install --confirmed|uninstall --confirmed`; MCP `service_status`, `service_install` and `service_uninstall`, the last two `External`; Settings › Start at login on the local page; `service_support`) keeps **one per-user entry per profile** in the operating system's service manager, without administrator rights. It needs `is_local_owner` like updates: the portal, a machine credential, a remote agent (`agent.rs` denies all three: they change the host computer itself) and a shared account are refused. No argument exists: the executable is the running host's own and the profile is the host's.

- **Identity.** The entry's name carries 12 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the profile path, so another profile gets its own and the name reveals no path: `dev.personalteams.assistant.host.<id>` (launchd label), `personal-teams-assistant-<id>.service` (systemd), `PersonalTeamsAssistant-<id>` (Task Scheduler). Every definition carries a fixed marker; an entry with that name but without the marker is never changed or removed (`state_conflict`).
- **Definitions.** macOS: `~/Library/LaunchAgents/<label>.plist` (owner-only), loaded with `launchctl bootstrap gui/UID`, `RunAtLoad`, `KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit: false}` (a crash restarts it after 30 s, a quit or a second start does not), `AbandonProcessGroup`. Linux: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/systemd/user/<unit>`, `daemon-reload` and `enable` (no `--now`), `Type=simple`, `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=30`, `KillMode=process`, `WantedBy=default.target`, every `ExecStart` word quoted with `%`/`$` escaped. Windows: a task registered with `schtasks /Create /XML` (UTF-16, a temporary file under `<profile>/service/` removed at once) with a `LogonTrigger` for `DOMAIN\user`, `InteractiveToken`, `LeastPrivilege`, `IgnoreNew`, no time limit, no battery conditions and three restarts on failure; the host is a GUI-subsystem program and `schtasks` runs through `security::program`, so nothing opens a console window and nothing needs WSL or PowerShell.
- **What it starts.** `<exe> --host --service`, plus `--profile-dir DIR` when the profile is not the standard one (a task cannot set variables; the host turns it into `PTA_PROFILE_DIR` before anything else runs). `--host` means no page opens. **Environment:** launchd and systemd get only `PATH` = the executable's directory, `~/.local/bin`, `~/.cargo/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin` (macOS), `/usr/local/bin`, `/usr/bin`, `/bin`, `/usr/sbin`, `/sbin`, besides what the manager itself sets (`HOME`, `USER`…); the host still looks for the coding CLIs, `az` and `gh` in the per-user install locations (`llm::locate`) as it does when a desktop session starts it, and `git` is on that `PATH`. A task inherits the user's own environment. No account variable, credential, `NAME_FILE` or token is ever written into a definition: the host reads credentials from the system store or the profile as always.
- **Install, uninstall, status.** `install` is idempotent (`changed: false` when the entry is current and loaded or enabled); with an entry for another executable it replaces it, except while the manager is running that entry (`state_conflict`: quit, then install again). `uninstall` removes only that entry (launchd `bootout` while not running and the plist; systemd `disable`, the unit and `daemon-reload`; `schtasks /Delete`); a host it is running keeps running until it quits. `status` reports `supported`, `manager`, `name`, `installed`, `current` (it starts this executable for this profile), `running` (as the manager says: launchd `state = running`, systemd `ActiveState=active`, the task's numeric last result 267009, never its localized status text), `last_error` (the manager's exit code or result, no text of the program), `linger` on Linux, `this_host` (this host was started by the entry) and `notes`: no path. On Linux the notes say when lingering is off: the user manager, and the host with it, stops at logout unless `loginctl enable-linger`; without a user manager (`systemctl --user` does not answer) install answers `not_ready`.
- **One host.** A manager start while a host owns the profile exits with status 0 (the profile is owned; `KeepAlive`/`Restart=on-failure` do not restart it). The entry installed from a host that `pta` started does not take over that host: it starts at the next login, or at the next update.
- **The assistant across a login.** The person's `start_assistant`, `stop_assistant` and `restart` (from the CLI, MCP, the GUI, the portal's machine control, a kept update) are written to `<profile>/assistant-intent.json` (owner-only); internal stops (quit, a settings restart, a lost lease) do not change it, and declining to keep it running after an update writes «stopped». A host started with `--service` (and not `--start`) reads it: if the assistant was left running it asks `teams_allowed` (this computer is the selected Teams machine and no other machine holds the lease; a connected worker asks its coordinator), and only then calls the ordinary start, which checks the lease again. An unknown answer (no network yet) is asked again up to ten times, 30 s apart; a refusal leaves the assistant stopped and keeps the intent for a later login. The start itself is asked once. The entry never starts the assistant otherwise, so it can never start a second Teams machine.
- **Tests.** `service/tests.rs` builds the three definitions and checks the command sequences of install, uninstall, start and stop on every platform through a scripted `Runner`; `tests/service.rs` registers a real entry for a temporary profile on the CI runners (Linux with lingering enabled for the runner's user, macOS, Windows), starts it through the manager as a login would, checks `pta status` and a second start, and removes it; it runs only with `PTA_SERVICE_LIVE_TEST=1`.

### One-command installation

`scripts/install/install.sh` (macOS and Linux, POSIX `sh`) and `scripts/install/install.ps1` (Windows PowerShell 5.1 or later, no WSL, no administrator rights) are attached to every release with their `.sha256` and build provenance by `binaries.yml`. They install the prebuilt archive for the current user without Rust or a compiler; the portal shows the command (Settings › Machines › Connect this computer, [§16](#16-work-item-implementation-and-execution-machines)). Every statement of `install.sh` runs inside `main`, called on its last line, so a truncated `curl | sh` runs nothing.

- **Target and version.** `uname` (an x86-64 shell under Rosetta on Apple silicon gets the native archive) maps to `aarch64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-apple-darwin` or `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` (glibc); Windows needs AMD64. Anything else (Linux on ARM, musl, FreeBSD) changes nothing and points to `cargo install`. The version is `--version X.Y.Z` or the repository's latest release (the `releases/latest` redirect, not followed further).
- **Download and digest.** `https://github.com/<repository>/releases/download/vX.Y.Z/<archive>` and its `.sha256`, over HTTPS only (`curl --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2`; `HttpWebRequest`/`WebClient` with TLS 1.2), following only GitHub's own redirect to `objects.githubusercontent.com`, `release-assets.githubusercontent.com` or `github-releases.githubusercontent.com` (any other location is refused before anything is downloaded). The digest file must name the archive and match its SHA-256 before anything is unpacked or run; then `gh attestation verify ARCHIVE --repo <repository>` runs when `gh` is installed and signed in (a refusal stops the install), and the script says when provenance could not be checked. The unpacked `pta --version --json` must report the version. A mismatch, a missing asset or an unsupported target changes nothing and says what to do.
- **Root and swap.** Default root `~/.local/share/personal-teams-assistant` (macOS and Linux) or `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\personal-teams-assistant`, with both binaries in `<root>/bin` (so `inspect` finds `bin/`), `--root`/`-Root` or `PTA_INSTALL_ROOT` to choose another. Everything is prepared in `<root>/.stage.*`; only then is the running host quit (`pta app quit`; its assistant intent is kept), `bin` moved aside, the new `bin` moved in (the previous one restored if that fails) and the record written. Running it again repairs a missing or damaged binary or upgrades in place.
- **Record.** `<root>/install.json`: `{"schema":1,"kind":"archive","package":"personal-teams-assistant","version","target","bins":["personal-teams-assistant","pta"],"repository","archive","sha256","installed_at"}`, read by `maintenance::archive` as above.
- **PATH.** macOS and Linux: a link `~/.local/bin/pta` when that directory exists and is already on `PATH` (and holds no other `pta`), otherwise one marked block (`# >>> personal-teams-assistant (install.sh) >>>` … `<<<`) that prepends `<root>/bin` when missing, written to each existing `~/.profile`, `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.bash_login`, `~/.bashrc`, `~/.zprofile`, `~/.zshrc` and the login shell's own file, replaced in place on every run (never duplicated; a linked dotfile stays a link); fish gets `conf.d/personal-teams-assistant.fish`. Windows: the user `Path` in `HKCU\Environment` through the registry API, keeping its value kind and every other entry (never `setx`), then `WM_SETTINGCHANGE`.
- **Service and account.** It starts the new host from the user's own session, runs `pta service install --confirmed`, quits that host and starts the entry through the manager (`launchctl kickstart`, `systemctl --user start`, `schtasks /Run`), so the host runs as a login service when the script ends; without a user manager it says so and leaves the host running. With `--origin`/`-Origin` (validated as `machine_login` validates a coordinator) it reads `machine_settings`: already connected to that origin keeps the identity, connected elsewhere says to disconnect first, otherwise it runs `pta auth machine login ORIGIN --no-browser`, opens the approval URL itself (the service has no desktop session to open it), prints the code and waits with `pta auth machine finish --wait`. The command carries no secret; trust is the existing browser confirmation and grants nothing else.
- **Existing installations.** A profile is never created, replaced or removed by the scripts; an existing one (a former desktop or Cargo installation) is reported and adopted as it is, with its machine identity. A Cargo or `cargo binstall` record of this package in `CARGO_HOME` or under the `pta` on `PATH` stops the script before any change, with the commands to switch (`pta service uninstall --confirmed`, `pta app quit`, `cargo uninstall personal-teams-assistant`, run again).
- **Unattended updates.** Only with `--unattended-updates`/`-UnattendedUpdates` does the script run `pta update unattended on --confirmed` once the host runs; otherwise it prints that command. The archive installation it leaves updates itself through [the updater](#installation-updates-discovery-and-staging) either way when the person asks.
- **Coding CLIs.** With `--integrations`/`-Integrations`, for each of `claude` and `codex` found: `pta skill install ~/.<cli>/skills/personal-teams-assistant` (kept when it exists) and the MCP registration `pta mcp config` describes (`claude mcp add --scope user personal-teams-assistant -- PTA mcp`, `codex mcp add personal-teams-assistant -- PTA mcp`) unless one is registered; without the flag it prints those commands.
- **Uninstall.** `install.sh --uninstall` / `install.ps1 -Uninstall`: `pta service uninstall --confirmed`, quit the host, remove the link or marked blocks (or the user `Path` entry), `<root>/bin` and the record; the profile, skill copies and MCP registrations stay.
- **Tests.** `binaries.yml` lints both scripts (`shellcheck`, `PSScriptAnalyzer`) and runs `scripts/install/test-install.sh` (macOS, both archives, the Intel one under Rosetta; Linux with lingering) and `test-install.ps1` (Windows PowerShell) against the archive each job just built, from a local test source that only `PTA_INSTALL_TEST=1` with `--test-source`/`-TestSource` accepts, into a temporary root and `PTA_PROFILE_DIR`: digest, missing-asset, unsupported-target, Cargo-copy and plain-HTTP refusals change nothing; then install, the record, `pta --version` from a new shell, `update status` (`kind: archive`, `supported: true`, unattended updates off), `--unattended-updates` on a repeated run, the service, a failed upgrade that keeps the installation, repair and idempotence (one PATH block, the same machine ID, the adopted profile) and uninstall.

## 4. Profile, data and credentials

**This is what keeps the user's session: do not change names or paths.**

| Item | Location |
| --- | --- |
| Profile (application identifier `dev.personalteams.assistant`) | macOS `~/Library/Application Support/dev.personalteams.assistant/`; Windows `%APPDATA%\dev.personalteams.assistant\`; Linux `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/dev.personalteams.assistant/` |
| Profile files | `config.toml`, `knowledge-map.toml`, `cloudflared-path.txt`, `control.json`, `control.lock`, `host-path.txt`, `settings-rollback.json` (transient journal), `skills/` |
| Data directory | `config.server.data_dir` (the imported one is kept; it may be outside the profile). In a new profile: the profile's own on macOS/Windows, `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/dev.personalteams.assistant/` on Linux |
| Data | `assistant.db` (service), `desktop-chat.db` (local chat/simulation), `activity-registration.db` (daily task snapshots, runs and legacy decisions), `todo-cache.db` (process metadata cache), `activity-cache.db` (legacy evidence cache), `knowledge-index.db` (sections of repository sources and verified notes, with optional vectors; a cache of authorized sources plus the notes), `instance.lock`, `repositories/` (GitHub clones) |
| Credentials | macOS Keychain / Credential Manager, service `personal-teams-assistant.default`, account = credential name. Linux: files `<profile>/credentials/default/NAME` (0600, directory 0700) |
| Account variables | `<profile>/variables/variables.db` (names, descriptions, versions and ciphertext) and, in a separate private directory, `<profile>/keys/master-N.key` (wrapping keys). Neither is a credential in the table above and neither is part of the published package |

Resolving a secret (`security::secret`): application names use account Variables (see below); internal credentials keep `NAME_FILE` → variable `NAME` → system store (Keychain/Credential Manager, or the file store on Linux, configured by `configure_credentials`). `secret_source` only inspects metadata (on macOS it uses `/usr/bin/security find-generic-password` without decrypting). Known credentials: `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` (required at start only if DeepSeek is active in `llm.chain`; otherwise `credentials list` shows it as `unused`), `GRAPH_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (both generated at the first start if missing), `CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_TOKEN` (tunnel mode with a token), `GITHUB_OAUTH_TOKENS`, the account store link's `CLOUD_ACCESS_CLIENT_ID`, `CLOUD_ACCESS_CLIENT_SECRET` and `CLOUD_STORE_TOKEN` (written by `cloud_connect`, never required to start; see «Account store link»), and those mapped in `[secrets]` (e.g. `AZURE_DEVOPS_TOKEN`). `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` is retired (`app::RETIRED_CREDENTIALS`): nothing reads it, it cannot be set, and a stored one is listed as `retired` so it can be removed; it is never deleted automatically.

**Local development without Keychain prompts (macOS).** Every `cargo build` produces a new ad-hoc signature, so the Keychain treats each build as a different application and asks again for the items of `personal-teams-assistant.default`. Resolve the credentials from private files instead. Bootstrap once (each read may ask for the login password; let the human click "Always Allow" — `/usr/bin/security` is Apple-signed, so that grant sticks):

```sh
mkdir -p ~/.config/pta && chmod 700 ~/.config/pta
for n in STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY GRAPH_WEBHOOK_SECRET DEEPSEEK_API_KEY AZURE_DEVOPS_TOKEN GITHUB_OAUTH_TOKENS CLOUDFLARE_TUNNEL_TOKEN; do
  f="$HOME/.config/pta/$n"
  if v=$(security find-generic-password -w -s personal-teams-assistant.default -a "$n" 2>/dev/null); then
    printf '%s\n' "$v" > "$f" && chmod 600 "$f"
  else
    rm -f "$f"
  fi
done
```

Then `source scripts/dev-env.sh` before `cargo run`: it exports `NAME_FILE` for every non-empty file in `~/.config/pta` (override the directory with `PTA_DEV_CREDENTIALS_DIR`). `STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` must keep its current value or `assistant.db` cannot be decrypted. Reads use the files; `pta credentials set` and the GitHub login still write to the Keychain.

Invariants:

- `STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (base64 of 32 bytes) encrypts the OAuth tokens in `vault` with AES-256-GCM-SIV (AAD `teams-oauth-v1`). It cannot be replaced or deleted while `assistant.db` exists; importing a different key is rejected (`validate_state_key`). A missing key is created on the first Microsoft sign-in or start; when `assistant.db` already holds a session (or cannot be read) the original key must be imported first, while a database without a session has nothing sealed and takes a new key (`holds_session`).
- A database is never reused with another tenant/client/user (`protect_identity`); one opened under the example registration that holds no Microsoft session belongs to nobody and takes the real registration.
- Profile writes: temporary 0600 file + atomic rename. The host reads and writes `config.toml`, `knowledge-map.toml` and `cloudflared-path.txt` only through the settings store (`store/settings.rs`, `LocalSettings`); its `commit` writes a journal and rolls back on failure, and opening the profile recovers a pending journal.
- Verified notes, answer evaluations and approved examples (`notes`, `question_feedback` and `examples` in `knowledge-index.db`) are read and written only through the notes store (`store/notes.rs`, `NotesStore`). Its local implementation (`LocalNotes`) writes each record and its search document in one transaction; the index's other documents (repositories, questions, organization) are a cache it does not touch.
- The audit history, the finished rows of `assistant.db` › `jobs`, is read and written only through the audit store (`store/audit.rs`, `AuditStore`): the worker's finished `record`, the `audit` listing, the user's rating and the question association. Its local implementation (`LocalAudit`) shares the state store's connection. The rows in flight (`pending`, `processing`, `refining`, `sending`), the worker's state machine, the send nonces (`outputs`), `vault`, `subscriptions`, `conversation_context`, `events` and `activity_cache` stay in `state::Store`; the `audit` listing joins the in-flight rows with the history, newest first.
- The activity record in `activity-registration.db` (daily task snapshots and summaries by day, finished runs by ID, the legacy decisions and claims, read only) is read and written only through the activity store (`store/activity.rs`, `ActivityStore`), whose local implementation (`LocalActivity`) shares the database's connection; the record source reads it read-only. Claiming, describing, staging and recovering a run (its slot keeps a scheduled run from repeating) and the legacy entries' recovery stay in `activity::store::Database`, whose run listing joins the running runs with the finished ones.
- The implementation record in `implementation/runs.db` (finished agent runs by launch ID, finished request runs by ID) is read and written only through the implementation store (`store/implementation.rs`, `ImplementationStore`), whose local implementation (`LocalImplementation`) shares the database's connection: finishing a run or a request run goes through it. Runs planning or running, requests generating or sending, the change feed (`agent_run_events`), the coordinator's session index, remote-session access and the machine files (`registry.json`, `connection.json`, `installation.json`, `requests/`) stay in `app::implementation::Database` and the machine; its listings join the in-flight rows with the finished ones.
- The organization (`[organization]` in the map) and its work item guides (`[[work_item_guides]]`) are read as one unit through the organization store (`store/organization.rs`, `OrganizationStore`) by the knowledge index's organization documents and the implementation agents. Its local implementation (`LocalOrganization`) keeps them in the map and writes through the settings store's journaled commit; the settings editors (GUI, CLI, MCP) save them with the rest of the map in one settings commit, and validation and `updated_at` stay with `save_settings`.
- Directories 0700 / the account's ACL on Windows.
- On Linux the file store keeps values in clear (protected only by permissions), next to the database whose tokens `STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` encrypts. On a server, prefer systemd credentials or a secret manager mounted through `NAME_FILE`.

## 5. Configuration

`Config` (`src/config.rs`) and `KnowledgeMap` (`src/knowledge.rs`) use `deny_unknown_fields`. **Do not remove or rename fields**: existing profiles (and older hosts sharing the profile) would stop loading. Add fields only with `#[serde(default)]`. The single exception is the retired `[jev]` section of profiles written before 0.6.1: `Config::retired_reviewer` (`rename = "jev"`, `IgnoredAny`, `skip_serializing`) accepts it and drops it, so it disappears the next time the profile is saved. A host or CLI older than 0.6.1 requires that section and cannot load a profile saved by 0.6.1 or later.

| Section | Relevant fields |
| --- | --- |
| `server` | `bind` (must be loopback), `public_url` (HTTPS origin), `data_dir`, `cloudflare_tunnel`, optional canonical `webhook_prefix` for portal profiles |
| `graph` | `tenant_id`, `client_id`, `user_id` (UUID; nil until login), `discover_all_chats` or `allowed_chats`, `self_chat {id,user_id,enabled_at}`, `channels` (legacy, must be empty); what this computer receives and answers |
| `teams_reading` | the account's chats read on request, off and omitted while unchanged: `all_chats`, `personal_chat`, `chats` (≤500 canonical IDs); see [Teams reading on request](#teams-reading-on-request) |
| `llm` | `style`; `language` (`es` by default or `en`: the language of the GUI, of everything sent to Teams and of activity drafts; overrides the style); `chain`: ordered list `{provider, model, effort, enabled}` (`codex`, `claude`, `opencode`, `deepseek`; up to 16 model choices, repeated providers allowed, at least one active). Legacy `provider`/`model`: apply only with an empty `chain` (DeepSeek, effort `max`) |
| `policy` | `dry_run`, `greeting`, `max_context_chars` (256–32000), `max_message_age_seconds` (30–3600), `sensitive_patterns`, `allowed_senders`; `max_answer_chars` and `max_detailed_answer_chars` are kept for compatibility but no longer applied |
| `secrets` | `"secret://..." = "CREDENTIAL_NAME"` (allowlist of references; never values) |
| `knowledge_index` | optional `embeddings` (`url` HTTPS or loopback HTTP, `model`, `secret_ref` mapped in `[secrets]`) for semantic search of repositories and notes; omitted when empty |
| `message_style` | off by default and omitted while unchanged: `enabled`, `label` (≤80 characters, one line; empty = the default in `llm.language`), `position` (`top`/`bottom`), `accent` (`#RRGGBB` stripe), `band` (`accent`, `emphasis`, `good`, `warning`, `attention`, `none`), `label_color` (`default`, `subtle`, `accent`, `good`, `warning`, `attention`), `icon` (an emoji before the label), `emoji` (any one emoji, up to 16 characters, not ASCII but for keycaps; empty or omitted = 🤖), `band_height` (the band's least height, 24–160 px; 0 or omitted fits the text), `stripe_width` (2–16 px, default 4), `text_size` and `label_size` (`small`, `default`, `medium`, `large`, `extralarge`; defaults `default` and `small`); an account setting: portable through a coordinator and in the account store's `config` record, so every computer of the account sends in it; `emoji` and `band_height` are written only once changed, so hosts that predate them keep installing the style. The running service reads the style from its profile file for each send (`Graph::message_style`), so a style the account syncs to a running computer applies without a restart. See §6 Send |
| `activity_registration` | disabled by default; `enabled`, advisory `daily_hours` (9), daily/interval schedule and workdays/time zone, Azure `sources`, actual `effort_field` and `effort_fields` (the field by work item type, such as `Bug = "Custom.EsfuerzoReal"`; omitted while empty so older hosts keep loading the account's configuration); legacy write fields remain accepted but cannot enable writes; see the [CLI guide](../desktop/skills/personal-teams-assistant/references/activity-registration.md) |

`validate_teams_setup` (host) also requires a real public URL, real IDs and a connected account before starting Teams. The local chat does not need them.

## 6. Message pipeline

Input: webhook or polling → `Store.enqueue(resource)` → worker → `Pipeline::process` (`src/pipeline.rs`).

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  M[Incoming message] --> E{Eligible and clean?}
  E -- no --> I[Ignored]
  E -- yes --> Q[Identify configured question by meaning]
  Q -- none or unavailable --> R[Unanswered for review]
  R --> A[Associate with a question or create one with sources]
  Q -- match --> S[Intersect question sources with authorized sources]
  S --> F[Read fresh evidence for actual requested dates]
  F --> V[Retrieve good and bad evaluations for this question]
  V --> G[Generate response]
  G --> C{Evidence, links, sensitive data and rejected-repeat checks}
  C -- fail --> R
  C -- pass --> D{Observation mode?}
  D -- yes --> P[Proposal]
  D -- no --> T[Re-read message and send once]
```

Details that matter when changing it:

1. **Eligibility** (`teams::IncomingMessage::eligible_in`): a user message, not deleted, not own (except a validated personal chat after `enabled_at`), ≤16,000 bytes, within `max_message_age_seconds`, the global `allowed_senders`, and in groups only with a real mention by Graph ID (never by `@name` text).
2. **Question recognition.** Loaded maps initialize the configurable Questions registry (§6.13). Exact owner-associated requests match directly; other wording is confirmed semantically with closed question IDs and a confidence threshold. No match, unavailable recognition or unavailable sources leaves the message for review. Legacy built-in case routing is not used by loaded profiles; only direct library maps with `questions: None` retain it for compatibility.
3. **Context and follow-ups.** `MessageAdapter::history` reads the `HISTORY_MESSAGES` (10) earlier messages of the same conversation (Graph: recent page of `chats/{id}/messages` by `createdDateTime desc`; excludes deleted messages, system events and those after the current one), once per message. Each carries its author (`me`, `assistant` if it is a recorded output of the app, or the display name) and local date/time; it is redacted, bounded to 6000 characters (the most recent are kept) and ends with the time of the current request. It goes as `conversation_history` to `classify_intent`, `standalone_request` and `generate_response`: it helps interpret the request, never as evidence. If Graph fails, it continues without history (`conversation_history_unavailable`). Besides, `conversation_context` keeps the last exchange per conversation (expires after 30 min): the resolved question and the answer **without** its links (copied URLs would fail verification). «dame más detalles», "tell me more" and equivalents reuse that question as the tool query. With another message, some authorized tool and either context (history or last exchange) or, without context, an authorized Wiki source and a request that is not a status question, `LlmProvider::standalone_request` rewrites the request as a standalone one (`question`) and extracts the topic to search (`topic`), e.g. «¿cómo se invoca si quiero pagar 2 servicios?» → topic «Crear ODC». `question` decides the tool, picks passages and is saved as the context question; `topic` is the Wiki Search query. Both are validated (length, no control characters, `Redactor::clean`); if the provider fails or does not apply, the literal request is used. Without context the call only extracts the topic: searching the whole first request («¿qué parámetros recibe en el body el endpoint crear usuario natural?») ranked generic API pages above the service's own page. It only shapes the query inside sources already authorized by code. Earlier context goes as reference, never as evidence; the current request wins. Activity reviews skip the rewrite.
4. **Sources.** `KnowledgeMap::available` requires `enabled`, `external_processing`, an exact conversation or `*`, and the source's `allowed_senders`. A matched question reads all of its selected sources that pass those checks; local simulation selections are intersected too. A local simulation without `sources` (`simulation::sources`) offers every enabled source with external processing when the map has questions, so the identified question reads its own, and otherwise the conversation's authorization (`*`); with nothing readable it is `invalid_input` (`simulation::InvalidRequest`) naming what is missing. No source is added by an old case route. Repository and notes sources use the incremental SQLite FTS5/optional-vector index, returning relevant sections. File/URL sources read configured locations; `urls` groups 1–10 exact public URLs. A Wiki tool with nonempty `page_urls` reads only those pages through its authorized catalog and credential, with canonical references and authorship, without Search. Other tools retain their native read methods. The activity record reads the requested days, defaulting to the incoming message's day in the configured time zone. See [Questions](questions.md) for scope, migration and review details.
5. **Evidence.** Redaction before trimming. For normal answers every source is read first and `max_context_chars` is then shared by need (`evidence::shares`): a source whose evidence is shorter than an even share keeps all of it and the rest share what remains (an even split gave the Wiki half the budget while the other source was a 1.3 KB page). The Wiki and Azure DevOps return typed JSON (`ado::wiki::WikiResult`, `evidence::Evidence`) with a reference registry (ID, verified URL, authorship) and Teams messages with their author; the rest is trimmed with `knowledge::excerpt`. `excerpt` returns a text that fits whole; otherwise it splits Markdown into sections at headings (a too-long section into paragraphs), never inside a fenced code block, weighs each question word by how few parts contain it (the page's own topic barely counts) plus a bonus when the part's heading or a parent heading has it, always keeps the most relevant part (cut if it alone exceeds the budget), adds the next ones whole while they fit, gives the room left to the best one that did not fit, cut, and keeps document order, so a parameter table or a curl example is not displaced by prose that repeats the page title. Inside the Wiki evidence the budget is also shared by need: a page found identical in several wikis is written once (the copies add only their reference), and a repository file whose lines are at least 80% in a page (a README copied into the Wiki) adds only its other lines. The status tool cuts each project at 5,000 characters with work items and run results first and commit details last. A work item the request names (`ado::progress::requested`: «HU #41553», «bug 4512», «AB#77», a bare «#41553», not one after «run», «build», «PR», «release», «pipeline», «commit» or «stage»; at most five) is read first and directly in the catalog's projects, whoever holds it now, and its block leads the evidence. It is described only if the user took part in it (assigned now or at some point in its update history, created or changed it, a child item assigned to, created or changed by them, or one of its recent comments); otherwise the block only names it with its current assignee and says it is not the user's work. A described item gives type, title and state, since when (`StateChangeDate`), the current assignee and the last change, and, from the last five weeks only, its state and assignment changes, the child items changed (all children still count by state), the commits and pull requests linked to it or its children with the latest link date and the newest comments as plain text; plus the dates on the item (non-system dates such as target, due or a custom commitment date, custom fields named through `_apis/wit/fields`, at most eight lookups) and its parent. A named ID outside the scope is reported as not found («do not write its number»). A pull request the request names (`ado::progress::requested_pull_requests`: «PR !7348», «PR #7348», «PR 7348», «pull request 7348», a bare «!7348»; at most five) is read by ID at the organization (`_apis/git/pullrequests/{id}`) and kept only in a project the **configured** catalog allows (`*`: any project the credential reads, not just the user's team projects, because a review request often concerns another team's repository): title, repository, project, state, who created it and when, closing date and branches, with a verified `pull_request` reference (aliases «PR #N», «PR !N», «PR N», «pull request N», «!N»), so the answer can link it; a 404 or a project outside the catalog is reported as not found. The user's work items also include those ever assigned to them and now held by someone else (`EVER [System.AssignedTo]`, changed in the window; a failed read loses only them), marked «previously assigned to the user, now assigned to X», with the state change date and the item's dates; the window keeps ten items.
6. **Legacy activity-review helper.** The specialized multi-tool review remains for direct library maps without Questions; migrated application profiles use only their question's selected source reads.
7. **Generation.** `LlmProvider::generate_response` returns JSON `{answer, detailed}`; the model chooses the mode. The prompt sets the voice: the user replying to a colleague, natural, warm and professional — never robotic, vulgar, slangy or overly casual —, with varied wording and without reusing earlier replies, without the app's internals (evidence, context, references, tools, searches, verification) or stock disclaimers, and without menus of options or generic offers of help. A missing fact is mentioned briefly, in plain words, only when the request needs it. A status round («avance, compromiso, riesgos o bloqueos») is answered part by part in the order asked: progress of a named work item from its state (still in development: child items, linked code and comments with dates; past development: since when and who holds it now, never «I don't have its progress»), where a work item previously assigned to the user is their work handed over; the commitment from the user's dated work items within two weeks and their production deployment pages with their date (or the nearest dated one when none falls within two weeks); and blockers from the pending items in those pages or comments (missing configuration, accesses, definitions), naming who was asked when the evidence says so. When the user has an answer written from the app (§6.12), `owner_guidance` and `previous_draft` (if any) reach the model: the user's statements about their work count as true and override the draft and the evidence, identifiers they give may be used, and nothing else may be invented; a message left unanswered is written as the user's reply (`unprompted`), not as an answer to a question. `answer` is bounded Markdown, without links, citations or a sources section (code links what the text names, §8): a work item, pull request, run or release it mentions goes with its identifier (#123, PR !45, build #678), a stage with the word «stage», and a Wiki page it relies on is named by its title so it can be linked. A direct first sentence, then formatting only where it makes the answer clearer (concise, never decorative): **bold**, *italics*, `-`/`1.` lists and `- [x]`/`- [ ]` checklists, pipe tables for items that share attributes, `code`, ```json blocks, `>` for one key warning, `###` headings only in long answers, ~~strikethrough~~ for something obsolete and `{green:…}`/`{orange:…}`/`{red:…}`/`{blue:…}`/`{gray:…}` to color a status in a word or two. A URL or endpoint is written only if it appears literally in the evidence (e.g. an environment address documented in the Wiki); otherwise the model says so and uses placeholders in examples (`<BASE_URL>`) so it does not trip the URL check or the `Redactor`. No character limit: only that the HTML fits in a Teams message (27,800 bytes). No autonomous tools.

   **Providers and fallback** (`src/llm.rs`). Prompts and contracts live in `Model` and are the same for every provider; each transport implements `Backend::complete_json(system, user, schema)`. `llm::from_config` builds a `Chain` with the active providers of `llm.chain` in order. **Every call** (`classify_intent`, `standalone_request`, `select_tool`, `generate_response`, `select_references`, `propose_registration`, `plan_links`) starts with the first and moves to the next only if it fails; the next call starts with the first again, so the default resumes as soon as it gets its quota back. Failures carry only a class (`usage_limit`, `not_installed`, `invalid_answer` — the reply broke the JSON contract —, `failed`; `llm::Unavailable`), never provider text, and are logged as `llm_provider_unavailable`/`llm_fallback_used`. `audit.provider` stores `provider:model:effort` of the one that wrote. Falling back is safe because it only repeats a model call; no Graph send is ever retried.

   - **DeepSeek** (`llm/deepseek.rs`): direct HTTP to `/chat/completions` (no SDK), JSON mode, the configured `reasoning_effort` (`none` turns `thinking` off), no `max_tokens` or temperature and **no deadline** (only the connection, 30 s, and TCP keepalive are bounded). 402/429 = `usage_limit`. Only `content` is used; `reasoning_content` is discarded and never logged.
   - **Codex and Claude Code** (`llm/agent.rs`): the installed CLI with **its own login** (the app stores no OpenAI/Anthropic credentials). Found in `PATH` or the usual locations (`~/.local/bin`, Homebrew…), because a GUI opened from Finder has a minimal PATH; they are looked up on every call, so installing a CLI needs no restart. Each call runs in an empty private temporary directory, with a minimal environment (`HOME`, `USER`, locale, proxy, `CODEX_HOME`/`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; **never** the host's `NAME`/`NAME_FILE`), the request and evidence on **stdin** (not argv) and the contract's JSON schema. Codex: `codex exec --ephemeral --ignore-user-config --ignore-rules --sandbox read-only --json --output-schema`, instructions as `developer_instructions` and `-c features.X=false` for shell, exec/code, apps, plugins, browser, computer use, sub-agents, skills, memories, hooks and web search (the `-c` form tolerates versions that do not know a feature); the last `agent_message` of a `turn.completed` turn is used. Claude: `claude -p --output-format json --tools "" --safe-mode --strict-mcp-config --no-session-persistence --system-prompt --json-schema`; `structured_output` is used, and `is_error` with HTTP 402/429 or credit/limit text = `usage_limit`. 20-minute deadline per call (the CLIs retry on their own); when it expires the process is killed and the next provider answers. These answer transports always stay tool-less and read-only; a delegated implementation is a separate transport (`llm/implementation.rs`, §16) that launches the same CLIs in their no-prompt modes.
   - **Catalog** (`llm::catalog`, IPC `llm_providers`, `pta llm providers`): detected CLIs and their version; Codex models from `codex debug models` (visible, without the `ultra` effort, which delegates to sub-agents); OpenCode 2 metadata from `opencode api --standalone model.list`, filtered by `favorite` in the TUI’s `model.json` (state directory from `opencode debug paths state`); Claude and DeepSeek from a fixed list. OpenCode efforts are `default` plus the selected model’s reported variant IDs; safe variant syntax is validated in code and OpenCode resolves support at execution. Other efforts are validated in `llm::efforts`; model IDs with a closed alphabet and no leading dash because they reach argv.

   **Holding notice** (`Pipeline::awaiting_model`): if the model is still working 5 minutes after processing of a Teams message started, the fixed text `holding_reply` («Déjame revisarlo.» or «Let me look into it.», per `llm.language`) is sent once and the answer is still awaited. `audit.holding_reply` records `sending` before the POST and then `sent` or `uncertain`; a retried job with that field never sends it again, and a failure is not retried. It does not apply to `dry_run` or simulations (`pta chat`), nor to intent classification (the message may not need an answer). In the personal chat it carries the same output marker, so it is not processed as a question.
8. **References and inline links.** There is no sources section: references are linked on the very words of the answer that name them, and the full list stays in the audit (`references`, `used_sources`), shown in the Messages tab as «Cited references» and «Wiki pages read, not cited». Code first finds the entities it sees named in the text (`evidence::named_references`: alias or `#id`, matched as whole words so «Notificador» does not name the entity inside «notificadorimport», and Wiki pages by a distinctive title). It links a reference by itself (`evidence::code_linked`) only on a **distinctive** name — one with a digit or a symbol (`#41477`, `PR !7348`, `contoso.pagos.ms.solicitud`) or of several words — or on a single word right after a word saying what it is («stage QA», «la página Runbook», «commit cccccccc»), outside code, existing links and bare URLs, joined with names of the same reference that follow, so «#41477 — PR Pipeline Template» becomes one link. A single common word is not linked by code: «Área Desarrollo» is a project, not the stage «Desarrollo». `LlmProvider::select_references` then gets every reference code did not see named or cannot link and returns, per used one, the exact words of the answer that name it (`{"used":[{"key":"r1","mention":"PR !7348"}]}`, `""` when the text uses it without naming it). The model sees short closed keys (`r1`, `r2`…) with kind, label, project and authority — never IDs or URLs — and any key outside the list is an `invalid_answer` (the next provider answers); words not found in the answer only leave that reference unlinked. `audit.reference_selection` is `llm`, or `code` when no model could choose (or nothing was left to choose); when no model could choose and no Wiki page was named, code counts every Wiki page read as used. `evidence::complete_answer` verifies every used reference, then links code's spans first and the model's words next, once per entity (the same entity captured twice is linked once); a Wiki page used but not named goes into one closing sentence in the language of `llm.language` («Más detalle en [A](url) y [B](url).» / «More detail in …»), so a Wiki-based answer always links its pages, and a third-party page with a known last editor carries «(última edición de Ana)» / «(last edited by Ana)» right after its link unless the text already names that person; own pages and unknown authorship add nothing (nothing is invented). URLs in the body must be verified references or appear literally in the evidence. Code still withholds the answer for: an invented ID, a concrete entity without a verified reference or a new URL (`invalid_references: …`), and sensitive data or size (`unsafe_proposal`/`unsafe_answer`). The withheld proposal stays in `audit.proposed`. `teams_message` references accept only `https://teams.microsoft.com/l/message/…` links; every other reference must be a `dev.azure.com` URL inside its organization and project. Answers recorded before 0.7.15 end with a «**Fuentes**»/«**Páginas consultadas**» section; the context saved from one sent by hand drops it.

   **Withheld answer** (`Pipeline::withhold`): in the personal chat, outside `dry_run` and simulations, a fixed text in the language of `llm.language` («No envié la respuesta a tu mensaje: <cause>…» / «I didn't send the reply to your message: …») is sent once, without the withheld content. `audit.withheld_notice` records `sending` before the POST and then `sent`/`uncertain`; a retry never repeats it. In chats with other people there is no notice.

   **The record of each message.** `Audit` also keeps the user's context for an answer written at their request (`refinement`, §6.12; omitted without `content`) and the sources they chose for it (`chosen_sources`, omitted when empty), the redacted text of the eligible message (`question`; never for ineligible ones), the interpreted request and the topic searched, the intent, the conversation type, the message time, the number of context messages, the provider and those that failed first (`provider_fallbacks`), the links of a confirmed plan with their result (`links`), and `trace`: timed steps with an explanation that never includes message content (step names in English; audits before 0.6.1 have Spanish ones). `Store::inspect` returns `created_at` and, without `content`, omits `question`, `resolved_question`, `topic`, `proposed` and `sent`. `final_check` exists only in audits written before 0.6.1.
9. **Send.** `teams::html` turns the Markdown into Teams HTML: escaped text; `<b>`, `<i>`, `<s>`, `<code>`; only `https` links as `<a>`; fences as `<codeblock>`; `#` headings as `<h2>`/`<h3>` (never `<h1>`); lists, with checklists as ☐/☑; one level of `<blockquote>`; `<hr>`; pipe tables as `<table>` with a `<thead>`; colors only from a fixed palette of five names, as `<span style="color:#…">`. Anything else (raw HTML, other color names, images) stays text. The URL check ignores a `|`, `}` or `~` right after a URL; every chat is sent with `contentType: html` and the personal chat adds its output marker. With `[message_style]` on, `adapters/card.rs` sends the same Markdown as an Adaptive Card attachment instead (the body holds only `<attachment id>` and the output marker), because Teams drops styles from chat HTML: a stripe 2 to 16 pixels wide in any `#RRGGBB` color (a one-pixel-tall PNG data URI, that many colored pixels then as many transparent ones, repeated down the card's left edge and painted again on the band), and a band with a label (default «Respuesta generada con IA» / "AI-generated response" by `llm.language`), above or below the text, whose background and text colors are Adaptive Card container and text styles, so Teams shades them for its light or dark theme. Paragraph and list lines become `TextBlock`s (bold, italic and checked `https` links stay Markdown; any other `](` is broken with a zero-width space so the card never makes a link the pipeline did not check), fences a Teams `CodeBlock` with a monospace fallback, quotes an emphasis container, tables a `Table`. `text_size` sets every text block and run of the message (headings one step larger; Teams' `CodeBlock` has its own size), `label_size` the band's text and icon. No actions, inputs or remote images. A card that would not fit Graph's limit together with its body is sent as plain HTML. The holding and withheld notices and `teams_post` messages use the same style, except a `teams_post` with mentions, which stays HTML because Graph `mentions` refer to `<at>` tags in the body. Reading Teams back (`card::message_text`: history, own-message activity) includes a card's text without its band. `valid_answer` also requires the HTML to fit Graph's limit (27,800 bytes) before `sending`. Re-read the message: same text and still eligible, with the age measured when processing **started** (`max_message_age_seconds` + processing time; no limit once the holding notice went out); persist `sending` with `synchronous=FULL` before the POST; never retry a send. A failure or restart during the send = `uncertain` (manual review).

10. **Retired Azure writes.** The installed `Tools` adapter reports `links_work=false` and implements no Azure write methods. Reviews do not propose registration or ask to create tasks. A persisted personal-chat plan from an older host cannot write: its source is no longer writable, and the result is reported without a request. Legacy types, audit records and generic test adapters remain readable for compatibility; stored write credentials are never removed or repurposed.

11. **Local/scheduled daily task record** (`app::activity`, `ado::tasks`, `activity::store`). The existing schedule keeps daily/interval slots, workdays and local/IANA time zones. Slots are claimed durably once; no historical catch-up. Selected enabled Azure sources use only their existing read credential; an empty selection means every enabled Azure activity source. Legacy Wiki/Teams selections and write settings are ignored. No model, commits, PRs, pipelines, Wiki pages, Teams messages or call discovery is involved.
    - `list_azure_tasks` accepts an exact `day`, optional `user` (catalog user by default), `source` and `project`. Project/team/catalog boundaries remain enforced. It discovers the project's task and bug categories (`Microsoft.TaskCategory` and `Microsoft.BugCategory`, so bugs with actual hours count like tasks; a process without a bug category answers 404 and only tasks are read), queries candidate IDs with historical assignment (`EVER`), and reads up to 1,000 revisions per task. A work item belongs to the day only when its actual hours grow while assigned to the user inside the configured day: the hours are the daily net delta of the type's effort field (`effort_fields` by type, else `effort_field`), never capped to `daily_hours`, and a close or any other change without hours records no row. A bug-category item with a child of a task-category type (by its links at the day's end or now) is not recorded: its hours are its tasks', which show it as their parent, so the day never counts them twice. Later edits/reassignments do not change the day or owner. The last pre-end revision supplies title, state and description. Candidate limit: 200 per project; overall Azure deadline: 110 seconds. Bounds/errors fail the snapshot and retain the old day.
    - A successful run replaces one row in the additive `task_days(day,payload)` table of the existing `activity-registration.db`. It saves the day/time zone, verified task and optional parent URLs, titles, descriptions, daily hours, advisory target and timestamp. Task URLs deduplicate overlapping sources. Empty days get rows too; failures leave previous records untouched. A failed run's `diagnostics` keep the generic `error` and, in `detail`, the real cause (`SOURCE: message` without the `[code]` prefix, or the store/binding error), which Activities shows below the error as «Cause»; the read's messages carry no credential values. Legacy confirmed tasks remain in the daily history unless a new snapshot supersedes their day; original rows and uncertain attempts remain intact.
    - `activity_history` returns `days` (newest first, counts/hours), `record` (selected day with tasks), `running`, runs and settings. `activity_run` is a local operation. `activity_pending` is empty and `activity_resolve` rejects writes; non-empty legacy run context is rejected. The CLI, MCP and GUI use the same operations.
    - `Database::recorded` reads the new daily rows as verified evidence through the existing `activity_record` source, falling back to legacy entries on days without snapshots. Tasks without HUs still carry verified task links; a saved empty day explicitly says no tasks were recorded. Recaps and task reports use this saved record, without live Azure activity reads. Source audiences/external processing remain enforced when answering; no schedule grants new Teams audiences. The record is local knowledge and needs no FTS re-index.

12. **Answering a message by hand** (`Pipeline::begin_refinement`, `Pipeline::refine`, `Pipeline::send_draft`; IPC `answer_refine`/`answer_send`, `pta answer refine|send`, the Messages tab). Any message addressed to the assistant that was not sent an answer qualifies (`unsent`): a job `ignored` or `dry_run` with a recorded `question` and no `sent`, outside simulations, except `sensitive_question` (its text never reaches a model). That covers messages the assistant left unanswered (`informational_message`, `personal_request`, `no_authorized_resource`) and answers it wrote but did not send (withheld, observed or written earlier at the user's request, `unsent_draft`). The assistant must be running (`not_ready` otherwise), because it reads and writes Teams with the service's session.
    - **Generate or regenerate.** `begin_refinement` validates the user's context (plain text, at most 4000 characters, `Redactor::clean`; `invalid_input` otherwise) and the chosen sources (each one must exist, be enabled and allow external processing; `invalid_input` otherwise), records them as `audit.refinement` and `audit.chosen_sources` and sets the job to `refining` under the operations lock, so a second request is a `state_conflict`; the IPC returns at once and `refine` runs in a host task. It runs the normal path in draft mode: the message is read again without the age limit, there is no link conversation or intent decision (the earlier intent is kept, so a review stays a review and a message left unanswered stays `statement` or `personal`; audits before 0.7.18 take it from their reason), no holding notice and no registration proposal; the sources are read again and `generate_response` receives `owner_guidance` and `previous_draft` (empty for a first answer). A message left unanswered is generated with `GenerationInput::unprompted`: the prompt asks for the user's reply to a message that asks no question (a brief acknowledgment and what it implies), not for an answer to a question, and an empty evidence does not ask the model to explain a limitation. Chosen sources replace the conversation's audiences for this answer only, like a simulation (enabled with external processing), and every chosen source is read, without tool routing, recap or status extras: the user reviews and sends the result. Without a choice, the conversation's authorized sources and the normal routing apply. Every check still applies: a failing answer is stored with its withheld reason, a passing one as `ignored`/`draft_ready`. Nothing is sent and the withheld-answer notice is never repeated. While it works the job is `refining`, never `processing` (which a restart would queue as a new message): `Store::open` turns a leftover `refining` into `ignored`/`refinement_failed`. A run that stops before writing restores the earlier audit with the reason it recorded (`no_authorized_resource`, `sensitive_question`, or `message_changed` when the message was deleted or stopped being eligible); any other failure restores it with `refinement_failed`. The earlier proposal stays until a new one is written.
    - **Send.** `send_draft` requires a proposal, re-reads the message and requires its redacted text to equal the recorded `question` (`state_conflict` otherwise), checks `valid_answer` (sensitive data, redaction markers, Teams' size), records `sending`, POSTs once and records `sent` (reason `manual_send`, saving the conversation context without its links) or `uncertain`. It is the user's own decision on a held answer, not a retry: like any send it happens once, and a sent, sending or uncertain job cannot be sent or written again.

12a. **Posting a new message** (`Graph::chat_directory`, `Graph::post_chat`; IPC `teams_chats`/`teams_post`, `pta teams chats|send`, MCP `teams_chats`/`teams_post`). The user's own message in a chat they belong to, without the assistant's reception allowlists, because the user chooses the destination and confirms the exact text and images (MCP elicitation or `confirmed`). Only chats (`ChatMessage.Send`, already consented; no channels, no new scopes). `post_chat` checks the account, requires the user among `chats/{id}/members`, renders Markdown with `teams::html` (or as the `[message_style]` card when it is on and nothing is mentioned and no image goes with it), resolves `@[Name]` against the members only (unknown or ambiguous refuses everything) into Graph `mentions`, appends the images, adds the output nonce marker in the personal chat, POSTs once (`retry_safe: false`; a client error is `PostRefusal::Refused`, whose message names the HTTP status and Graph's error codes, `error.code` and the inner error's, kept only when they are identifiers, never Graph's message) and records only `teams_post` events (`sent`/`refused`/`uncertain`, with ` images=N bytes=B` when there are images; never their content or names).
    **Images** (`adapters/images.rs`, since 0.12.18). Up to 4 PNG, JPEG or GIF images (`MAX_IMAGES`), 2.9 MB together (`MAX_TOTAL`: Graph caps a write request at 4 MB and base64 adds a third; each hosted content may be up to 4 MB), travel inside the message as Graph hosted contents: each is `<p><img src="../hostedContents/N/$value" alt width height></p>` after the text and a `hostedContents` entry `{"@microsoft.graph.temporaryId": "N", contentBytes, contentType}`; no external host, no link. The type and size come only from the bytes' header (`images::detect`; WebP, SVG and anything else are refused), never from a name or a declared type; the alt text (at most 200 characters, no control characters) is escaped like the rest of the text. Images do not count against the 27 800 characters of the HTML body. A message with images is sent without the style card (the card never carries an image, remote or embedded), like one with mentions, and the result says so in `style`; the text may then be empty. The process that receives the call reads a path: `pta mcp` (`tools::prepare`, before the confirmation) or `pta teams send --image PATH [--alt TEXT]`; the host only receives `{name, alt, data}` and checks everything again (`images::from_wire`), so it never opens a path an IPC argument names. The confirmation shows each image's name, type, dimensions, size and the first 8 hexadecimal digits of its SHA-256, and the bytes read for it are the ones sent (nothing is read again after the approval). A `pta` with images refuses a host without `teams_images_support` in its `Endpoint`, which would otherwise drop them and post the text alone. Uses the service's session (`activity_graph`) or the profile's when stopped, so posting and the reads below work on any computer of the account that signed in to Microsoft; the account's Teams machine only decides which one receives and answers on its own. Not in the web-profile method allowlist.

13. **Questions** (`questions::Question`, `map.questions`, `pta questions`, Knowledge › Questions). Questions replace configurable Cases. The initial daily-activities question selects the activity record; equivalent requests keep their own date parameters. Unknown messages are reviewed through `question_associate`, which saves an owner-associated request or creates a question with source IDs. Legacy case fields are retained and ignored by loaded profiles. [Questions and answer evaluations](questions.md) documents recognition, permissions, storage, review, CLI and tools.

14. **Ratings, evaluations and people.** `answer_feedback` rates sent/proposed Teams or local-test answers good/bad with a comment. Ratings for an identified question automatically update its indexed evaluation in `knowledge-index.db`; later answers retrieve both kinds within the same question and authorized source set. Good evaluations suggest an approach; bad ones tell the model what to avoid, and code blocks identical rejected repeats. Clearing a rating removes its guidance. Facts always come from current evidence; no weights are trained. Legacy approved examples remain readable for direct library maps without Questions. **People** (`[[people]]` in the map: `id`, `name`, `senders` (Graph user IDs), `aliases`, `address`, `notes`) say how the user addresses each person: the sender's profile, by Teams ID or else by name (accent- and case-folded), reaches `generate_response` as `recipient` only when answering that person (never in the personal chat), for the form of address, tone and length, never as evidence (`audit.person`, trace step `person`). Eligible messages record `audit.sender_id` and `audit.sender_name` so the GUI can offer the profile. Messages › an answer has **Your rating** (Good answer / Needs work, comment, «Use it as an example for similar requests» with its context) and **Save as a note** (Knowledge › Notes prefilled, origin `answer`, reference the message; `notes::checked` passes title, text and keywords through the `Redactor` and names the field it refuses, while a reference that is a canonical `chats/…/messages/…` resource, a `simulation:SESSION:UUID` or an HTTPS link without user info skips the digit-run identifier patterns, whose 13-digit message IDs read as phone numbers); a sender links to their profile or adds them to People.

`jobs` states: `pending → processing → ignored | dry_run | failed | sending → sent | uncertain`; a message the user has an answer written for goes `ignored | dry_run → refining → ignored`, and one the user sends `→ sending → sent | uncertain`. Failed reads/providers retry with backoff 2^n s up to 5 attempts. When the database opens, `processing` goes back to `pending`, `sending` becomes `uncertain` and `refining` becomes `ignored` (`refinement_failed`). The audit (`jobs.audit`, redacted JSON) keeps reason, sources, tools, confidences, references and proposal; `pta audit` reads it.

Frequent reasons: `ineligible_message`, `sensitive_question`, `no_authorized_resource`, `informational_message`, `personal_request`, `deterministic_greeting`, `deterministic_thanks`, `unsafe_proposal`, `invalid_references: …`, `supported_answer`, `message_changed`, `send_result_unknown_manual_review`, for linking `links_planned`, `links_applied`, `links_cancelled`, `links_unresolved`, `links_not_configured`, and for unsent answers `refining`, `draft_ready`, `refinement_failed` and `manual_send`. `reference_selection_failed` and `final_gate` appear only in audits from before external reviews stopped withholding answers.


### Task registration reports (0.7.24)

The `report_tasks` case recognizes reminders to log hours and requests for registered tasks before activity-review phrases or model classification. It reads the authorized local activity record only, for the requested dates or today in the registration timezone, and includes saved `recorded` tasks, explicit `recorded_empty` days and legacy confirmed `done` / `already_registered` entries. Pending, uncertain and dismissed entries are excluded. The recap evidence route supplies verified task and parent links without remote tools or document searches; empty records produce an explicit no-confirmed-tasks answer, without falling back to an unregistered-work review. The case has configurable keywords and instructions, a fixed source kind, and appears as «Reportar tareas» in Spanish.

## 7. Microsoft Graph and Entra

- **OAuth** (`adapters/oauth.rs`): public client, system browser, PKCE S256, redirect `http://localhost:<port>/` served by the host during login (600 s). Fixed scopes `offline_access User.Read Chat.Read ChatMessage.Send` (`Config::scopes`). `/me` is checked against `graph.user_id` before storing tokens. Refresh is serialized and each rotated token is persisted encrypted before use.
- **Entra registration:** *Mobile and desktop* platform with `http://localhost`, multi-organization accounts, delegated permissions only. Do not add scopes: it would change the consent already granted.
- **Subscriptions** (`Graph::reconcile_subscriptions`, every 60 s): `discover_all_chats` → one `users/{id}/chats/getAllMessages` resource; otherwise `chats/{id}/messages` per chat in `allowed_chats`; plus the personal chat. 50-minute duration, renewed with <10 min left. Own subscriptions are recovered by exact callback and `applicationId` (Graph hides `clientState`). Lifecycle: `subscriptionRemoved`, `reauthorizationRequired`, `missed` (queues recovery of the recent page).
- **Webhook** (`adapters/webhook.rs`): validates the whole batch before queuing (constant-time clientState, tenant, persisted subscription, allowed collection, canonical path `chats/{id}/messages/{id}`). Never follows URLs from the notification. Answers 202.
- **Personal chat**: `48:notes` (notes) or a oneOnOne whose only member is the user; validated by `validate_self_chat`. Received by webhook + polling of 50 messages every 10 s. Each output carries a `https://personalteams.invalid/output/{nonce}` link registered in `outputs` before sending, to recognize echoes and ambiguous sends after a restart. An output without an ID pauses the personal chat until `pta self-chat reconcile`.
- **Own messages** (`Graph::own_messages`, activity reviews only): `me/chats` with the last message preview, then each readable chat's recent messages; only the user's own, non-deleted, in the window, never the personal chat or app outputs.
- **Chat reads** (`adapters/chats.rs`; IPC `teams_messages`/`teams_search`, `pta teams messages|search`, MCP tools of the same names; `teams_read_support` in `Endpoint`). Operator reads with `Chat.Read`: `Graph::readable_chat` resolves `self` and accepts only canonical IDs that `readable_on_request` allows (this computer's reception plus the account's [`teams_reading`](#teams-reading-on-request)). `chat_messages` reads `chats/{id}/messages` with `$orderby=createdDateTime desc` and, for an end or a `before` cursor, `$filter=createdDateTime lt …` (the only creation filter Graph accepts); the start is checked locally and stops the paging, which follows `@odata.nextLink` only on the same origin and path, at most 20 pages per call. Each `ChatLine` is plain text (`card::message_text`), where each image of the message reads as `[image]` or `[image: alt]` and `images` counts them (emoji drawn as images stay out), the author's display name, `mine`, the app's output marker as `assistant` and `messageReference` attachments as `replies_to`; no address, no store write. A page is cut at its limit on a millisecond boundary (`cut`) so a `before` cursor (the oldest kept instant) misses nothing. `chat_message` reads one message by resource. `own_chat_messages` lists `me/chats` by last message (10 pages), reads up to 40 readable chats (six at a time, 10 pages each) and merges the user's own; `search_messages` posts `search/query` for `chatMessage` (KQL `sent>=`/`sent<=` for the window, eight pages of 25; with no hit it searches the words alone and applies the window itself), keeps hits in readable chats (no channels) matching `chats`, `from` and the window, and reads each kept message; when Search fails with anything but a session renewal it scans the history the same bounded way, matching every folded word, `method: chat_history`. Bounds and failures set `partial` with `warnings`. `teams_chats` sorts `chat_directory` (all pages of `me/chats`) newest first and pages it by `offset`/`limit` with the `total`.
- **Simulations** (`local_chat::Teams`): when a source the simulation may read is the own Teams messages tool, `app::simulation_teams` gives the local chat's Graph the running service's token source, or one access token the profile's session yields for that call (`FixedToken`, the dataset lock held only while obtaining it); otherwise `NoTeamsToken` carries a fixed reason (`NoTeamsSession`), which `ToolError` keeps so the review's trace step and `warnings` name it.

## 8. Knowledge and tools

`KnowledgeMap` = `repositories` (alias → local Git checkout) + `resources` (at most 250) + `people` (at most 500, §6.14) + `questions` (configured questions, §6.13) + `cases` (retained legacy data) + `contexts` (at most 50: `id`, `name`, `description`, `keywords` (≤50), `sources` (existing IDs), `instructions`; omitted when empty) + `work_item_guides` and `agent_prompts` (the implementation agents' guides and instructions, §16; omitted when empty) + `organization` (below; omitted when empty) + `workflows` and `workflow_blocks` (§16 [Workflows](#workflows); omitted when empty, changed only through their operations) + `workflow_settings` (§16 [Workflows](#workflows); omitted while every value is its default, changed only through its operations). Each resource: `id`, `description`, `topics`, `enabled`, `external_processing`, `allowed_conversations`, `allowed_senders` and an access:

| `kind` | Rules |
| --- | --- |
| `file` | Relative path inside a declared checkout, without `..` or escaping symlinks; Markdown/TXT/JSON/TOML/YAML; ≤1 MB. |
| `repository` | A declared checkout, searched through the knowledge index (§6.4); symlinks are never followed. |
| `notes` | The verified notes in the knowledge index; optional `contexts` (existing context IDs) keeps only theirs and the general ones. |
| `organization` | The organization's entries and work item guides in the knowledge index (`@organization`), searched like the notes (audit tool `search_organization`). No parameters. |
| `url` | Exact HTTPS URL, port 443, no credentials; DNS resolved and pinned, private IPs rejected, no redirects. |
| `tool` | `ToolSpec` (`src/tools.rs`), always read-only; a `secret_ref` when it has one must be allowlisted in `[secrets]`. |

| `ToolSpec` (`type`) | Name in the audit | What it does | Timeout |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `azure_devops_status` | `get_azure_devops_status` | Recent activity (7/14 days): work items (including those handed over to someone else), own commits, pipelines/stages, releases, relevant Teams context; a work item the request names is read directly with its owner, history, dates, children, linked code and comments (§6.5). In a review, own work compared with work items (`ado::review`). Catalog (`organization`, `projects`, `author_email`) inline in the map (`catalog`, an array of those entries) or, in older maps, a TOML file in a knowledge repository (`repository` + `path`); exactly one of them (`ToolSpec::catalog`), and the Wiki tool takes the same; `projects = ["*"]` means the projects of the teams the user belongs to (`_apis/teams?$mine=true`, resolved by `Catalog::own` for the status, review and Teams-link reads and by `wiki::Reader::own_scope` for Wiki, Search and repositories), never the whole organization; an organization where the user is in no team is not read; repository index in `activity_cache` (active 5 min, inactive 6 h; review commits 10 min). Legacy optional `link_secret_ref` remains readable but is unused: the installed application never writes to Azure (§6.10). | 180 s (work item read and each link 20 s) |
| `azure_devops_wiki` | `search_azure_devops_wiki` (`read_own_wiki_pages` in a status round) | Native Search + Pages + authorship through Git (`created_by_me`/`edited_by_me`/`other`/`unknown`). `wiki_ids`, `author_mode` (`prefer_mine`, `mine_only`, `all`), `repository_docs` (off by default): also reads the README and one OpenAPI file (`swagger*`/`openapi*` YAML or JSON) at the root of up to two Git repositories of the catalog's projects whose name carries every word of the topic (accents folded, generic words such as «microservicio» or «endpoint» dropped, a word of five or more letters may lose its last one), at the default branch, cited as `repository_file` references (`_git/{repo}?path=…&version=GB…`); a separate 30 s deadline, and a failure only adds a warning. Limits: Search 25 per page/100 candidates, 10 histories, 4 context pages, 1 MB, 8 s per request, 30 s overall (90 s for a review's own edits). The operator's `azure wiki search --limit/--offset` (`wiki::Review`, `Reader::search_page`) reads candidates `offset..offset+limit` (1–25 per call) of the first 200 in Search's order, keeps every page read and returns `next_offset`, within 100 s; answers keep the bounds above. `azure wiki read` returns the page's whole Markdown (Mermaid and code blocks included); the CLI path redacts the result and names in `warnings` (with `partial`) every page whose content redaction changed (`app::redact_wiki`). Needs a PAT with `vso.wiki` (+ `vso.code` for authorship). | 32 s (review 180 s) |
| `teams_messages` | `get_own_teams_messages` | The user's own Teams messages in the window (see §6.6). No parameters. | 60 s (review 180 s) |
| `activity_record` | `read_recorded_activities` | What the Activities section recorded by day (`activity-registration.db`, read-only): saved existing Azure tasks with verified links, optional HU, descriptions and daily hours, plus legacy records (§6.11). Read by the pipeline, locally, without a credential. No parameters. | local |
| `azure_devops` | `get_work_item` | One work item by `id: N`. | 5 s |
| `sql_server` | `get_payment_status` … | Three fixed parameterized queries over `assistant_readonly.*` views; no dynamic SQL; verified TLS. | 5 s |
| `rabbitmq` | `get_queue_status` | Metadata of one queue (`/api/queues/...`). | 5 s |
| `http` | `http_get` | GET to a fixed URL, optional bearer, ≤64 KB. | 5 s |

**Organization** (`organization.rs`, `[organization]` in the map): the conventions of the organization the user works for, as account data that names nothing in code. `name` (display only, one line, ≤120 characters), `entries` (at most 200: `id`, `kind` — `norm`, `architecture`, `convention`, `glossary_term` or `template` —, `title` ≤200, `text` Markdown ≤20000, `projects` with empty = all, and `updated_at`, which `save_settings` sets when an entry's content changes and keeps otherwise) and `sources` (at most 50 existing source IDs of kinds `file`, `url`, `urls`, `repository` or the Wiki tool, labelled as documenting the organization; the label grants nothing). Its work item guides stay in `[[work_item_guides]]` (§16), unchanged, and the Organization view shows them as its `work_item_guide` entries, so no migration exists. `Index::sync_organization` keeps one document per entry and per guide (`guide:ID`) under `@organization`, rewritten when its stamp (the serialized item) changes; the pipeline, `knowledge_search` and the save path synchronize it, so the index follows the map without a refresh. Entries are untrusted data: the `organization` source reads them for answers only where it is enabled and authorized, and the implementation agents receive them as §16 says.
