Answer rules (also in `AGENTS.md`): the user's own Wiki documentation can back an answer with authority; third-party documentation says where it is and its verified author (or unknown authorship); every Wiki answer links its pages; concrete work items, pull requests, commits, pipelines, stages and releases carry their verified link (run ≠ configuration); with Teams messages, name the relevant interlocutors keeping who said what; in an activity review, verified authorship proves the user's own action.

## 9. Cross-cutting security

- Transports are authenticated, never declared: the control endpoint's local, browser, machine and agent tokens (`authority::Transport`), the owner's local page's in-process mark (`Window`) and the portal's edge assertion decide authority; arguments never do. The remote MCP endpoint's agent transport is narrower than the browser's: it can never reveal or type a value, approve, define or run launchers, stage or apply updates, hold keys or administer the portal, and it refuses browser requests ([Remote MCP endpoint](#remote-mcp-endpoint-01064)).
- No model withholds answers or grants permissions: the model chain classifies the intent of ambiguous messages (a step that can leave a message unanswered), decides whether a question about the user's work is an activity review, rewrites follow-ups, chooses a tool among authorized ones and picks references among verified ones. Audiences, paths, URLs, tools and limits are checked in code; sensitive data, IDs, unverified URLs and size are enforced by code (`Redactor`, `complete_answer`, `valid_answer`).
- Questions, documents, results and conversation history reach the models as untrusted data.
- Images posted to Teams (§6, 12a) are checked by their bytes, count and size in code, on both sides of the IPC; the host never opens a path from an argument, and the person confirms each image by name, type, size and hash before the one send. Their content is never logged or stored.
- Azure task reads and daily snapshots never create or change Azure work items. Legacy write credentials/attempts are preserved; old plans cannot write (§6.10–11). Model decisions grant no permissions.
- The model CLIs run without tools (no shell, file reading, web or sub-agents), without user configuration, in an empty directory and without the host's credentials; OpenCode 2 uses a generated `pta` agent with denied permissions and policies for all tools, plugins, MCP and skills, an isolated config directory, disabled project/Claude discovery and a private server. It keeps its provider login database and deletes only its fresh session after the call; cancellation schedules the same cleanup. Their output goes through the same checks (`Redactor`, references, limits) as DeepSeek's.
- `security::Redactor`: loaded secrets (exact match), tokens/JWT/keys, credentials in URLs, emails, phone numbers, RUT, `secret://` and `sensitive_patterns`. Applied to the question, evidence, proposal and audit; a proposal with sensitive patterns is blocked.
- Logs: only fixed own events (`tracing`, filter `personal_teams_assistant=info`); prompts, HTTP bodies and provider errors are never logged. Errors to GUI/CLI become sanitized messages (`app.rs::fail`, `cli.rs::run`); only fixed texts typed as `app::Rejected` (activity input or state the user can fix: `invalid_input`/`state_conflict`) pass through as they are. Entra token HTTP 400/401 with fixed OAuth `invalid_grant` or `interaction_required` codes becomes `AuthorizationRequired`, reported as `not_ready` with a Microsoft reconnection instruction through native GUI, CLI and web. Provider descriptions are never returned; failed renewal preserves the saved encrypted session. Stored Microsoft linkage alone does not prove live authorization.
- HTTP clients without redirects; bounded response sizes (`adapters::bounded_json`). The one exception is the release download of an archive installation's update (§3), which follows at most five redirects and only to the release host's own asset hosts, over HTTPS, bounded in size and time, and is checked against the release's published SHA-256 before anything is unpacked.
- The GUI's documents, on the local page and in the portal, carry the same strict CSP (`default-src 'self'`, no inline script, `connect-src 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`); the GUI reaches the host only through its origin's `/api/control`.
- The owner's local page (§3) is loopback-only, checks `Host`, `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-Site`, signs in only with a single-use code of at most a minute that the host writes into an owner-only handoff file and gives the system browser as a `file://` address, and keeps its session in an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict browser-session cookie plus a key that only this origin's script holds, with the portal's anti-CSRF header. Its calls carry the owner's transport (`Transport::Window`); the control token never yields a code, key or session. What others on the same computer can do: **another OS user** can connect to the loopback port but cannot read the handoff file, the profile or process arguments carrying the code, so it never obtains a code, cookie or key; **another server on `127.0.0.1`** (any port, e.g. a development server a coding agent started and the user opened) receives the page's cookie, because cookies ignore ports, but not the key, which travels only in a fragment and lives in this origin's `sessionStorage`, so its requests with the cookie alone, even with a forged `Origin`, get 401. A process running as the same OS user can read the profile and the handoff file, like every other credential of the profile (the boundary of [Account variables](#account-variables)).
- Git: managed clones with askpass (`PERSONAL_TEAMS_GIT_ASKPASS`), no token in URL/argv; `sync` requires a clean checkout and a fast-forward.

## 10. CLI: contract 1

**Shared tools and To-Do.** `app/tools/mod.rs` owns the tool catalog, closed argument schemas,
effect classifications, confirmation texts and adapters formerly defined inside `app/mcp.rs`.
`pta tools list` returns this catalog without a host; `pta tools call NAME` takes a JSON object
on stdin and reuses its adapters. Existing operations for messages, answers, sources, contexts,
questions, people, notes, indexing, activities and assistant control remain available through their
dedicated CLI commands and GUI host methods. External tool calls require `confirmed:true`;
MCP still asks through client elicitation when available. The tool catalog is project structure,
not another GUI section. CLI capabilities advertise `tools_support` and `todo_support`.

**Start at login.** `pta service status`, `pta service install --confirmed` and `pta service uninstall --confirmed` map to the host methods `service_status`, `service_install` and `service_uninstall` (no arguments; MCP tools of the same names, install and uninstall `External`). Replies carry `supported`, `manager` (`launchd`, `systemd`, `task_scheduler`), `name`, `installed`, `current`, `running`, `last_error`, `linger`, `this_host`, `notes` and, after a change, `changed`; never a path. Refusals: `authority_denied` (6) off the owner's own computer, `state_conflict` (6) for an entry pta did not create or one running another executable, `not_ready` (3) without a systemd user manager, `operation_failed` (1) when the manager refuses. `capabilities` advertises `service_support`. See [start at login](#start-at-login).

`list_azure_hus` and `list_github_epics` are read-only host methods shared by To-Do, CLI tools
and MCP. They need no running Teams service or model and never change source audiences.
`tools/todo.rs` defines `Filters` (optional source/project/state, kind (hu, bug or task) or one exact
`type`, limit 1–100, default 10), `List` (items, all states, every Azure type with its group, projects, assignment fields, partial coverage/warnings and has_more),
and item dates retaining their field reference, display name and nullable value.
`ado/todo.rs` reads an enabled Azure activity source's catalog, resolves `*` to the user's team
projects and discovers requirement-category HU and bug-category types, plus the task-category
types whose work item guide sets `listed_with_bugs` (§16), workflow states and developer identity fields. Each item carries its type's `category` (`requirement`, `bug` or `task`); the GUI decides task detail and implementation by it, never by type name. Assignment is checked separately per type: all its developer roles are ORed, with
Assigned To used only when that type has no developer role. The default includes both categories;
`kind` narrows to a group (`task`: the task-category types not listed with the bugs) and `type` to one
discovered type before querying and limiting. Every type of the three categories is returned in `types`
whatever the filter, so the GUI's type selector offers them.
WIQL filters assignment/type/project/state and orders creation descending; the item read requests
only displayed/verification fields, and verifies assignment and scope again. Every start/finish,
target and closure date field stays distinct, including development dates. There is no recent
activity window. The process metadata cache (`todo-cache.db`, same Store cache mechanism) lasts
five minutes and includes the read credential fingerprint in its key; items are read fresh.
Unread projects produce partial coverage rather than a complete-looking empty list.

`app/github.rs::epics` uses the existing GitHub connection, searches issues of type Epic assigned
to its authenticated user and verifies type, assignee and canonical issue URLs. Filters accept
open/closed and optionally OWNER/REPO. GitHub supplies creation/closure; an unavailable start is
null. `open_work_item` validates canonical HTTPS Azure/GitHub item links before calling the
host's shell. The portal opens its returned item links with target=_blank.

`get_azure_hu` and `get_github_epic` are read-only detail operations in the same catalog,
CLI/MCP adapters, dispatcher read lists and portal allowlist. `DetailRequest` requires a positive
ID; GitHub also requires OWNER/REPO. Azure verifies the response ID, configured project and
process requirement/bug category without restricting current assignment; duplicate IDs across
catalog organizations fail as ambiguous. It reads every returned standard/custom field with
organization field metadata, comments (7.1-preview.4, at most 500 latest), updates (first 200)
and original relations. It resolves at most 200 related work item summaries in scope, with
the shared `Item` shape and a process-verified `task` flag on each resolved relation, and turns
supported vstfs Git branch/PR/commit/build artifacts into pinned Azure web links. GitHub verifies
issue type, number and canonical repository URL, then reads fields, comments/sub-issues (500 each)
and events (200) through constructed API URLs, never response URLs with the credential. Missing
sections and bounds set `partial` with explicit warnings. All clients have timeouts, bounded
responses and no redirects; these tools make no external writes and change no audiences.

`pta [--json] [--non-interactive] COMMAND`. `pta`, `pta help`, `--help` or `-h` show the help in English with every command described (a test requires it to document every accepted command). `--json` prints `{contract, version, ok, code, exit_code, message, data, revision}`; without `--json`, `status`, `start`, `stop`, `restart` and `app quit` print a readable summary and the rest print the data as JSON. Progress goes to stderr, no streaming. Credentials only on non-interactive stdin (≤16 KiB). `pta --help` is the command reference.

| Exit | Code | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | `ok` | Completed |
| 1 | `operation_failed` | Failed; inspect the state before repeating |
| 2 | `invalid_input` | Input/validation |
| 3 | `not_ready` | Missing configuration or connection |
| 4 | `authorization_pending` | Needs a human action (OAuth) |
| 5 | `dependency_or_network` | Network, provider or installation (also `test providers` when no provider passes; `data.provider_checks` keeps each failure class) |
| 6 | `state_conflict` / `revision_conflict` / `contract_mismatch` | Incompatible revision, contract or version |

**MCP** (`app/mcp.rs`, `pta mcp`; `pta mcp config` prints the client configuration). A Model Context Protocol server over stdio (JSON-RPC, protocol 2025-06-18 with 2025-03-26 and 2024-11-05 accepted) that exposes the app's operations as tools, each a call to the same IPC contract (`cli::call`, profile edits through `snapshot` + `save_settings` with its revision), so every check stays in the host. stdout carries only protocol messages. Tools are `Read`, `Local` (notes, contexts, questions, people, work item guides, agent prompts, ratings, answer drafts, index refresh, daily task snapshots and their schedule) or `External` (sending to Teams, source authorization, the mode and the assistant's state). An `External` tool needs the user's confirmation: when the client declared MCP elicitation, the server sends `elicitation/create` with the action in plain words and runs it only on `accept` with `confirm: true` (messages arriving meanwhile are queued and handled after), whatever the agent sent; `decline` (or `accept` without `confirm: true`) stops it whatever the agent sent. A question that gets no answer falls back to the rule for clients without elicitation, because Claude Code declares the capability whether or not its host can show the question: `cancel` or an error reply (print mode answers `cancel` at once), and a refusal sooner than a person could have read the question (under one second: in the desktop app's Code tab and SDK sessions Claude Code forwards the question to its host, which declines for the user without showing it). Without elicitation, or after such an unanswered question, it refuses until called with `confirmed: true`, which the server's instructions and the skill allow only after the user approved that exact action. A question with no reply within two minutes (`ANSWER_WITHIN`) expires, because a client may hold it where nobody sees it (Claude Code under Remote Control from the phone app declares elicitation and never shows the form): the server withdraws it with `notifications/cancelled`, the call does nothing even with `confirmed: true` and tells the agent to ask the user in the conversation, and the session stops asking through the client, so the repeated call with `confirmed: true` decides. A late reply is a response nobody waits for and is dropped. A `decline` given in time still stops the action, and the session keeps asking. Tool annotations mark `readOnlyHint` and `destructiveHint`/`openWorldHint` accordingly. A host left from an update is replaced first, keeping the assistant's state, like any `pta` command.

**Remote MCP** (`app/web/agents.rs`, `POST /mcp` on the portal; [§3](#remote-mcp-endpoint-01064)). The same catalog, protocol helpers and confirmation rule over Streamable HTTP for agents with nothing installed, with the agent transport's narrower authority. `pta mcp config` prints, beside the stdio configuration, `remote`: the address (this installation's portal origin, or the coordinator's when the host is running and signed in), the Claude Code and Codex lines, a JSON client entry, the offered tools, and `transports` saying when to use each. `pta tools list` marks each tool `remote: offered` or the reason it is denied.

To add an operation: a method in `control::operate` (the browser only through `host.shell`, so it works without a browser session) (and in the lists allowed during login if it is a read) → a tool in `app/tools/mod.rs` (schema, effect, adapter and, if External, confirmation text), automatically available through CLI tools and MCP → optionally a dedicated CLI command (`HELP`, `validate_args`, mapping and `help_documents_every_accepted_command`) → if it changes a schema an older host does not understand, announce it in `Endpoint` like `wiki_support` → document it in the skill.

## 11. GUI

- **Serving.** `app::gui_assets::ASSETS` is the one list of the files under `desktop/ui` (a test fails when a file is missing from it or a listed file is missing); the GUI is flat, every file directly in that directory and served at `/<file>`. The portal and the local page route each one, and the document and every module but `i18n.js` and `catalog.js` (they also translate the pages shown without a session and hold no account data; a public module may import only public ones) need the listener's session. Every module is `text/javascript`; tests also check that each static import names a served module, that `index.html` loads one entry (`<script type="module" src="app.js">`) and preloads exactly its graph, that no module uses dynamic `import()`, `eval` or HTML injection, and each file's size limit (a module 40 KiB, the catalog 320 KiB, `index.html` and `style.css` 128 KiB). Each listener's middleware adds the same headers whatever the source (the CSP, `nosniff`, `no-referrer`, `DENY`) and `no-store` on every response but the GUI's files other than the document: those carry `Cache-Control: private, no-cache` and a strong `ETag` (a hash of the bytes served), and a request whose `If-None-Match` names it (strongly or weakly, as a compressing proxy marks it) gets `304`, after the same session check as the file. The document stays `no-store`, so the entry point, and the module graph it names, always come from the host; a new version or an edited file on the development path has new tags. Release builds serve the copies compiled into the binary. A debug build started with `PTA_DEV_UI_DIR` set to an absolute directory holding `index.html` reads each file from it on every request (logged as `gui_from_disk`); only plain file names directly inside it are read, so `..`, separators and links that resolve outside are refused, and a file missing there is a 404, never the embedded copy. The variable is read under `cfg(debug_assertions)` only and is on none of the allowlists a child inherits (the agents' `PASSED_ENV`, the portal accounts' `WORKER_ENV`, the update's build environment; tests check all three). `scripts/dev-host.sh` starts such a host on a temporary profile with an emptied environment and synthetic notes, and prints the single-use address its opener stand-in receives (docs/ui-design-conventions.md §10).

- **Workflows**: the eighth sidebar item, after Activities; below 860px it opens from More with Knowledge and Test chat, so the phone keeps five destinations, and the section opens with its first view. Three views (`workflows.js`, `workflow-blocks.js`, `workflow-runs.js`). *Workflows*: one row per workflow (enabled state, name, project, repositories, machine, agent, block count, last change) with an enabled switch (`workflow_save {id, enabled}`) and an editor fold that remembers being open: name, the ID (derived from the name until saved), the description the director reads, the project (a searchable sheet over `implementation_request_catalog`'s Azure targets of the enabled source, as Create request), the repositories (a searchable multi-select sheet over `workflow_repositories`), the execution machine (`machine_list`), one checkout per repository chosen with `chooseDirectories()` over `implementation_browse` on that machine (never a free-text path; another machine clears them), CLI, model and effort from `implementation_catalog` on that machine, instructions with a character counter, the ordered blocks (add from the library, move up or down, remove), `auto_start`, and Save, Disable/Enable and Remove (a second tap; `workflow_remove` with `confirmed`). What the host would refuse first is checked in the form; the host's reasons show in the editor, and a refusal naming a checkout also next to it. After a save each repository shows its checkout as verified, not verified (the reply's warning that names its directory) or mismatch (the refusal that names it). *Blocks*: the library as rows (kind, name, stage, the workflows that use it) with an editor fold: kind, name, ID, stage for a block that acts; an instruction's text; a notification's chat (a searchable sheet over `teams_chats` plus the personal chat), the mentions as choices among that chat's members, and the template with its placeholders as insert buttons, unknown ones refused in the form; a launcher's choice among the coordinator's approved launchers (`variable_consumer_list`, with program and digest), offered only where that list includes launchers (the owner's local page or portal session) and explained elsewhere. A notification or launcher block shows what it will do (stage, chat, mentions and text, or launcher, program and digest) and saves with `confirmed` only on a second press; removing a block that a workflow uses is refused in the form with those workflows' names. Every write goes through `applying` (pending settings edits are saved first, the form reloads after), since a workflow save changes the profile's revision. On a computer signed in to a coordinator the rows are read-only with a notice. **Settings › Workflows** (`settings-workflows.js`; its edits are unsaved Settings edits like any category's, flagged on its tab, kept when the category is shown again and saved or discarded with the save bar): the ready states of every project a workflow names or that already has some, as choices among the process's discovered states (`workflow_settings {project, organization}` per project, read after the settings), the watcher interval, how many runs advance at once and the pull-request wait (checked against the reply's `bounds` before sending), and the workflow director (machine, CLI, model, effort from `implementation_catalog` on that machine, or none) with a link that opens its `direct` prompt in Guides and instructions. The save bar saves the profile, then sends `workflow_settings_update` with only what changed (the director only when the person changed it) (each changed project's states, which replace that project's alone; `director: null` to clear it) and shows the reply's warnings. The section's «Ready states and director» link and each editor's ready-states link open the category; Home lists Workflows once some exist (reading only the saved `workflow_settings`, never catalogs or process states) and warns, linking there, while an enabled workflow that starts by itself has a project without a ready state. Settings › Workflows also shows the watcher (`workflow_settings`'s `watcher`): idle while no ready state is chosen, the interval, the time and outcome of the last check and any project it could not cover; on a computer where it never runs it says so. *Runs* (`workflow-runs.js`): the account's workflow runs (`workflow_runs`, at most 100, filtered by state and workflow), those that wait on the person first (`plan_review`, `unassigned`, `needs_attention`, `interrupted`, `failed`, marked «Waits for you») and then by last change, each row the state in words, the item's number and title, the reason it waits or its type, the workflow's name, how many children and when it last changed. A row opens the run's detail (`workflow_run_status`, read again every 15 seconds while it is open: a stale answer, or one that arrives while the person types or a confirmation is open, changes nothing, and an unchanged record is not re-rendered): the state and workflow, the verified work item link, type, project, times and whether the watcher started it, the attention or error text and what the director said, the stage timeline (each stage with its times and outcome, the current one marked), the children (link, coding state, repository, branch, outcome, pull requests with their status, and «Open agent session» through `implementation_sessions` and the existing session opener), the acting blocks' outcomes (stage, outcome, reason, exit code, duration) and the stored summaries. Its actions are the host's operations and nothing else: **Review plan** (in `plan_review`) reads the planning run from its machine (`implementation_runs`) and shows the same child review as Implement (`planChildrenReview`, shared), which creates the selected children only on a second press with the existing `implementation_create` (the item as a `Selection`), after which the run continues on its own; **Choose workflow** (in `unassigned`) offers the enabled workflows of the item's project with the director's reason and assigns one on a second press (`workflow_run_assign`); **Resume** (`needs_attention`, `interrupted`, `failed`) and **Cancel run** (any unfinished state) act on a second press (`workflow_run_resume`, `workflow_run_cancel`; a cancel's warning about a coding run that may still be running is shown); **Summary** asks the director (`workflow_summary`) and shows the stored text through the safe Markdown renderer with its time and model, earlier summaries in a fold, each one selectable; **Send by Teams** opens the searchable multi-select sheet over `teams_chats` (people and group chats, never the personal chat), then one confirmation that shows the exact text (editable; an edit is sent as `text`), each destination with its group's members as mention choices, and sends once per chat on a second press (`workflow_summary_send` with `confirmed`), showing each send's outcome (sent, refused with the reason) and keeping the run's send records in a fold. Home's attention rows and the Implement form's started run open the run's detail; a To-Do card whose item has an active run shows a small «workflow run» button (the active runs are read with the list, `workflow_runs`, silently when the host has no engine) that opens it. **Home** shows the runs waiting on the user (the snapshot's `workflow_attention`: `plan_review`, `unassigned`, `needs_attention`, `interrupted`, `failed`) once, in a panel «Workflow runs waiting for you» with one row per run (the state in words, the item's title, the reason, and the item's verified link) that opens the run's detail, and a button that opens the Runs view; it is hidden while no run waits.
- **To-Do**: daily work next to Home, with provider/type/state/count filters and pressable cards;
  Azure task-category types a work item guide lists with the bugs are included with them and open through task detail.
  Card backgrounds and borders reinforce known workflow states with green for completed, blue for
  active/development, amber for review/testing/validation and gray for not started; unknown states
  keep the default card style.
  Detail reads keep process-specific rich fields (HU criteria, bug reproduction/severity, GitHub
  epic description/milestone), assignment, discussion and evidence prominent; all fields and
  history fold below. Back preserves list/filter/scroll/focus; refresh, partial warnings, inline
  errors and stale-response guards apply to both views. Provider HTML is parsed by the core with `scraper` into bounded `RichNode` trees,
  then built by the UI from allowed tags and text without browser HTML parsing or original attributes; external images become links, with no content execution or remote
  image loads. The adjacent original-link and copy buttons open the provider URL or copy it to the clipboard, separately from opening detail.
  Tablet/phone grid rows reserve only the header's content height, so loading, empty and error states never stretch the sticky header.
  Plain-text Markdown and Markdown embedded in provider preformatted evidence use the existing
  safe Markdown renderer; explicit code blocks remain code. Bare HTTPS URLs in discussion and
  rich text become validated links. Links and evidence filter locally by the resolved work item
  type or other links. Resolved Azure tasks reuse the Activities task cards and shared detail;
  other related work item types retain their original links and remain filterable.
  process task categories choose `get_azure_task` (including custom task names). Nested Back
  restores the preceding detail, relation filter, scroll and focus, including during pending reads.
  Provider and state selectors call the shared list tools, with an Azure source selector when
  needed. The list asks for 10 items and «Show more» for the next step (25, 50, 100), keeping
  what is shown; a new filter starts again at 10. A row shows the two recorded dates nearest to
  today (its creation date when none is recorded); the detail retains each HU and development
  date separately and shows unavailable dates explicitly. Errors and partial coverage appear inline; request
  generations discard stale responses. On the local page the link icon uses `open_work_item`; the portal
  opens a new tab. On phones the floating bar keeps five destinations: the four daily tasks
  and More, which reveals Knowledge and Test chat and closes after selection or Escape.
  This extends the current sticker design, without replacing its colors, typography, theme
  control, Home layout, icons or interaction patterns. The preview takes its version from
  Cargo.toml so fixture metadata cannot mislabel current UI screenshots.

`ui/` is plain HTML/CSS and native ES modules without a framework, a bundler or a build (the module layout, the typing rule and why are in [ui-design-conventions.md](ui-design-conventions.md) §2), written in English, with a sidebar and a look each device chooses in Settings › General › Appearance (kept in `localStorage`): one of three styles (Stickers, the default sticker sheet with ink outlines, a color per section and hard shadows on what can be pressed; Soft; Minimalist), one of ten palettes (Paper by default) and a light or dark mode that follows the system unless chosen; the assistant's face shows its state. The sidebar groups the sections by task: daily work (Home, To-Do, Messages, Activities, Workflows), then what feeds the answers (Knowledge, Test chat), and Settings pinned at the bottom above the status pill. Its visual and interaction conventions are in [ui-design-conventions.md](ui-design-conventions.md). `i18n.js` (imported before the views, its catalog in `catalog.js`, both served without a session) shows it in `llm.language`: the English text is the key of its catalog; static text, placeholders, titles and labels are translated when the page loads (`translate="no"` and `<code>` keep technical values), every module passes its strings through `t()`/`tn()` with named `{placeholders}`, and `tx()` translates the known English host messages, run stages, older stored notes and the activity names the host stores in English (Teams messages and calls, Wiki pages, commits, pipeline runs, releases), retained for legacy records. The language is cached in `localStorage` so the first paint is already translated; a different saved `llm.language` reloads the page once. Dates and numbers use the device's locale when it speaks that language. `transport.js` sends every request to the same-origin `/api/control` with the existing contract, in one of two modes: the local page or the portal. The portal reuses every tab and adds GitHub account sign-out controls (also in Settings), remote Microsoft login, links that open in a new tab and a phone layout: below 860 px the sidebar becomes a top bar with short labels and an icon-only Settings, and tab lists become a grid of large choices instead of a strip that scrolls sideways; below 680 px wide (an iPhone 15 is 393 px) the four daily tasks and More (Knowledge and Test chat) form a five-destination floating tab bar (the active one shows its short label, the others their icon) and Settings stays in the title bar as an icon named for assistive technology, the page respects the safe areas (`viewport-fit=cover`), inputs use 16 px text and controls are at least 44 px tall. The page never scrolls sideways or bounces past its edges (root `overflow-x: clip` and `overscroll-behavior: none`), and hover styles apply only to pointers that hover, so a tap never leaves one stuck. The portal also serves the icons and a PWA manifest (`display: standalone`, `short_name` «Assistant»), so the page installs on a phone's home screen with the assistant's face; those files answer without Cloudflare Access (an exact Bypass app each, and the portal's `PUBLIC_ASSETS`), because WebKit fetches a page's icons without cookies and would otherwise get the login page; iOS keeps the icon it saw when the page was added, so a new icon needs removing and adding it again. There are no GUI-only IPC methods. It respects the CSP that the local page and the portal send (§9): no inline styles or scripts (only CSSOM properties from JS), no `innerHTML`, and fetch only to its own origin; session cookies are never available to JS.

- **Where it runs**: the [owner's local page](#owners-local-page) in the system browser (launching `personal-teams-assistant`, or `pta app open`; this computer's GUI with the owner's authority), and the portal (`browser: true`, the person's own device; the GUI of an account). Both are the same GUI, with its translations, themes, directory sheet and responsive layout; `transport.js` chooses the mode.
- **Account sign-in and Synchronize**: on the owner's local page (never the portal browser, a portal account or a coordinator, see `coordinating`), the sidebar footer carries an account indicator above the status pill: «Not signed in» with **Sign in**, «Sign-in no longer valid» with **Sign in again** (revoked), or «Signed in» with the coordinator host and the configuration state and **Synchronize** (`account_sync`; refused while the forms hold unsaved edits, then the forms reload). Below 860px it is the action's icon only, still named. A standalone computer that could join an account opens a sign-in screen once per page load (skippable per device, `pta-sign-in-skipped` in `localStorage`): the portal address (prefilled with the remembered origin) and «Replace this computer's settings with the account's», checked by default; Sign in runs the browser-assisted `machine_login` dialog and, once connected, `account_sync {reset}`. Home's «Your account on this computer» is shown on such a computer too, with its coordinator step's Sign in. These are the only sign-in and synchronize controls: Settings › Execution machines keeps Disconnect, Variables' Refresh only rereads, and Account configuration keeps only its replace action.
- **Home** (default tab): the assistant's state (running, observing, Teams pending, stopped) in a block of its color with the assistant's face and Start/Restart/Stop; a notice while a daily task snapshot runs; «Setup», computed from the snapshot (required credentials — `GRAPH_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `STATE_ENCRYPTION_KEY` count as ready because they are generated at start —, an active model, the Entra registration with the same rules as `validate_teams_setup`, account, public URL, at least one enabled source with external processing and an audience, assistant started and, optionally, sending on; signing in to an account is not repeated there), hidden once every step including the optional one is done; «Status», one row per part (Microsoft account, the Azure and GitHub CLI sessions, mode, reception, tunnel, models, sources, personal chat) that opens the Settings section or tab where it is changed: the rows that need attention stay in sight and the parts in order fold into one line («Everything in order: 9 parts») that opens them; and the recent activity from `audit`: four outcome tiles (answered, withheld or unanswered, errors, in progress) that open Messages filtered, and rows that open that message expanded. Recent activity takes the wide column, setup and status stack beside it; a phone reads setup, recent activity, then status. The sidebar flags errors or uncertain sends of the last 24 h.
- **Settings**: one category at a time in an ARIA tab list (arrow keys, Home and End): **General** (answers: observation mode, language, style; the Teams message style with its combinations of colors, each color, the stripe width and the two text sizes under a fold, and a preview drawn with Teams' own colors; this device's appearance (style, palette and mode); on a signed-in installation, **Account configuration** with the loaded and account revisions and the local setup still required, see [Account configuration](#account-configuration); the web account in the portal), **Microsoft Teams** (Teams and Entra with a link to the machine that answers Teams, personal chat, and public URL and tunnel, a panel that folds while the URL is configured and shows its host beside the title), **Models** (one row per model with on/off, model, effort, order and removal; Add model can repeat a provider with a different model or effort, up to 16 entries; saves `llm.chain`), **Machines** (this computer's name and allowed directory roots, and the account's machines in one list with the Teams radio, Start/Stop, **Update to X.Y.Z** (shown for a machine with `capabilities.upgrade` that reports a pending release or runs an older version than another machine of the account; a second press confirms; disabled while it is offline, preparing or waiting) and Manage; in the portal, **Connect this computer** folds the [one-command installation](#one-command-installation) for the visitor's system, with macOS, Linux and Windows selectable and Copy command), **Daily record** (daily record with the actual hours field under a fold, schedule with its time zone under a fold, Azure sources and advisory target), **Variables** (stores changes right away, automatically imports existing application credentials and lists the account's stored variables by name and description with a masked value, Reveal/Hide, Copy value, Edit (description and replacement value separately, with Cancel) and a two-tap Delete, refreshes on request, shows agent-requested entries waiting for a value and adds one through a masked field, see [Account variables](#account-variables); a second panel, **Where variables are used**, shows each built-in consumer's fixed destination, the variable it uses by name and version, a selector with Use this variable, Test key and Stop using; then one row per `[secrets]` reference naming the sources (read or work-item linking) and settings that use it, the variable it resolves to or that it is missing, and a selector whose Use this variable rewrites that reference's name in the configuration and saves it (several references may share one variable, and Home's missing-variables step opens this panel); and lists approved launchers with Remove) and **Advanced** (Connect an agent: the remote MCP endpoint's address with Copy address and how to add it; import, updates and reviewed cross-account credential migration). A section with unsaved edits shows a lime dot; Microsoft Teams, Language models and Variables show an amber dot while they block the assistant. Links from Home open the right section. Fields that do not apply stay hidden with their value kept: the allowed-chats list while all chats are discovered, the daily time or the interval and window depending on the schedule. Workdays are seven toggles (`weekdays`, at least one; checked before asking the host). Retired task-write settings are no longer shown. It no longer shows `max_answer_chars`/`max_detailed_answer_chars` (not applied; the saved value is kept).
- **Messages**: the last 100 jobs of `assistant.db` (method `audit` with content): state, message, interpretation, intent, answer sent or proposed (rendered as Markdown), model and fallbacks, references and how they were chosen, notices and the step log. Filters by state with their counts, a local search over the loaded messages' text, outcome and kind (accent- and case-insensitive), refresh every 10 s and, by default, without messages not addressed to the assistant. A message the assistant left unanswered (informational, a request for the person, no authorized source; not one with sensitive data) shows **Answer this message** with **Generate answer** (`answer_refine` without context). Once an answer exists (withheld, observed or written here, «Draft ready») the block becomes **Review and send**: a context box, **Regenerate answer** (`answer_refine`) and **Send answer** (`answer_send`; a second tap confirms). Both states carry a folded **Sources** chooser: «Automatic» (the sources authorized for the conversation) until the user checks some of the enabled sources with external processing, each marked when it is authorized for that chat; the last choice is preselected. While the job is `refining` (state «Generating») the card follows it every 3 s and reports the outcome; the typed context and the chosen sources survive refreshes, which pause while the context has focus. Both pass no revision, like reads, so they never advance a stale form's revision.
- **Knowledge**: lists **every** source (file, URL and tools such as the Wiki, Azure DevOps activity, own Teams messages or the activity record) as a compact row: state, description, location, topics and audience, the enabled/external-processing switches, and a fold to edit description, topics and audiences (open folds survive re-renders). The GitHub App client ID is folded as advanced. It creates `repository` and `file` sources and, while the map has none, the activity record («Add activity record») and the notes source, all born disabled, without audiences or external processing; other tools are added with `pta sources add`. Its Organization tab holds the organization's name, the sources that document it (checks over the map's Wiki, repository, file and URL sources), its entries (rows with an edit fold) and, while the map has none, the organization source («Add organization source», born disabled); the work item guides (their only editor) and the agent instructions open from To-Do («Guides and instructions»). Removing a source also removes it from the contexts and the organization. It does not let you remove a repository a tool uses. Local and GitHub repositories.
  It has six views in a tab list: **Sources** (above; a repository row says how many files and sections its index holds and offers «Refresh index»; the add form takes a whole repository or one file), **Contexts** (one row per context with its keywords and sources, and a fold to edit name, keywords, description, the sources it reads as checkboxes and its instructions; saved with the map), **Questions** (add/edit/remove question text, selected source checkboxes and associated requests from review), **People** (one row per person with how the user addresses them, and a fold to edit name, other names, how to address them, what to keep in mind and Teams IDs; «Add a person», prefilled from a message's sender), **Notes** (search, the notes as rows with their contexts and origin, an editor per note and «Add a note»; saved at once through `notes_save`/`notes_remove`, with a second tap to remove, and an offer to add the notes source while the map has none) and **Repositories** (local checkouts — «Use as a source» on those without a repository source, and a description in «Add a local checkout» adds one — and GitHub). Settings › Advanced has **Semantic search**: endpoint URL, model and credential name (mapped as `secret://knowledge/embeddings`).
- **Test chat**: offers the **saved** sources that are enabled and allowed external processing, of any type (the simulation reads the persisted map); shows the answer as Markdown (the same subset Teams receives), the verified references, partial coverage and a readable reason when it does not answer. Links copy the URL to the clipboard instead of navigating the page.
- **Activities**: recorded days, newest first, with date-range filters and counts/hours. Selecting a day opens its saved task list; selecting a task opens `get_azure_task` through the reused To-Do detail renderer. Cards and detail headers keep a separate original-link icon; Back restores scroll and focus. Saved empty days remain visible. A folded manual save control, at the top of the screen under the toolbar, and schedule settings replace the previous proposal/editor flow. Daily hours only warn; no creation, refinement or pending decisions remain. Async reads discard stale responses and do not steal focus during polling.

## 13. Change recipes

- **New tool type:** a `ToolSpec` variant + `validate` + `name` (+ `reviews_activity` if a review should read it) + a branch in `Tools::execute` (with a timeout) and, for reviews, in `Tools::review` → if it returns typed evidence, deserialize it in the pipeline and register its references → wiremock tests → document the schema in the skill and in `knowledge-map.example.toml`.
- **New LLM provider:** implement `Backend` (one JSON object per call; credit/limit failures as `Unavailable(Failure::UsageLimit)`, without provider text), add it to `PROVIDERS`, `efforts` and `llm::model_for`, and to the catalog (`llm::catalog`) for the GUI. Prompts, the chain and `runtime`/`local_chat`/`diagnostics` do not change.
- **Change what the user may configure in a case:** a field of `cases::BuiltIn` (and its default in `llm::prompts`), its check in `cases::validate`, where the pipeline reads it (`Pipeline::case_on`/`case_named`/`case_sources`/`prompts`/`categories`) and the GUI's Cases tab. A new built-in case also needs its route and a `caseTexts` entry in `app.js`.
- **Change a model decision:** the prompt and contract live in `llm::Model` (`classify_intent`/`classify_intent_as`, `select_context`, `select_references`, `select_tool`, `standalone_request`, `generate_response`, `propose_registration`, `plan_links`; texts a user may replace are in `llm::prompts`) and the fallback in `Chain`. Contracts reject missing/extra fields, unknown values and invalid confidences (`invalid_answer`). In the pipeline, a failed classification of an ambiguous message retries the job; every other decision degrades: the literal request, no tool, or code-chosen references.
- **New step in a message's log:** `audit.step("name", "detail without message content")`, in English; the GUI shows it unchanged.
- **New storage implementation (phase 2 of the [cloud storage design](organization-and-cloud-storage.md#10-phases)):** each account aggregate has one trait under `store/` with a shared `contract::run` suite; a new implementation (the cloud account store) passes that suite unchanged before any caller uses it. Validation and access rules stay with the callers, never in a store; search and ranking stay in the local index, which reads a store's records. Today: `SettingsStore` (configuration, knowledge map with the organization, tunnel path), implemented by the profile's files (`LocalSettings`); `NotesStore` (verified notes, answer evaluations, approved examples), implemented by the knowledge index's record tables (`LocalNotes`); `AuditStore` (the finished messages' audits), implemented by the finished rows of `assistant.db` › `jobs` (`LocalAudit`); `ActivityStore` (daily snapshots, summaries, finished runs, the legacy record), implemented by `activity-registration.db` (`LocalActivity`); `ImplementationStore` (finished agent runs and request runs), implemented by `implementation/runs.db` (`LocalImplementation`); `OrganizationStore` (the organization and its work item guides as one unit), implemented by the map's sections through the settings store (`LocalOrganization`).
- **New configuration field:** `#[serde(default)]`, validation in `Config::validate`, exposed in the GUI if it applies; `pta config set` accepts it by path automatically.

## 14. Current decisions and why

- **Daily tasks are read, never created (0.7.33).** Activities is a day → tasks → shared-detail history. The schedule saves existing Azure tasks; hours are advisory, and recaps read verified saved tasks without rediscovering activity. It supersedes the earlier task registration and linking flow, whose data and credentials remain compatible (see Git history for its decisions).

- **No model vetoes retrieval.** A selector used to decide the source before reading it; in a real sample (2026-10-01) it rejected half the documentation questions with confidence 0.29–0.38 and the Wiki was never read. Do not reintroduce a topic or relevance prefilter.
- **No model withholds answers.** On 2026-10-01 a follow-up («cuál es el endpoint para el ambiente de test») was written well but the external reviewer did not tie it to any page and the rule «every Wiki answer cites a page» discarded it silently. Reference selection is informative; if no page is chosen the consulted ones are listed. What withholds an answer is decided by code, and the personal chat is notified.
- **The language model replaced the external classifier (0.6.1).** A separate classification API classified ambiguous messages, picked references and ran an informative final review; it was a second provider, credential and point of failure, and `pta test providers` failed on it without detail. The model chain now does the first two with closed contracts and code validation; the final review was dropped because it never blocked anything.
- **Unregistered work is reviewed, not guessed.** On 2026-10-03 «¿qué tareas o trabajo he realizado que no están registrados en tareas?» went to the Wiki, cited one page and asked which system the user used. The review reads the user's real activity in every authorized source, compares it with work items in code and lets verified authorship speak in the first person. Only work items Azure DevOps returns are named, because an unverifiable number would withhold the whole answer.
- **A stale GUI must not overwrite the profile (0.6.5).** The GUI took the revision of every reply, including its 15-second `snapshot` polls, without reloading its form, so «Save» from a GUI opened earlier passed the revision check and wrote back an old `config.toml` and `knowledge-map.toml` (it erased `link_secret_ref` set with `pta`). Its form revision now changes only when it reloads or writes; a stale save gets `revision_conflict`. Since 0.10.61 the poll also compares the saved configuration and map with the ones the form loaded (`reloadIfChanged`): without unsaved edits a change made underneath it (an account synchronization by the host, `pta config set`) reloads the form, so an open window never shows an older copy; with unsaved edits the form is kept and the revision check still refuses the stale save.
- **The app speaks the user's language (0.7.5).** The interface was English while the user's answers and tasks were Spanish. The source stays English for code and agents; one catalog translates the GUI, and a test keeps it complete.
- **Answers sound like the user, not like the app.** The same 2026-10-13 reply («El contenido y el estado del elemento enlazado no se pueden verificar con la evidencia disponible. ¿Necesitas un resumen, revisar su estado o ayuda para redactar el hotfix?») repeated the prompt's own vocabulary and offered a menu. The prompt now asks for a colleague's natural, professional voice, forbids internal terms, stock disclaimers and menus, and mentions a gap only when the request needs it; attributions are plain phrases too.
- **A shared link is thanked, not answered.** On 2026-10-13 «Hotfi: <work item URL>» was taken as a question only because the URL had `?showParents=false&workitem=…`; the Wiki was searched and the answer said the item could not be verified and offered help. Someone sharing a link expects an acknowledgment: code ignores `?` inside links and answers a bare share with a fixed thanks by first name, without a model.
- **Requests for the person are not answered.** On 2026-10-02 the assistant answered «te puedo llamar», «necesito llamarte» and «necesito que revisemos lo que se debe subir…» with unrelated Wiki pages. Calls, meetings, joint reviews and availability can only be answered by the person: code discards them before the question shortcut and the intent classification has the `personal` category for those code does not recognize.
- **Conversation context, not only the last exchange.** The 10 earlier messages with author and time let short follow-ups be understood; they go as context, not evidence.
- **Wiki attribution is built by code** from verified metadata.
- **Links go where the entity is named.** On 2026-10-14 a reply ended with «Fuentes» and «Páginas consultadas»: a release stage cited because «Área Desarrollo» contains «Desarrollo», three deployment pages the text did not use, and «PR !7348» without a link. Answers now carry no sources section: code links the distinctive words that name each entity («#41477 — PR Pipeline Template»), the reference model marks the words for the rest and tells a project from a homonymous stage, a pull request the request names is read by ID so it has a verified link, and the full list of what was cited and read stays in the audit and the Messages tab.
- **The model does not copy IDs or page links.** It returns only text and mode; links come from the verified registry, placed by code on the words that name each entity. It may copy a URL that appears literally in the evidence (a documented endpoint), because forbidding it prevented answering «¿cuál es el endpoint de test?».
- **Fallback per call, without memory.** The chain holds at most 16 model choices; providers and models may repeat, including different efforts. Every call starts with the default provider; it is not remembered that it was out of credits. It costs a fast failed attempt while the exhaustion lasts, in exchange for returning to the default as soon as it recovers. It falls back on any failure, not only credits: an invalid contract or a missing CLI must not leave the question unanswered either.
- **CLIs instead of APIs for Codex and Claude**: they use the subscription already signed in on those CLIs, with no new keys in the app. In exchange they are invoked without tools or customizations so they behave like one model call, like the API.
- **Formatting is decided in code.** The model writes bounded Markdown and code turns it into Teams HTML; links are built from the registry. Colors are names mapped to a fixed palette, not CSS from the model, so a model cannot inject styles or markup.
- **Follow-ups are resolved before searching, not vetoed.** Searching only the words of «¿y si quiero pagar 2 servicios?» did not find the page. The standalone rewrite only changes the query; tool selection and audiences stay in code.
- **One tool per normal answer**, to bound cost, latency and surface; activity reviews are the exception because comparing activity with registered work needs every source at once, status rounds add only the user's own recent Wiki pages, because a committed deployment date and its pending items live there, and an answer written from sources the user chose reads all of them (§6.12).
- **A status round reads the HU itself and the user's deployment page (2026-10-14).** «Ronda de actualización: 1) avance de lo que tienes en curso: HU #41553, 2) compromiso, 3) riesgos o bloqueos» was withheld (the answer named HU #41553 without a verified reference) and its proposal said the progress was not at hand. The HU had been sent to validation and reassigned to Alex Rivera, so the status tool, which kept only items assigned to or last changed by the user, never read it; and the committed production date and the missing production variables were in the user's own Wiki page. Code now reads a named work item directly with its owner and history, keeps handed-over items, adds the user's own recent Wiki pages, and the prompt says how to answer each part.
- **Status reads stay within five weeks and the user's own work (2026-10-14).** Reporting activity or status is about what the user did recently. Every activity and status read looks back at most five weeks, a named work item is described only if the user took part in it (otherwise the answer says it is not theirs and who holds it), and the work the Activities section recorded by day is added as context, filtered by the conversation's authorized sources. On the same day the user's real deployment page, «PAP - HU 41796 - …», was missed because «PAP» was not a deployment word and its date sat far down a 23 KB page: «PAP» in a title now marks a plan, and its excerpt favors dates and pending items.
- **What the user did is answered from what they approved (2026-10-15).** The Activities section already kept every approved task by day in `activity-registration.db`, but «¿qué hice el martes?» went to the status tool or to a full activity review, which read Azure DevOps for two weeks and answered at length. A question that names a day or period now reads only the record of those days, so the answer is short and matches the tasks the user approved; the GUI shows the same record as a History view. The store stays SQLite: one user and one profile per host need no server, the file already lives next to the other databases, and a move online would go to libSQL/Turso (SQLite's dialect and schema, unchanged SQL) rather than Postgres, only if several devices ever need to share it.
- **The user decides on a held answer (2026-10-14).** A withheld proposal could only be read in the app. The user can now give the agent context to rewrite it (never sent automatically) and send the reviewed text once; code still refuses sensitive data, oversized answers and an edited message, and records the send before the request like every send.
- **The user may answer what the assistant left unanswered, from the sources they choose (0.7.18).** On 2026-10-15 a colleague's instruction («para un hotfix de producción va como Development Bug (no HU): describe el síntoma…, la causa y el cambio, y vincúlalo al incidente») was correctly classified as informational and left unanswered, but the user wanted to acknowledge it without typing the reply in Teams. Any unanswered message (except one with sensitive data) can now get an answer generated on request, written as a reply rather than as an answer to a question, then regenerated with context and sent once. The user picks its sources, which may lie outside the conversation's audiences because the user reviews and sends the result; Teams-triggered answers still read only the conversation's authorized sources, and conversations without one still never reach a model unless the user chooses a source.
- **The activity record is a source of its own (0.7.19).** On 2026-10-16 the user expected what they approved in Activities to be a source to choose, and could not find it: it was read only as part of Azure DevOps activity, and an answer from chosen sources never read it, even with that source chosen. It is now an `activity_record` tool authorized on its own. Its registered tasks show wherever it is authorized, because their title, HU, hours and description are what the user approved and wrote to Azure DevOps (the first production check, choosing only the record, found every task of the day hidden: each had a Wiki page or Teams message among its activities); work not registered yet still needs the Wiki and Teams sources it was drawn from; a map without one keeps the old behavior so existing profiles answer as before. The Azure DevOps catalog moved into the map too (`catalog`), so a knowledge repository is no longer required to read Azure DevOps.
- **Agents operate the app through MCP, and the user confirms what leaves it (0.7.23).** On 2026-10-16 the user asked to operate everything the app does from agents such as Codex or Claude Code, with the agent asking when it must confirm. The MCP server is a thin translation of tools into the existing IPC contract, so it adds no permission and every audience, check and record is the host's; it runs as `pta mcp` because `pta` already finds or starts the host. Writes the user can undo in the app (notes, contexts, questions, people, ratings, drafts) run directly; what reaches others or grants access (Teams, Azure DevOps, sources, mode, the service) needs the user's confirmation, asked by the client itself through MCP elicitation when available so an agent cannot confirm on the user's behalf, and otherwise required as an explicit `confirmed` argument.
- **The app learns from what the user approves, not from what it wrote (0.7.22).** On 2026-10-16 the user asked to rate answers, tie information to contexts and improve how each person is answered. Ratings are kept with the message; the improvements come only from what the user explicitly approves: a corrected answer saved as a verified note (evidence, authorized like any source), a good answer kept as an example (structure and tone only, never facts, because an example may carry facts from a chat the next conversation may not read) and a note on how to address each person (only when answering them). Nothing is learned automatically from sent answers, so a mistake is never reinforced without the user seeing it.
- **The knowledge index is SQLite, not a vector database (0.7.20).** On 2026-10-16 the user asked for repositories as sources of their own, notes, contexts such as «cobros» and a possible move to a vector database for semantic search, keeping SQL for configuration. One user and one profile per host hold thousands of sections at most: SQLite's FTS5 (already compiled into the bundled SQLite) gives BM25 ranking with folded accents and no new dependency, and vectors are optional, stored next to each section and compared by brute force, fused with the text ranking. A separate vector server (Qdrant, LanceDB) would add a process, a dependency of tens of megabytes and a second store to back up, for no gain at this size, and none of the configured models (Codex, Claude Code, DeepSeek) offers embeddings, so they come from an optional OpenAI-compatible endpoint (or Ollama on the same computer). The activity record stays in `activity-registration.db`: it is answered by day, not by meaning. Contexts are code-matched keywords rather than a model decision, so they never veto retrieval: they only narrow to sources the user grouped and add the user's instructions.
- **No send is retried**: Graph has no idempotency; skipping an answer is preferred to duplicating it.
- **Public desktop OAuth with the scopes already granted**; the existing Entra registration allows `http://localhost` and other organizations. Asking for new permissions would require a new consent. Own Teams messages are read with `Chat.Read`, already granted.
- **Cargo only**: no signed/notarized bundles or Docker server; the host is the only runtime. Each release also attaches plain archives of both binaries for macOS (Apple silicon and Intel), Windows and Linux, built from the same package with the same flags, with SHA-256 sums and GitHub build provenance, so `cargo binstall` installs in seconds instead of compiling (2026-10-30). They are unsigned, not bundles, and nothing in the application downloads them.
- **No LTO in release builds (2026-10-30).** Thin LTO re-optimized every dependency once per binary: on the server (four jobs) an update with dependencies already built took 4 min 23 s with it and 2 min 18 s without, and a build from scratch about 5 min without it; the binaries are nearly the same size. The application waits on the network, not the CPU.
- **GitHub Actions for CI/CD, the server pulls (2026-10-17).** Merging, then installing on the server, restarting and publishing by hand took every delivery. The repository is public, so GitHub's standard runners are free on Linux, macOS and Windows; Cloudflare Workers Builds (3,000 minutes a month, 20-minute builds, no Rust or GUI libraries) only keeps publishing the landing. The server is never a runner, because on a public repository a pull request from anyone could run code there: it pulls releases from crates.io, which only the release workflow (or the owner) publishes to. Publishing uses crates.io Trusted Publishing, which rejects `workflow_run`, so the release runs on push and waits for CI itself.

- **One headless host, the GUI in the browser (0.11.0).** The owner's local page already gave this computer the whole GUI with the owner's authority, so the Tauri window only duplicated it, at the cost of a second shell, GTK and WebKit build dependencies on Linux (with their RustSec notices), a second build and test pass in CI and a third version file. The host is now one build on every operating system and its GUI is the local page or the portal; the tray and native notifications ended with it (§15).
- **CI work split by event (2026-10-26).** PRs retain the complete path-selected Linux/UI/macOS/Windows checks. Pushes to `main` retain security, Linux tests and verified Cargo packaging for the exact delivery commit, while skipping duplicate linting and UI/macOS/Windows jobs. A new unpublished version always requests Linux verification. The weekly run checks the complete matrix on `main` and refreshes its trusted caches. No PR result/artifact authorizes publishing a different commit. The release gate, read-only CI permissions and standard hosted runners remain unchanged. See [delivery](delivery.md).

- **The organization is data, and account data may move to Cloudflare (2026-11-02).** The user may change employer and keep the same flow, and the employer's conventions change over time, so the repository names no real organization: work item guides, norms, architecture, conventions, glossary and templates become entries of an optional, detachable organization layer of each account, whose documents come from existing sources, never from code. Configuration, knowledge and history are to move to one SQLite-backed Durable Object per account (per-account D1 needs a privileged API token or a redeploy per account, and cannot export databases with FTS5), encrypted by the client with search in the local replica; secrets, sessions, checkouts, roots and in-flight sends stay local. The analysis, the options with their verified limits and the phases are in [organization-and-cloud-storage.md](organization-and-cloud-storage.md); the owner chose the Free plan of the existing Cloudflare account, no jurisdiction, client-side encryption, one store per user and no organizations shared between accounts. Phase 1b (0.10.16) added the local layer: `[organization]` in the map, the `organization` source, entries for the implementation agents, CLI/MCP/GUI parity, and the organization with the guides in the portable configuration.
- **A delegated implementation is fully autonomous on its machine (0.11.10).** The first real delegated run (2026-11-07, issue #231, a Mac, Claude Code with `acceptEdits`) wrote the code in 81 seconds and then stopped in `needs_attention`: `git switch -c`, `git commit`, `git push`, `gh pr create` and even `sh -n` needed approvals nobody at the portal could give, and the owner had to open the Mac and resume the session to approve each one. The owner decided: «in a delegated implementation the agent is fully autonomous on the execution machine; the person chose the trusted machine, the directories, the CLI and the model and confirmed the start, and that is the authorization. No prompt, no stop before the end of the request.» Each CLI is launched in its no-prompt mode (§16), the prompt says so, and a prompt that still happens is recorded instead of waited on. What still bounds a run is the trusted machine, its allowed roots, the restricted environment, PTA's own confirmations and the shared server's refusal; merging, publishing and deploying stay with the person.
- **Workflows compose the implementation flow; the person keeps the gate (epic #238, 0.12.0–0.12.10).** The user asked for a software factory: an HU or bug assigned to them and in a ready state taken through its plan, children, code, pull requests, release and a message to whoever waits for it, with a summary on request. Each piece already existed (To-Do reads, Implement's plan and coding runs, `teams_post`, approved launchers), so a workflow names them and an engine on the coordinator advances a run through the existing operations; it never starts a provider process of its own, never writes to Azure DevOps and never merges. The decisions: **the plan-review gate stays**: children are created only by the person's confirmation of the reviewed plan (`implementation_create`), and no setting removes it, because writing to the organization's backlog is the one step a model must not take alone. **The director decides nothing else**: it returns a workflow ID from the list code gave it, or none, and code checks the ID, the enabled state and the project; its summaries carry no links of their own. **Blocks that act are authorized when they are saved**: a notification names its chat, mentions and text and is confirmed then; a launcher block names an approved launcher of the coordinator by digest and only the owner saves it, so a run acts later without anyone at the screen and still only within what was approved; a launcher whose digest changed runs nothing. **Launchers run only on the coordinator** (where the engine and its authority are), never on another machine through the worker protocol. **Teams chats only**, no channels and no new Graph scopes. **One child at a time**, so a run never has two coding agents in the same checkouts. **Never retried**: a notification or send whose result is unknown stays `uncertain`, an attempted launcher whose result is unknown stops the run for a person, and the GUI leaves a chat with an uncertain earlier send out of a new send unless the person sends it again. The rehearsal (`app/workflows/rehearsal.rs`) runs the whole cycle with synthetic providers and counts every provider write.
- **Reception stays on each computer; reading on request follows the account (2026-11-05).** A second computer of the account refused `pta teams messages self` until «Discover all my authorized chats» was ticked there by hand. Carrying `graph.discover_all_chats`/`graph.allowed_chats` with the account would have let an edit on any computer widen what the machine that answers Teams subscribes to, receives, answers and sends to, because `allowed_collection` gates all of that. The account's separate `teams_reading` widens only the operator's reads on request, made with each computer's own Microsoft session; it starts off, replicas may only narrow it, and once set an older host cannot install the account configuration until it is updated.

## 15. Known limits

Archive installations update from the release archive of this package's GitHub repository only: if a release's archive for the computer's target is missing (or not attached yet, the minutes after crates.io lists the version), staging fails with that reason and unattended updating tries again an hour later. Build provenance (`gh attestation verify`) is checked by the install scripts when `gh` is present, not by the updater, which relies on HTTPS to the release host and the published SHA-256 next to the archive (both come from the same release). On a Mac whose credentials are in the Keychain unattended updating only prepares: a new version's ad-hoc signature makes the Keychain ask, so the person installs it. That behavior was reasoned from the Keychain's access lists and the per-build signatures (architecture §4), not observed on a Mac in CI, whose runners have no interactive session to answer the dialog. Unattended updating cannot see work in a profile outside this one, like a person's update. The installers cover the four release targets only (no Linux on ARM or musl, no FreeBSD), need `curl` and `tar` (macOS, Linux) and do not check their own integrity before running beyond GitHub's HTTPS: `curl | sh` and the PowerShell download trust the release host, and build provenance is checked only when `gh` is installed and signed in. The Intel macOS archive is tested under Rosetta on an Apple-silicon runner, not on Intel hardware. A Linux computer without a systemd user manager (some containers, WSL without systemd) gets the host running but not started at login. The portal does not list pending sign-in requests in the computers list: they are unauthenticated and not bound to an account, so the approval stays the page the command opens, with its code.

The owner's local page keeps its key in the tab's `sessionStorage`: a new tab, or a browser without storage after a reload, has no key and needs `pta app open` again. Browser-session cookies survive a browser that restores its previous session, until the 12-hour limit or a host restart, but are useless without the key. A process running as the same OS user can read the short-lived handoff file, as it can read the profile itself. Detecting a browser session is a heuristic per platform.

On Linux the handoff file is under the profile directory (`~/.config/dev.personalteams.assistant/page-open/`), which is hidden. A browser installed as a snap or flatpak cannot read hidden directories in the home folder, so `pta app open` may show that browser's «file not found» or «access denied» page. Make a browser that is not sandboxed the default handler of HTML files (for example a distribution package of Firefox with `xdg-mime default firefox.desktop text/html`) and run `pta app open` again. The code is never passed on a command line, so there is no other way to hand it over.

The remote MCP endpoint offers only what the portal forwards for the account: Teams message reads and posts (`teams_messages`, `teams_search`, `teams_post`) and `account_records` are not in the portal's allowlist, so a remote agent cannot use them; an agent on a computer with the host uses `pta mcp` for them. The chat directory (`teams_chats`: each chat's name and members, never a message) is forwarded and offered, so the portal's GUI and a remote agent can choose a workflow notification's chat. Its sessions are sealed with a key held in memory, so a portal restart makes clients initialize again (they receive 404 for the old `Mcp-Session-Id`, as the protocol expects). In the application window and the owner's local page, Settings › Advanced › Connect an agent shows an address only when the installation is signed in to a coordinator.

Images posted with `teams_post` rely on behavior Microsoft documents only in part: Graph documents hosted contents in chat messages and their 4 MB, but lists no image types and says nothing about animated GIFs or the reserved personal chat (`48:notes`), and the only table of types (for bots, another path) names PNG, JPEG and GIF. WebP is therefore refused.

The Teams message style (`[message_style]`) was checked with the Adaptive Cards renderer, not in every Teams client: the band's emoji is a plain text block, because Teams does not draw the `Icon` element in chat messages; a client without the `CodeBlock` element shows its fallback (monospace lines), and a client that ignores card background images shows no stripe. A computer whose host predates 0.12.13 cannot install an account document that carries the style until it updates, and until then sends in its own.

Each portal account the operator enables has a store of its own, shared by its computers: one
receives the content key and the service token at trust time (or from the key backup) and either
mirrors only its history (signed in to a coordinator in `mirror`), joins `primary` once the
coordinator has, or, standalone, adopts the account's configuration when it migrates. An older host
(before 0.10.35) pulling another installation's `installation/<ID>` record stops its pull (it knows
only the two account records), so since Worker 0.5.0 no computer joins an account (`connect`),
adopts its configuration or makes the store its source while another active installation of the
account reports a host older than 0.10.37; an offline computer counts with the version it last
reported, so it is updated or revoked first. A revoked computer keeps the content key and service token it holds until they are replaced
(`cloud lost`, which needs the approved launcher run from the coordinator's own local page or local
CLI). A replaced service token and a rotated content key reach the account's
computers trusted with the account store since 0.10.38 from their coordinator within a minute
(announced in their poll; an offline one when it comes back); one trusted earlier is trusted again once, and a standalone second installation (no
coordinator) is given them by hand (`cloud connect`, the key backup). Until a computer receives the
rotated key, what the coordinator seals with it does not open there (`delivery.key.behind`).
Computers in `mirror` keep receiving their configuration through the coordinator's `portable_*`
until they join the store (`cloud promote` there), and variables, machines and the Teams lease stay
with the coordinator. Conflicts are resolved per record by keeping one side's whole version (`cloud resolve`, after
seeing which fields differ); there is no automatic or field-by-field merge. A
backup is restored only on the computer itself by its owner (`cloud restore`, which restarts the
host); a portal account asks the operator. `erase` cannot undo itself: the 30-day point-in-time recovery of the
Durable Object needs the operator and the retired key, and erased R2 backups are gone. A deletion
is noticed by the next sweep (up to 10 minutes) rather than announced. A targeted repair (`cloud repair`) sends again only
what this computer had copied; records only the store holds are reported, never removed, and in `primary` the
configuration's differences are conflicts to resolve.

Workflows (§16 [Workflows](#workflows)) have definitions, settings, the director's classification, runs with their engine, acting blocks, summaries, operations, CLI, MCP and the GUI's Workflows section with its Runs view and Settings › Workflows (§11, since 0.12.9); Agent history shows a classification or a summary with the generic implementation badge. What epic #238 leaves out: GitHub epics and issues as workflow items or targets (the engine reads and plans Azure DevOps items only), Teams channels (chats only), launchers on machines other than the coordinator, children coded in parallel within one run, and any merge, completion or state change of a work item or pull request by the engine, which only reads them; a release through anything but an approved launcher of the coordinator is the person's. A summary is written from the record alone, so it can only say what the engine knows (a pull request the engine has not read yet is absent), is limited by the director's 60-second session like a classification, and a send to a chat is checked against the chat directory, which Graph pages 50 chats at a time and the host reads whole within 60 seconds; a user with very many chats may see the read time out and nothing is sent. The summary's Links list names the item always and the children and pull requests the text names by number or title; a summary that names one only loosely (a paraphrase) does not link it. An acting block is never retried: a notification whose result is unknown, or a launcher that was attempted when the host stopped, stays `uncertain` and the person checks Teams or the launcher's own effects by hand; a launcher that failed is not run again by a resume, only after the person starts the work again outside the run. A launcher block waits while another launcher runs and a launcher's output is never kept, so its failure shows only as `failed` or `timed_out` and an exit code. The `{summary}` of a notification is the run's latest stored summary and is empty until one exists. The watcher's last poll is kept in memory (a restart empties it; the seen ledger stays), it opens at most 25 runs per poll, and an Implement start with a workflow takes the workflow's own directories and instructions: the form's context is not passed to a workflow run. A run finds a child's pull request only through the branch its coding session reports on its last `Branch:` line and only in the repository it was coded in; a session that pushes elsewhere or reports no branch stops the run in `needs_attention`. The run plans and codes with the definition as it was when the run got its workflow, so an edit reaches only new runs.
