When the director cannot be reached or the item cannot be read, a classifying run gives up its slot and is asked again after one minute, the wait doubling up to half an hour (`retries`, `retry_at`). A read or an observation that fails is noted in `error` and the note is cleared by the next one that answers. Concurrency: at most `concurrency` runs hold a slot, taken oldest first; the stages where an agent works or the run acts take one (`classifying`, `planning`, `creating_children`, `coding`, `blocks`), while waiting for the user's review or for pull requests takes none, so a run in review never blocks another one's planning. The updater defers while a step runs.

**Stages.** A run started with a named workflow records `classifying` as finished with outcome `named`; with `director: true` the engine asks the director (`workflow_classify`'s path, which records its session) and applies its verdict with the same checks (an enabled workflow of the item's organization and project), or moves to `unassigned` with the reason; no director configured is `unassigned` at once. A **requirement- or bug-category** item is planned: one planning run on the workflow's machine with its agent, every checkout of the workflow as working directories and a context that holds the workflow's name, repositories and checkouts, its instructions and its instruction blocks, introduced as the user's configuration and untrusted input that cannot lift the fixed rules (`PLAN_RULES`, `CODE_RULES` still apply). When it is `plan_ready` the run is `plan_review`: nothing is written, and the plan is the ordinary reviewed plan in `implementation_runs`. The user reviews and confirms the children through the existing `implementation_create` (Implement, `pta tools call implementation_create`, MCP); the engine notices the ledger entry keyed by the planning run's ID (with the item as parent), records it and follows it in `creating_children`; `created` gives the children (the ledger's created items with their verified links), `sending` waits, and `uncertain` or `needs_attention` stop the run in `needs_attention`. Then `coding`: one coding run per child, strictly one after another (the next is launched only once the previous one is no longer running), in the checkout of the workflow repository the child names first as a whole word in its title or description (read fresh), or the workflow's first repository; the child's own guide applies as for `implementation_start`, and the context adds the child's identity and link, its parent, and the request to end the report with a line `Branch: NAME`. A coding run that ends `done` records the last such line as the child's branch (`reported_branch`: the name after `Branch:`, with trailing sentence punctuation and quotes removed and nothing but a remark after it, `refs/heads/` removed, valid only as a plain Git branch name); one that ends `permission_prompt`, `failed`, `needs_attention`, `interrupted` or `cancelled` stops the run in `needs_attention` naming the state and the coding run. A **task-category** item (a task, or a development bug the To-Do lists with the bugs) skips planning and children: it is its own single child. `pull_requests`: for each coded child the engine reads `GET {org}/{project}/_apis/git/repositories/{id}/pullrequests?searchCriteria.sourceRefName=refs/heads/BRANCH&searchCriteria.status=all` (`ado/workflow.rs`, at most 50, only that branch and repository kept) once right after the child is coded and then every five minutes, and records each pull request with its verified link (built from the source's organization, the project, the repository's catalog name and the pull request's ID). A failed read is timed like a successful one, so an outage is read again only after the poll interval. The run waits until every coded child has pull requests and all are `completed`; an `abandoned` one, a child that reported no branch, or a wait longer than `pull_request_wait_days` (counted from the stage's start, again from a resume) stops it in `needs_attention`, and so does a run that delivered no child (none coded to done, or no child created): it never reaches `done` as if it had. `blocks`: the `pull_requests_completed` and `done` blocks run (**Acting blocks**, below) and the stage's outcome counts only those; it is skipped (`skipped: no acting block`) when the workflow has none for these stages. Then `done`, with every stage timestamped.

**Resume and cancel.** Startup marks a run `interrupted` when the engine's first look finds that its active implementation run (the planning run, or the coding run of the child being coded) reads `interrupted` (as `runs.db` marks an in-flight run when it opens); a run whose machine cannot be asked waits, and the look is repeated every tick until every active run was answered; a creation in flight reads back `uncertain` (the registry marks it) and stops the run in `needs_attention`. `workflow_run_resume {run, confirmed}` continues a `failed`, `interrupted` or `needs_attention` run from `stopped_in`, accepting what stopped it: a planning run that did not end ready, or that its machine no longer has, is replaced by a new planning run with a new ID (planning is read-only); a ledger entry that names another parent is dropped and the run plans again; an uncertain or incomplete creation continues with the children that were created; a child whose coding run did not end done, or whose run its machine no longer has (recorded `missing`), is `accepted` as it ended (its branch, if it reported one, is still followed) and the next child is coded; an abandoned pull request is `accepted` (it settles its child) and a child without a branch is accepted without one; the wait for pull requests starts again; a run that delivered nothing is accepted as `done` without pull requests. It never resubmits a plan (only the user's `implementation_create` writes children, once, through the ledger), never starts again a coding run recorded as started, and issues no provider write. `workflow_run_cancel {run, confirmed}` works in every stage that is not final: the active implementation run, when its machine reports it in flight or cannot be asked (a run whose launch was not confirmed may already be there), is cancelled with the existing `implementation_cancel` (its file changes stay), and the run is `cancelled` and advances no more; a creation already sending is not stopped. The reply says whether the implementation run was cancelled, and when its machine could not be reached it warns, naming the run and the machine, to cancel it there with `implementation_cancel` once it is reachable.

**Acting blocks** (`app/workflows/acting.rs`, since 0.12.8). The engine runs a run's `teams_notification` and `launcher` blocks at their stage, before the stage's own step. A stage occurs in a run when its record says so (`occurrences`, a pure function of the record): `before_planning` while an HU or bug is in `planning` and its planning run is not started (a task has no planning, so none); `plan_ready` while the run waits in `plan_review` (keyed by the planning run); `children_created` once the ledger confirmed the children (keyed by the ledger); `child_done` for each child whose coding run ended `done` (keyed by the child, so once per child); `pull_requests_completed` (when a pull request exists) and `done` in `blocks`, before the run becomes `done`; `failed` when the run fails (keyed by the failure, so a failure after a resume is another occurrence; a failed run keeps its `failed` blocks in the engine's sweep until each has an outcome, so a busy launcher or a host that stopped does not lose them). The blocks of one occurrence run in the workflow's order. **Runs of older versions:** a run recorded by 0.12.3 or 0.12.4 has no `acting_since`; when the engine first sees it, every occurrence its record already shows (a failure long past, a plan that waited, children coded) is recorded as `skipped` with the reason `before acting blocks existed` and never acted on, and only occurrences that happen after act. A run created by 0.12.5 or later carries `acting_since` from its start. **Once:** before it acts the engine records the block's outcome as `attempting` (saved, under the run's lock, released while the block acts so a cancel is never kept waiting) and after it acts the result: `sent`, `refused` or `uncertain` for a notification; `succeeded`, `failed`, `timed_out`, `not_started` or `uncertain` for a launcher, with `reason`, the launcher's `exit_code` and `duration_ms`, the notification's `message_id` and the `occurrence` key (`BlockOutcome` in the run record, shown by `workflow_run_status`, `pta workflows status` and MCP). A block with an outcome for the occurrence never runs again, so a restart never posts or runs twice; an attempt found without a result (the host stopped between the two records) becomes `uncertain` with that reason and is never repeated; every such attempt of every run is settled once when the engine starts, so a stopped run never shows `attempting` for good. A refused or uncertain notification does not stop the run; a launcher that `failed`, `timed_out` or is `uncertain` (the host stopped while it ran) stops a run that is advancing in `needs_attention` (`stopped_in` is the stage) for a person to check its side effect; the decision belongs to the stage the block began in, so a result that returns after the run was resumed or cancelled stops nothing, and the engine's loop ends there. A resume continues with the blocks that have not run, never the failed one. One launcher runs at a time on the coordinator: the engine takes the launcher slot before it records the attempt, so a block that finds another launcher running records nothing and is asked again on the next tick. **Notification.** The template is rendered by code in one pass (`render`): `{item}` is the title and number, `{link}` the verified link, `{pull_requests}` a Markdown list of the verified links of the pull requests, `{summary}` the latest entry of the run's `summaries` (its `text`; empty when there is none), `{state}` the stage's state word; at `child_done` the item, link and pull requests are the child's. What replaces a placeholder is never read again, and `@[` anywhere in the rendered text (an item title, a pull-request title, a summary, or a template `@` followed by a value that starts with `[`) is made inert, a mention written literally in the template included, so only the block's own `mentions` (written first, as `@[Name]`, after everything else was made inert) mention anybody. It posts through `post_chat`, the path of `teams_post` (`retry_safe: false`: one request, a failure after it is `uncertain` and never repeated; since 0.12.5 a failure after Teams accepted the message, such as a missing message ID, is also `uncertain`): the running service's session or the profile's, a chat the user belongs to (the personal chat included), mentions resolved against that chat's members only; an unknown or ambiguous mention, a chat the user left, an invalid or too long message and a missing Microsoft session refuse the whole post with the reason recorded, and a failure before the request is a refusal too, and a failure to write the event log after the request went out changes nothing. No reception allowlist applies and no channel is reachable. Each notification is recorded as a `workflow_notification` event (`sent`, `refused`, `uncertain`) in the assistant's event log (the `events` table of `assistant.db`, where `teams_post` records its own), beside the `teams_post` event of the send itself. **Launcher.** `consumers::run_for_block` runs the coordinator's launcher through the path of `variable_launcher_run` (program path and digest checked again, directory inside the machine's allowed roots, every variable resolved at the moment of use, the vetted minimal environment, one at a time, the launcher's timeout) only while it is still approved with the digest stored in the block; removed, not approved or approved again with another digest is `not_started` with the reason and nothing runs. The program's output is not captured and no outcome, log or tool result holds a value. **Authority.** The engine calls both paths under `Transport::Workflow`, an invocation made only by `authority::workflow` (the account owner, in this process): no token, session or credential maps to it (the control endpoint, the portal and the remote MCP endpoint only produce `Local`, `Browser`, `Machine` and `Agent`), `authorize` refuses a request that carries it, and the two paths check `require_workflow` again, so the control token, a browser session, a machine credential, MCP and `pta tools call` cannot reach them this way (tests try each, and one asserts that no client module names it).

**Authority.** `workflow_run_start {provider: "azure", id, workflow | director: true, project?, source?, confirmed}` is `External`: the confirmation says that the run plans with the workflow's agent on its machine, waits for the user's review of the plan, then codes each created child with full permissions on that computer and no further prompts (a task directly), and posts the workflow's Teams notifications and runs its launchers at their stages. It runs only where the engine does, and it refuses a disabled or unknown workflow, a workflow of another organization or project, an item that is not an HU, bug or task, an item not assigned to the user (its Assigned To or a developer role of its process, as To-Do reads them, names the user of the source of its organization; `ado::workflow::assigned`) and an item with an active run. `workflow_run_assign {run, workflow, confirmed}` gives an `unassigned` run an enabled workflow of its item's project with the same confirmation; `workflow_run_resume` and `workflow_run_cancel` are confirmed too. There is no switch that removes the plan-review gate: the engine has no path that creates children. The director's choice and the agents' reports (the branch line) grant nothing: code checks the workflow, the item, the branch name, the repository and the pull request's branch.

**The watcher** (`app/workflows/watcher.rs`) is a second tracked task of the control loop, beside the engine and the daily scheduler. Every 30 seconds it asks whether a poll is due (`watch_interval_minutes` after the last one) and, on the account owner's coordinator only (`engine::may_run`: never a computer signed in to one, never a shared portal account that is not the owner's), with at least one ready state configured, polls; with no ready state it reads nothing, and it needs no running Teams service. A poll takes each project of `[workflow_settings].ready_states` that has at least one enabled workflow with `auto_start` (a project with none is skipped and nothing is read for it) and reads, once per ready state, the To-Do list `list_azure_hus` (`tools::todo::read`, so the same assignment rules, the read credential and the process cache) with `{source, project, state, limit}`; with several enabled Azure sources, `tools::todo::source_for` maps the entry's organization to the one source whose catalog names it (none, or two, is reported for that project and nothing is read), and the same source goes to the run's start. A failed read of one ready state is reported in the project's coverage and the project's other states are still read; the failed state's rows stay. The list holds: HUs, bugs and the tasks a work item guide lists with the bugs, assigned to the user, at most **100 items per project per poll** across its ready states (the states left when the bound is spent are read at the next poll). Items are kept only if their link names the configured organization and their state is the ready state. The list carries each item's `System.ChangedDate` (`changed_at`, additive on `tools::todo::Item`). Nothing here changes a source's audiences.

**The seen ledger.** Table `workflow_seen` in `workflows.db` (its own connection beside the runs' store): `item_url` and `state` (the key), `item_id`, `organization`, `project`, `changed_at`, `run_id`, `marked_at` and `first_seen`. A row holds the ID of the run it asks for, written **before** the run is requested; the engine creates the run under that ID (`runs::start_confirmed`'s `run_id`), so the row and the run agree. A row whose run exists is seen: a state starts at most one run per entry, and a cancelled or finished run does not start another while the item stays in the state. A row without its run (an abort or a restart between the two, or a refusal) is waited for one interval (the start may be in flight) and then asked for again under the same run ID, which the engine's one-active-run-per-item index also guards; when the item already has an active run (started by hand, say) that run becomes the row's. The row is forgotten at once only when the start could not read Azure or the engine was not ready (`[network]`, `[not_ready]`). An item that is no longer listed in a ready state is forgotten, so one that leaves and comes back is seen again; every listed item counts as present, whether or not the poll may start it, and nothing is forgotten from a read that was partial (`partial` or `has_more`) or failed.

**Starting.** For a new item the watcher calls the engine's own start path (`runs::start_confirmed`, the same code as `workflow_run_start`, which re-reads the item, checks the assignment and the one active run per item) with `auto: true`. When exactly **one** enabled workflow of the project starts by itself, the run takes that workflow directly and needs no director. When **several** do, the run asks the director (`director: true`); a run with `auto_start` (a record field, `#[serde(default)]`) asks it over the enabled workflows of the project **that start by themselves** only (`classify_item`'s `auto_only`) and the engine applies the verdict only to such a workflow, so a director answer naming a workflow that does not start by itself, a disabled one, none or no answer leaves the run `unassigned` for the user, and nothing is coded. With several and **no director configured** nothing starts (no read, no run): the poll reports `waiting_for_director` for the project, the watcher view says `waiting_for_director` with `waiting_projects`, and Home shows one warning row linking to the director's settings. The watcher authorizes only this start: the run then stops at `plan_review` and the plan-review gate is unchanged. At most **25 new runs per poll** (`MAX_STARTS`): items beyond it are not recorded and are taken by the next poll, so a first poll over a long backlog never opens dozens of director sessions at once. The last poll (time, runs started, per project: complete or partial, items seen, started, already seen, deferred, errors, or why it was skipped) is kept in memory and shown by `workflow_settings` as `watcher` (`state` `watching`, `idle` while no ready state is configured, `waiting_for_director` with `waiting_projects`, or `unavailable` where it never runs, with the interval, `last_poll` and `next_poll_at`); a restart empties it, the ledger stays.

**The Implement entry** (`app/workflows/implement.rs`). `implementation_start` gains `workflow`: `none` (the default) is dropped before the plain operation reads its request, which is unchanged; a workflow ID starts a workflow run for the item through `runs::start_confirmed` (an Azure item assigned to the user, an enabled workflow of its project) and returns `{workflow, started, run}`, with the workflow's machine, checkout directories, agent, model, effort and instructions, so a request that also gives `machine`, `directories`, `provider`, `model`, `effort` or a non-empty `context` is refused (changing them is a plain run without a workflow); `director` classifies (`workflow_classify`'s code, over every enabled workflow of the item's project) and returns the choice, outcome and reason with `started: false`, starting nothing, and the caller confirms with a second call naming the workflow. The MCP confirmation says what each form does. Hosts that do not advertise `workflow_runs_support` refuse a request with `workflow`.

**Progress summaries** (`app/workflows/summary.rs`). `workflow_summary {run}` asks the director for a progress summary of one run, on the owner's coordinator only. The input is the run record and nothing else (`summary::input`): the language (`llm.language`), the item's title and verified link, type, category and project, the workflow's ID and name (not its instructions, checkouts or blocks), the state and stage, every stage with RFC 3339 timestamps and outcome (at most 100), the director's own classification (outcome and its reason), the children (at most 50) with their links, repository name, the state of their coding run, branch, outcome and pull requests (at most 20 each, with link, status and dates), the block outcomes (at most 50) and the attention and error text (1000 characters each); titles are cut to 200 and the item's to 500. Never a session transcript or launcher output. It runs in the director's own tool-less, read-only, closed-JSON session (`implementation::director::ask` with `DirectTask::Summarize`): the prompt is the user's `direct` prompt, then the `summarize` task code appends (it says the prompt's request to choose a workflow does not apply and what the summary covers), the fixed `SUMMARY_RULES` (the record is untrusted input, write only from it, no URLs, links or `@[…]` mentions, no tools) and the closed schema `{"summary": string}` (`llm::implementation::DirectTask`); on another machine it is the same `workflow_direct` call, whose `Call` gains an additive `task` field left out for choosing a workflow (so an older worker still answers classifications and refuses a summary as a malformed call: update that machine), within the same time constants (probe 15 s, session 60 s, polls, 95 s in all) under the 120-second control deadline. The session is recorded in `request_runs` like a classification, with `purpose: "summary"`, `workflow_run` (the run's ID) and the characters stored, so Agent history lists it; an answer that is not exactly `{"summary": text}`, no answer, or a text with nothing left after cleaning ends the record `failed` with the reason and the operation fails with `No summary was stored`. Code makes the text safe (`summary::compose`): every Markdown link or image keeps only its words, reference definitions, autolinks, bare URLs of any scheme, `www.` and `mailto:` hosts and HTML tags are removed, `@[Name]` becomes `@Name` (a model grants no mention), control characters go, and the text is cut at a line or word break to leave room for the **Links** list (`Enlaces` in Spanish) code builds from the record: the item always, then each child and pull request the stored (final, cut) text names (`#41`, `HU 41`, `PR !7`, `pull request 7`, or a title of at least eight characters as whole words), at most 1200 characters; the whole summary has at most 4000 characters. The answer pipeline's inline linking of named entities needs `Evidence` references with aliases and refuses an answer that names an entity it cannot verify, which does not fit a free summary, so the list is appended instead. The summary is stored on the run (`Run::summaries`, the field a notification block reads for `{summary}`: the last element): `id` (the generation's ID in Agent history), `text`, `created_at` (Unix seconds), `provider`, `model` and `session_id` (the native ID); at most 20, the oldest dropped. Writing one changes nothing else on the run and takes the run's lock only to store.

`workflow_summary_send {run, summary, chat, mentions?, text?, confirmed}` posts one stored summary, or `text` (the edited version, at most 8000 characters), to one chat. It is `External` and refuses without `confirmed`. What the person approves always includes the exact text that will be posted, with the run, summary ID, chat and mentions: the host's refusal has it, and so do the MCP question, the portal's and the CLI's prompt (`tools::question` reads the stored text from the run through the same transport, and asks nothing when it cannot be read); the CLI's unconfirmed command prints the latest summary, its ID and the text, and the confirmed command must repeat `--summary ID`, so a summary written meanwhile is never posted unseen. When an earlier send of that summary to that chat is `uncertain`, the question says to check Teams first. **Authority:** an edited `text` is the same capability as `teams_post`, which the remote endpoint denies, so the host refuses `text` for the `Agent` transport (the remote endpoint) and keeps it for the person (the portal's browser transport and this computer's page and CLI); an agent sends a stored summary as written. The chat must be in the user's chat directory (`Graph::chat_directory`, what `teams_chats` lists: people as their one-to-one chat, group chats; read within 60 seconds); the personal chat, a channel and any other ID are refused. Each mention is a member's display name, resolved with `post_chat`'s own matcher (`graph::mention_target`) over the member list the post reads (`Graph::chat_members`, the user included), so a mention that passes cannot be refused as ambiguous after confirmation; an unknown or ambiguous name refuses the whole send, the same person named twice is mentioned once, and an edited text may also write `@[Name]`; mentions not written in the text open the message in one line. All of this is checked before anything is posted, and a refusal leaves nothing on the run. Then the send record is written (`Run::summary_sends`: `id`, `summary` (the stored summary's ID), `chat` {id, topic, type}, `mentions`, `chars`, `text` only when it is an edit, `edited`, `outcome`, `at`) already `uncertain`, so an interruption never reads as a send that did not happen; the message goes through `Graph::post_chat` (the `teams_post` path: the user's own message, Markdown through `teams::html`, one POST, never retried, recorded as the `teams_post` event `sent`, `refused` or `uncertain`) and the record becomes `sent` (with `message_id`), `refused` (nothing was sent: Teams refused, `post_chat` refused, or Graph could not be reached before the request) or `uncertain` (only for a failure after the request, which `post_chat` reports as `PostRefusal::Uncertain`), and a send that is not `sent` fails with that reason and where it is recorded. The post's real outcome is always the answer: when its record cannot be saved the reply still says sent (with the message ID and `record_saved: false`) or uncertain, and says the record could not be saved, never that nothing happened. At most 100 send records are kept per run. Several recipients are several confirmed sends, each with its own record and outcome. Both operations run only for the owner on the coordinator (`require_engine`, shared with the run operations); a machine credential reaches neither.

**Surfaces.** Host operations `workflows_list`, `workflow_show`, `workflow_save`, `workflow_remove`, `workflow_blocks_list`, `workflow_block_save`, `workflow_block_remove`, `workflow_repositories` (`app/workflows.rs`) and `workflow_settings`, `workflow_settings_update`, `workflow_classify` (`app/workflows/director.rs`) are dispatched before the operations lock (catalog and machine reads can take seconds) and take it only to commit; the run operations `workflow_run_start`, `workflow_runs`, `workflow_run_status`, `workflow_run_resume`, `workflow_run_cancel` and `workflow_run_assign` (`app/workflows/runs.rs`) and `workflow_summary` and `workflow_summary_send` (`app/workflows/summary.rs`) never take it. Hosts and CLIs advertise additive `workflows_support`, for the three director operations `workflow_director_support`, and for the run and summary operations `workflow_runs_support` (a host older than 0.12.6 that advertises it answers the two summary operations as unknown); the CLI refuses these methods to a host without them. CLI: `pta workflows list|show ID|save|enable ID|disable ID|remove ID --confirmed`, `pta workflows blocks list|save [--confirmed for a notification or launcher]|remove ID`, `pta workflows repositories --project NAME [--organization URL] [--source ID]`, `pta workflows settings [--project NAME [--organization URL] [--source ID]]`, `pta workflows settings --set KEY VALUE…` (`watch_interval_minutes`, `concurrency`, `pull_request_wait_days`, `director.machine|provider|model|effort`, which the host merges with the stored director, `director none`, `ready_states.PROJECT "STATE,STATE"` or `ready_states` with a JSON entry) `pta workflows classify ID [--project NAME] [--source ID]`, `pta workflows run ID (--workflow WORKFLOW | --director) [--project NAME] [--source ID] --confirmed`, `pta workflows runs [--state STATE] [--workflow WORKFLOW] [--limit N]`, `pta workflows status RUN`, `pta workflows resume RUN --confirmed`, `pta workflows cancel RUN --confirmed`, `pta workflows assign RUN WORKFLOW --confirmed`, `pta workflows summary RUN` and `pta workflows summary RUN --send CHAT_ID [--mention NAME…] [--text-file PATH] [--summary ID] --confirmed` (the send posts the run's latest stored summary, or the one named, or the edited file; the CLI reads the run first so its confirmation shows the exact text). MCP tools of the same names share the host adapters: reads (`workflow_settings` included) are `read`, `workflow_save`, `workflow_block_remove`, `workflow_settings_update` and `workflow_classify` (it records a session and writes nothing else) are `local`, `workflow_runs` and `workflow_run_status` are `read`, `workflow_summary` is `local` like `workflow_classify` (it runs a model on the director's machine, so it takes time and uses that agent's quota, and writes the summary on the run, rotating the 20 kept, and its session in Agent history; nothing to a provider, no confirmation), `workflow_remove`, `workflow_run_start`, `workflow_run_resume`, `workflow_run_cancel`, `workflow_run_assign` and `workflow_summary_send` need confirmation, and `workflow_block_save` does for a notification or launcher block. Their errors map to codes with the dispatcher's own helper (`control::failure`). The portal forwards all nineteen and `teams_chats` (read-only, so the GUI can choose a notification's or a summary's chat there; it posts nothing), and the remote MCP endpoint offers them all and `teams_chats` (the host runs the director on its own machine only for an invocation with execution authority, and starts, resumes, assigns, summarizes and sends only on the owner's coordinator). `workflow_summary_send` is the one Teams post the remote endpoint reaches (`teams_post` stays denied there): it posts only a stored or user-edited summary to a chat of the directory, after the same confirmation of its exact text and chat; a launcher block saved through it is refused by the host. Machine credentials reach none. The GUI's Workflows section and Settings › Workflows are described in §11.

### Request creation and agent history

To-Do places Create request below its filters. The form uses the selected provider: GitHub creates an issue of type Epic, while Azure offers HU/bug radios and resolves the default type from the project's Requirement/Bug category. Project/repository choices open a searchable modal sheet (a bottom sheet on phones), with the name filter first and up to three recent destinations before the remaining choices. Explicit requests enumerate all accessible projects in the enabled Azure source's configured organizations, independently of its team-project evidence catalog. GitHub runs `gh repo list --limit 100000 --json nameWithOwner,isPrivate` with the profile's GitHub CLI session, listing owned public and private repositories independently of GitHub App installation selection. Catalogs fail explicitly at their coverage bounds (1,000 Azure projects per organization or 100,000 GitHub repositories) rather than returning a truncated list. Recent choices come from this profile's latest recorded requests, deduplicated and intersected with the current catalog. The selected installed CLI/model/effort generates one bounded title/description in `llm.language`, in a retained, tool-less native session on the host. No directory selection is required: each session uses its private `implementation/requests/<run-id>` directory. The model never chooses URLs, targets, types, credentials or permissions.

Home shows the GitHub CLI installation/authentication state and verified login through the shared read-only `github_cli_status` operation. Its row refreshes directly; missing authentication explains `gh auth login` on the host. Child commands use a restricted environment, pin `github.com`, disable prompts and discard stderr. Isolated profiles pin `GH_CONFIG_DIR` to their own `home/.config/gh` and never inherit operator GitHub tokens or configuration. The existing GitHub App login, evidence and checkout flows remain separate.

Shared CLI/MCP/GUI operations are `implementation_request_catalog`, `implementation_request_start`, `implementation_request_status` and `implementation_history`. Create is explicit user authorization for this single new request, independent of reviewed child plans. Azure uses the host profile's authenticated Azure CLI session; read credentials and daily evidence adapters remain read-only. Catalog membership is checked before generation and again before writing. GitHub uses the same GitHub CLI account for explicit request creation (its token remains in host memory), requests Epic type and own assignment, and preserves the verified issue link with `needs_attention` if either property was silently dropped ([GitHub create issue contract](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/issues#create-an-issue)). Azure uses the process-defined default HU/bug type, the matching To-Do assignment role and the type's description/reproduction field ([category contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/azure/devops/wit/work-item-type-categories/list?view=azure-devops-rest-7.1)).

`implementation/runs.db` additionally stores `request_runs`: stable client UUID, exact confirmed submission, destination, CLI/model/effort, live native session ID, start/finish times, generated draft, durable send intent and verified created item. Repeated submissions with that UUID return the saved execution and never write again; a different submission cannot reuse the UUID. Startup marks generation interrupted and pending sends uncertain, retaining native IDs without inventing finish timestamps. Provider writes are never retried. The updater defers while generation or a send is active.

Agent history is a view of To-Do, opened from it; like Create request, Implement and Guides and instructions it has no navigation item and keeps To-Do current. It merges local request executions, local implementation sessions and the coordinator's persisted session summaries from enrolled machines, showing the latest 100 within an 800 KB response budget and reporting bounded history. It shows app run ID separately from native agent session ID, model, effort, machine, state, dates and verified work item links. Opening a session reuses the existing recorded-session operation and its provider/platform limits. Request sessions run on the host serving the form; they are not dispatched to execution machines. Existing implementation runs retain their execution-machine selection.

### Azure CLI session

Explicit Azure request/child creation uses `az account get-access-token --resource 499b84ac-1321-427f-aa17-267ca6975798` on the host performing the write, with the existing catalog/read credential still confining destinations and process metadata. The resulting Entra token is held only in host memory and sent as Bearer authentication; it never reaches IPC, model processes, app logs or stored credentials. A fresh session is checked before generation/child submission and reacquired before a send. No Azure CLI write command is used, so the host retains its existing single-send/no-retry ledger. This follows the [Azure CLI Entra flow](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/devops/cli/entra-tokens?view=azure-devops).

The shared read operation `azure_cli_status` reports `state`, `authenticated`, account `user` and `tenant`, never a token. It distinguishes missing CLI, no selected authenticated account and an account whose Azure DevOps token cannot be acquired or is expired. Authentication does not prove a project's write permissions. Home adds Azure CLI to its status rows, starts browser sign-in from an unavailable session and refreshes an authenticated session; checks run independently of the ordinary profile snapshot, once on load and every minute while Home is visible. The request form links to that status if no session is ready. The additive `begin_azure_cli_login`, `finish_azure_cli_login` and `cancel_azure_cli_login` operations (CLI `pta auth azure login|finish [--wait]|cancel`, plus `status`) run `az login --use-device-code --allow-no-subscriptions --scope 499b84ac-1321-427f-aa17-267ca6975798/.default` in that host profile, pin the configured Graph tenant, disable the terminal subscription selector and suppress account output. Both Microsoft verification prompts (`microsoft.com/devicelogin` and `login.microsoft.com/device`) are accepted with exact URL boundaries; the public IPC URL remains the former for existing clients. Only that known verification URL and a bounded user code leave the host; stderr is parsed with a bounded deadline/size for that prompt and otherwise discarded. The GUI shows the code and a link that opens Microsoft on the requesting device, polls completion, closes the dialog and refreshes the session automatically. Cancellation, host quit and a ten-minute deadline kill the CLI child. The [Azure CLI interactive flow](https://learn.microsoft.com/cli/azure/authenticate-azure-cli-interactively) handles browser authentication and MFA. Manual `az login` under the same OS user/profile remains available; a session on another execution machine is not the coordinator's session.

CLI subprocesses receive the existing restricted environment plus Azure CLI configuration and Python CA paths, never app secrets or PAT environment variables. Ordinary profiles reuse their configured/default Azure CLI directory. Isolated web profiles force `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` under their own `home/.azure`; an inherited operator login cannot authorize their writes. CLI calls have bounded deadlines and no stdin; status/token calls discard stderr, while device login reads only its validated public verification prompt before discarding the remaining diagnostics. The retired `AZURE_IMPLEMENTATION_WRITE_TOKEN` name/store entry is preserved for compatibility, shown as retired only if already stored, and is no longer requested or read by creation flows. Graph OAuth and scopes are unchanged.
